# Chess Application — Architecture

**Status: Baselined v1.0, 2026-10-26.** Every decision is AGREED except D-21 (DEFERRED, two-way, decided at implementation). ASM-1 is CONFIRMED. All three rounds of the user's answers are applied, and every question this document raised for the user is answered (§14). Nothing here is implemented yet.

**Amended 2026-10-27 (v1.0 amendment 1, user decisions; nothing renumbered):** a reset is allowed while the Player has unfinished games, and voids them (D-26, D-30, OQ-8 and G-20 closed). Stockfish 19 (`sf_19`) is the pinned engine (D-9, TBD-26 closed). One new non-blocking question for the user: Q-A18 (the architect's interpretation of how a voided game is shown, with a working default). See the change log below and §11.2.

**Amended 2026-10-28 (v1.0 amendment 2; requirements Amendment 2 / CR-4 and [the SE handoff](handoff_se_to_architect.md); nothing renumbered):** the architecture now implements the systems engineer's decisions CR-4.1 to CR-4.20. New decisions D-31 to D-37; D-17, D-18, D-19, D-21, D-24 and D-29 amended; risks R-19 and R-20; ASM-5 and OQ-12 added. In short: a **build-and-release view** with the Release engineer and the blocks that carry the release-time functions (§2.1, D-31); a **named allocation-block list** that makes the allocation usable below "chess-app core" and names the Tauri shell (§2.2, D-32); name rules corrected (§5.5, §7.2); `games.json` in the full-data archive (§5.7); duplicate-PGN prompt without "replace" (D-34); takeback, draw-offer and move-now rules (D-33); headless, `--data-dir`, `chess serve` errors and shutdown, `chess install-cli`, and distinct export and import command forms (D-36); journal and restore recovery with "voided: unreadable" and a backup cadence (D-35); a CLI Elo chart in character cells (D-37). Where the architecture pushes back on the systems engineer, the reason is in §10.4. See the change log below.

**Amended 2026-10-28 (v1.0 amendment 3; requirements Amendment 3 / CR-5; nothing renumbered; no decision added):** the systems engineer's consistency pass answered the points this document raised in §10.4. The **automatic backup** (24 h, newest 3 kept, TBD-33) is now stated in requirements DAT-5, so D-35 is driven by a requirement. The **restore limit** (R-19, OQ-12) is stated in DAT-5 too, and OQ-12 is closed; the journal is kept in `journal/unrestored/` and no automatic recovery is offered in v1. Exit code 4, `chess games`, `--on-duplicate`, `--yes` and the `chess export`/`chess import` forms are accepted as design (CLI-4, CLI-5; TBD-21 closed). `--config` and `CHESS_CONFIG` are accepted as test-only. The R-20 draw-offer clock stop is accepted. The confirmation wording in §4.1 and the UI-12 note on the void offer are aligned with the functional decomposition (F-2.5.7 is on `chess-app core`). Details are in §10.4 (last rows) and §14.

**Amended 2026-10-28 (v1.0 amendment 4; requirements Amendment 4 / CR-6; nothing renumbered; no decision added):** the user answered the readiness check ([SRR-SFR-readiness.md](SRR-SFR-readiness.md)). The software has ordinary criticality (not safety-related, not regulated), and SEI CERT is the coding standard with the standard Rust and TypeScript checks and every warning treated as an error (a new cross-cutting row in §8, a new row in §10.2; the source is a user decision, not a requirement). Q-A18 is answered: the reading of a voided game stands. Design changes inside one component are approved by the system-architect agent as a desk review, and anything wider by the user ([engineering/SWE-001-engagement-profile.md](engineering/SWE-001-engagement-profile.md)). No Intel Mac is available for testing and none is expected soon: PLAT-2, D-25 and R-13 are unchanged and the consequence is open (readiness FIND-015).

**Amended 2026-10-30 (v1.0 amendment 6; requirements CR-8; no decision added):** the ECO table source is chosen (G-15 closed, D-20 amended): the Lichess `chess-openings` dataset (CC0-1.0), a pinned snapshot in `third_party/eco/`, stored as code and name, matched by position (deepest named position). See D-20, §8.1 and §10.3.

**Amended 2026-10-30 (v1.0 amendment 5; requirements Amendment 5 / CR-7; nothing renumbered; no decision added):** the user answered the open readiness findings ([SRR-SFR-readiness.md](SRR-SFR-readiness.md), run 3). **Ratings are whole integers** and the Elo update is fully specified (ELO-1, §7.2: the rating columns change from `real` to `int`; nothing is released, so there is no migration). **The PGN export now follows the standard's export format** (§5.7; TBC-6 and TBD-24 closed). **Baseline versions** of every language and open-source component are recorded in the new §3.4. The NFR-8 budget allocation in §4.8 is a Should. The coding-standard row (§8) now reads "CERT informs best practice", the security row (§8) reflects a local-only v1 with CVE scanning, and the platform matrix (§9) is best effort for hardware that is not available. D-25 and R-13 are unchanged.

**Amended 2026-10-31 (v1.0 amendment 7; [SDD-architect-triage.md](design/SDD-architect-triage.md) section 5, decisions 1 to 13, approved by Brian Prascher as recommended with the modifications listed below; the systems engineer's desk answers [SDD-SE-desk-answers.md](design/SDD-SE-desk-answers.md) are confirmed; nothing renumbered):** the architecture now applies the architecture amendments AMD-1 to AMD-14 of the triage and records ten new decisions, **D-38 to D-47**, as accepted, and three more, D-48 to D-50, for the protocol package, file transfer and wire types (decisions 10, 9 and 8), which the triage had no draft record for. In short: the **front-end edge rule** and the missing `chess-app -> chess-rules` edge are fixed in §3.2, which closes the known open issue of the SDD triage; the store is a **concrete handle** and "in-memory store" is struck (§1, §4.1, §9); **UUID newtypes** are the identity outside the store; the **journal contract** gains two record kinds and a one-to-one `Fact` table (§7.6); a **stored-format batch** (nullable `content_hash`, `error_permille`, `build_version` as text, `MOVE_EVAL` from White's point of view, `VARIATION` creation time) is applied in §7.2 before any store code exists; `Query::ParseMove`, subscriptions, `FrontEndSession`, recovery mode and **file transfer by base64 in frames** join the protocol (§5); the roster no longer depends on the live engine (§4.6); the dependency policy and the dependencies admitted in principle are in §3.4 (no versions, all `[UNV]`). **Modifications by the user to the triage text:** (1) decision 9: the archive import has a documented size limit, an `ESTIMATE`, with a clear `ValidationFailed` message; the PGN limit stays 10 MB; (2) decision 11: the **single-instance mechanism is dropped** (it is not a requirement, and the store lock covers concurrent launches), and the dependencies for `signal-hook`, a native dialog plugin, an `arc-swap`-like cell and `rand`/`rand_chacha` are rejected as in the triage; (3) decision 13: no `player_id` on `VARIATION`; it gains `created_at` and keeps its anchor ply, and **saving a what-if branch from the final position is refused**. The approval is recorded as relayed to the architect in the task for this amendment; the systems engineer logs it in the requirements Decision Log (CR-9 is the expected entry). Evidence tags in this amendment: `[OBS]` read in this session, `[INF]` inferred, `[UNV]` unverified. **No dependency version is stated for any new dependency: each is marked `[UNV]` and waits for a registry lookup (§3.4).** The pinned `UCI_Elo` range has no value in any document until the Stockfish 19 build is run (ASM-3).

**Amended 2026-10-31 (v1.0 amendment 7, addendum A; nothing renumbered):** Brian Prascher (G2/G3 approver) ruled on the [SDD v0.2 joint review](design/SDD-v0.2-joint-review.md) the same day. Eight rulings are applied here, and six new decisions **D-51 to D-56** (his "delegate and apply" for the protocol shapes) plus **D-57** (calibration owner) and **D-58** (crash recovery) are recorded in §11.2. (1) `content_hash` is carried in the `GameEnd`, `Abort` and `Voided` journal payloads and in the reset's void specification (§7.6, §7.4; D-41 and D-42 text amended; G3). (2) The domain `Fact` carries every payload field its journal record has, and §7.6 now lists the exact fields per kind, so the FF-3 round trip holds by construction (G3). (3) The store returns **all stored `roster_index` values** (Brian's option C, OQ-21 closed; no class column) and the domain computes *k* per class (§4.6, §5.7; D-46 text amended; G2). (4) `chess-app` owns the calibration run (§2.2, §4.6, §5.7; D-57). (5) The `ParseMove` error and success shapes, the event envelope, `MalformedFrame`, `ExportWriteFailed` with the export reply, and the enforcement of "switch the active Player only while no game is open" (D-51 to D-56; §4.4, §5.1, §5.2, §5.5, §5.6). (6) The ID exception is accepted (`EI-EN-002`; no `R-EN-n` citation was found in this document). The stale `signal-hook` drawing in §6.1 is fixed, `Orientation` and the `FakeEngine` factory join the curated `chess-app` list (§3.2, §5.7), and crash recovery is settled at architecture level (D-58, §7.6). **Not applied here, because they are not in the rulings:** the ECO rule for an imported custom start (FIND-JR-012), the CLI and UI exit-code tables (FIND-JR-016) and the source of the `too_large` limit numbers (FIND-JR-019). The Review Board pass of OQ-16 still precedes store code and now also covers the items listed under OQ-16 and OQ-20 to OQ-22 (§14). D-59 (§5.8, AGREED by the user) defines the two-step import for CR-9.12; OQ-ECO-1 to OQ-ECO-3 are closed. **Addendum B (2026-11-01, rulings on the SDD v0.3 joint review):** D-60 refines it (plain mode asks at a terminal; hyphenated CLI values; tag normalisation; ply 0; `moves_san` and no `tag_name`; stateless archive imports; 10-minute idle time-out, `ESTIMATE`) and D-61 gives `EngineFactory` to `chess-app`; the stale text of FIND-JR3-010 is fixed. **Addendum C (2026-11-01):** D-62 closes OQ-23 (duplicates and the ECO question stay two steps; §5.8 reworded, no shape change). The status line of the diagrams is at the end of the "Diagrams" paragraph below. The systems engineer logs the rulings in the requirements Decision Log and `RELEASE_NOTES.md` (not edited here).

**Amended 2026-11-04 (v1.0 amendment 8; status APPROVED by Brian Prascher on 2026-11-04, carried into the SDDs (chess-cdb; recorded in beads chess-a0r.1.14); D-74, proposed in Addendum A, is CONFIRMED by Brian as a G2 decision (2026-11-04, beads chess-a0r.1.19); addenda A to F below; nothing renumbered):** The independent Board pass of OQ-16 on the store and journal decisions is held ([Board-pass-store-journal.md](design/Board-pass-store-journal.md), findings BP-001 to BP-017, task chess-a0r.1.2), and Brian Prascher (G2/G3 approver) dispositioned it at gate G-1 on 2026-11-04: he accepted every proposed disposition of its section 8 and ruled on the `F_FULLFSYNC` policy (BP-014: refuse) and on the closing condition of OQ-16. This amendment applies those dispositions. **Amendment 8 (D-63 to D-73, the stored-format batch SF-01 to SF-22 and the six format choices) was written by the architect and is APPROVED by Brian Prascher on 2026-11-04, carried into the SDDs.** He also approved the G2 tier of D-67, the D-72 and D-73 minor items, D-58 as one-way after release, and the architect's recommended value for all six format choices (SF-02, SF-03, SF-04, SF-15, SF-16, SF-21). **That approval is the text of the amendment. It is not a review: no Review Board pass, no desk review and no lightweight ATAM of Amendment 8 had been held when this paragraph was written** (*update, later on 2026-11-04: the two OQ-16 desk reviews have since been held and returned PASS WITH CONDITIONS; their conditions are not yet met, so nothing is passed; see Addendum E*) (see GATE-008 in §11.3 and the independence statement in §11.4), and **OQ-16 stays open** until the batch is carried into the SDDs and a desk review by the system-architect and the systems engineer (different invocations from the author) has passed. Nothing here claims independent review or any sign-off beyond what Brian gave. In short: new decisions **D-63 to D-73** (status APPROVED 2026-11-04, §11, §11.2, §11.3); **GATE-008**; one enumerated **stored-format batch SF-01 to SF-22** (§11.4); the finalize time totals join `GameEnd` (D-63); "custom start" is defined once and gets a `custom_start` column (D-64); terminal positions are re-checked on resume and a failed multi-record step adopts the durable prefix (D-65); `db_seq > last_intact_seq` counts as damage (D-66); OQ-20 is decided as option A with five conditions (D-67); the hash scope, the `Date` rule and a canonical-form version in the value are fixed (D-68); `Voided` gains `ended_at` and a rule for `journal_seq` of voided games (D-69); the remaining format details are frozen as one batch (D-70); a data directory that cannot do `F_FULLFSYNC` is refused (D-71); four minor Board recommendations are applied and were confirmed by Brian on 2026-11-04 as minor items (D-72, D-73). OQ-16 stays **open** with Brian's closing condition (§14); OQ-20 is closed (approved); the seven flags of OQ-22 have outcomes (§14); OQ-24, OQ-25, ASM-9 to ASM-11 and Q-A20 are added. Every G2/G3 item is labelled "FOR BRIAN" in §11.4 and now carries its approval date. **Addendum A (2026-11-04, architect):** OQ-13 gets a ruling, **D-74** (`GameContextProvider`; proposed here, CONFIRMED by Brian as a G2 decision on 2026-11-04, beads chess-a0r.1.19), and the readiness condition C-1 (FIND-001) is ruled by the architect under Brian's delegation, **D-75** (the ECO table and `eco::lookup` stay in `chess-rules`; the "deepest named position" rule stays in `chess-domain`; D-20 corrected). **Addendum B (2026-11-04, Brian, G2, approved):** **D-76**, no nightly Rust toolchain ever; `cargo-llvm-cov` 0.9.1 admitted; `cargo-fuzz`, `libfuzzer-sys` and `arbitrary` not admitted; NFR-3 is verified by stable-only means. GATE-009 records the addenda (§11.3). The text the systems engineer must log in `docs/requirements.md` and `RELEASE_NOTES.md`, and the SDD sections that need alignment (chess-a0r.1.8), are drafted in §11.4 and are not edited here. Evidence tags: `[OBS]` read in this session, `[RAN]` executed in this session, `[INF]` inferred, `[UNV]` unverified. **Decided since this paragraph was first written (same day):** the ECO table placement (D-75), the coverage and fuzz tooling of §3.4 and the nightly toolchain policy (D-76); OQ-13 has a ruling, D-74, which Brian confirmed as a G2 decision (beads chess-a0r.1.19). **Answered by Brian later the same day (Addendum C, D-77):** OQ-24 (no `calibration` Cargo feature in v1) and OQ-25 (no `chess-store` code of any kind before OQ-16 closes). **Addendum D (2026-11-04, architect, under Brian's delegation; §11.4.12):** D-74 is CONFIRMED by Brian (G2). Three major findings that the software engineer raised in the SDD alignment (chess-a0r.1.8) were ruled by the architect under Brian's delegation. **Brian Prascher ratified D-79 and D-80 on 2026-11-04 (beads chess-a0r.1.30).** **D-78 was not ratified at that point ("ruled under delegation, not ratified, awaiting Brian's choice"; the proposal is [PROPOSED-D-78-DAT-3-for-Brian.md](design/PROPOSED-D-78-DAT-3-for-Brian.md), §11.4.13, OQ-28). Update, Addendum F: Brian ratified D-78, option A, on 2026-11-04.** The three rulings were: **D-78** (chess-a0r.1.16; the time totals of a game that is suspended and resumed are seeded from records the journal already has, so no journal field is added; the store minors XC-ST-A8-1 to -4 are confirmed), **D-79** (chess-a0r.1.17; `Game::replay` takes `ReplayItem{fact, check}` so the SAN check of D-72 stays in the domain and `Fact` does not change) and **D-80** (chess-a0r.1.18; a session in the `JournalDegraded` state accepts read-only queries, `Detach` and `Shutdown`, which append nothing, and a game is resigned or aborted after a resume). **D-81:** `cargo-nextest` is not adopted for now; tests run with plain `cargo test`. None of D-78 to D-81 changes a stored format, a requirement statement or a §3.2 edge `[INF]`, and nothing is reviewed beyond the author. **Still not decided here:** the G-4 change request, and whether `cargo-insta` is admitted (it is not). **Addendum E (2026-11-04, architect, chess-a0r.1.31; §11.4.13):** the two OQ-16 desk reviews were held on 2026-11-04 (system-architect part and systems-engineer part, [OQ-16-desk-review-architect.md](design/OQ-16-desk-review-architect.md), [OQ-16-desk-review-systems-engineer.md](design/OQ-16-desk-review-systems-engineer.md); both verdict PASS WITH CONDITIONS) and this addendum answers the architect-owned findings with text only: **D-82** (writer after a failed append), **D-83** (the journal rebase), **D-84** (one stamp for the start; the `NewGame` field was PROPOSED FOR BRIAN and is decided in Addendum F, option A), **D-85** to **D-88** (four recovery-path rulings), the FF-3 and FF-4 wording, ASM-10 closed, and Brian's acceptance of exit class 3 for `DurabilityUnsupported` (G3). **None of these has been reviewed by anyone but its author; the independent re-review has not been held; OQ-16 stays open.**

**Addendum F (2026-11-04, architect, chess-a0r.1.41; CR-3 closing pass T-D2; records Brian Prascher's decisions of 2026-11-04, logged as CR-12 in requirements.md; §11.4.14).** Two interface changes come first, because the front-end SDDs derive from them. **(1) `NewGame` gains `started_at: i64` (OQ-26, option A, G2, decided by Brian).** `NewGame` is the Rust input of `StoreApi::start_game` in `chess-store`. The app supplies the one start stamp (the `WallMs` of the session's start `Stamp`, D-84); the store writes it unchanged to `GAME.started_at` and reads no clock for it; the same value is `Fact::Start.started_at` and the `wall_ms` of the `Start` record. `NewGame` is not a protocol type: it has no `ts-rs` derive, no front-end sees it, and the wire command `StartGame{game_uuid, GameSetup}` and the generated TypeScript types do not change (§5.1, §5.7). **(2) `canonical_form` omits `SetUp` and `FEN` when the `FEN` equals the standard start (OQ-27, option A, G3, decided by Brian).** "Equals" is the six-field comparison of SF-02; the omission is consistent with CR-9.11 (2). `v1` of SF-03 is defined with this rule (nothing is released, ASM-6), so a game imported with the standard `FEN` tag has the same `content_hash` as its own export and FF-4 has no known gap. **Also decided by Brian:** **D-78 is RATIFIED, option A (OQ-28, G2):** a game that is stopped and resumed **may lose up to one turn of time at each stop; it is not required to** (a recovery at a clean stop stays open), and the DAT-3 statement is not changed. **D-83 is rated G2** (it adds two names to the data directory) and Brian approved it; D-82 stays G1. The D-71 refusal is an NFR-2 verification case (CT-ST-085, CT-APP-33, CT-ST-001); exit class 3 applies in the TUI, in plain mode and in subcommands (TBD-36 is not narrowed); the desktop app shows the refusal as an in-window screen. **The architect added no decision beyond these.** The re-review findings RR-A-1 to RR-A-5 are fixed as text (D-83, D-87, §7.6, §11.4.14). **None of this has been reviewed by anyone but its author; OQ-16 stays open.**

**Changes 2026-10-24 → 2026-10-26:**
- First draft created from the requirements and the user's stack choices.
- CLI changed to a full-screen ratatui TUI, keyboard only with typed moves and no mouse, plus plain and headless modes (§6.1, D-6).
- Offline operation for desktop, web, and CLI made explicit, with enforcement and tests (§8.1).
- Section 11 decisions recorded (§11.2).
- D-4 decided after two trade studies ([first pass](trade-study-gui-board.md), [refined](trade-study-gui-board-refined.md)): chessground + shared keyboard/accessibility overlay, with react-chessboard (patched) as the fallback (§6.2, §11.1).
- 2026-10-26: all diagrams reviewed against the requirements and decisions. Added: offline boundary, bundled Stockfish, CLI modes (§2); third-party dependencies (§3.2); Policy and event stream (§4.1); step, engine error, and draw rule (§4.3); board revert path (§5.3); full screen lists (§6.1, §6.2); clock checkpoint and `GAME_TAG` (§7.2); fallback adapter (§11.1).
- 2026-10-26, architecture review (traceability and completeness):
  - New sections: concurrency and threading model with timeouts (§4.8); error catalogue, protocol reliability (reconnect, idempotency, versioning), and internal interface contracts including PGN/FEN I/O (§5.5–§5.7); data ownership and transaction boundaries, and schema version skew (§7.4, §7.5); deployment and packaging (§8.2); accessibility details (§8.3); open items (§14).
  - Expanded: Policy table (Resign, CancelEngine), engine-move legality check, and `Calibration` scope (§4.4–§4.7); state machine (takeback during an engine search, pause freezes clocks, D-14/D-15 hooks; §4.3); protocol commands and events (§5.1); §5.3 (the engine reply happens only after an accepted move); §5.4 (post-game analysis goes through `AnalysisService`); CLI run-mode rule (§6.1); GUI settings effects (§6.2); `APP_SETTING` and notes on imported games (§7.2); `ui/` row and requirement IDs in §3.3.

The architecture adds an **automatic backup cadence**, because without one "journals are kept until the next backup" had no meaning (D-35).

| Desktop shell | Tauri. |

---

## 1. Architectural principles

1. **One Rust core owns every rule and decision.** Move legality, mode policy (Training/Strict), clocks, Elo, roster generation, game lifecycle, and persistence live in Rust. Front-ends render state and send intents; they never decide what is allowed. This is what makes "same scripted game in all three front-ends gives identical PGN" (PLAT-1) and "each front-end exposes exactly what the policy allows" (test-strategy principle 5) achievable.
2. **Functional core, imperative shell.** Pure crates (rules, domain) take data and return data. I/O — Stockfish processes, SQLite, wall-clock time, sockets, terminals — sits at the edges. Where more than one real implementation exists (the engine, the clock, randomness) the edge is a trait, so tests can swap in fakes (fake engine, fake clock, seeded randomness). The store is **not** faked: tests use a real store in a temp directory (AMD-2, D-39).
3. **The engine is never exposed to a front-end.** Stockfish runs only inside the Rust process. Every engine request goes through one gateway that checks the request against the mode policy. Strict-mode integrity (ANA-1) is therefore enforced in one place, and a spy on that gateway proves it.
4. **One protocol, thin transports.** The core's API is a set of serializable commands, queries, and events. The CLI calls it in-process. The desktop app reaches it over Tauri IPC. The web UI reaches it over a localhost WebSocket. The TypeScript types are generated from the Rust types, so the two languages cannot drift apart.
5. **Every state change is durable before it is acknowledged.** Each move, clock checkpoint, and game end is appended to the game's local move journal and flushed to disk (`F_FULLFSYNC`) before the event is broadcast. SQLite is updated from the journal as soon as it is available. If the two disagree, the journal wins (§7.6, NFR-2, RULE-5, Q54).

---

## 2. System context and deployment

This section is the **run-time view only** (D-31). It shows the system while a person uses it. The build, packaging and release environment is a second context view in §2.1, and §2.2 names the blocks that requirements and functions are allocated to.

Everything runs on the user's Mac. The desktop app, the web page, and the CLI all work with no Internet connection, including first launch (PLAT-3, §8.1), and nothing makes outbound connections (NFR-4).

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    user(("User"))
    internet(["Internet"])

    subgraph mac["User's Mac — fully offline at run time (PLAT-3), no outbound traffic (NFR-4)"]
        direction LR
        subgraph p1["Process: chess (one binary on PATH)"]
            cli["CLI front-end<br/>ratatui TUI · plain mode · headless"]
            core1["chess-app core<br/>(in-process)"]
            cli --> core1
        end

        subgraph p2["Process: chess serve (same binary, started by the user)"]
            srv["axum HTTP + WebSocket<br/>127.0.0.1 only · Host/Origin checks · CSP<br/>serves embedded GUI bundle"]
            core2["chess-app core<br/>(in-process)"]
            srv --> core2
        end

        browser["Browser tab<br/>Safari · Chrome · Firefox · Edge<br/>TypeScript GUI"]

        subgraph p3["Process: Chess.app (Tauri desktop, ad-hoc signed local build)"]
            webview["WKWebView<br/>same TypeScript GUI (bundled)"]
            tauriShell["Tauri shell and IPC adapter<br/>desktop/src-tauri"]
            core3["chess-app core<br/>(in-process)"]
            webview -- "Tauri IPC" --> tauriShell
            tauriShell --> core3
        end

        db[("chess.db + journals<br/>SQLite, WAL<br/>~/Library/Application Support/Chess/<br/>journal locks + job queue coordinate processes")]
        sf1["stockfish<br/>(bundled, pinned)"]
        sf2["stockfish<br/>(bundled, pinned)"]
        sf3["stockfish<br/>(bundled, pinned,<br/>bundled as Tauri helper)"]

        core1 --> db
        core2 --> db
        core3 --> db
        core1 -. "UCI over stdin/stdout" .-> sf1
        core2 -. "UCI" .-> sf2
        core3 -. "UCI" .-> sf3
    end

    user --> cli
    user --> browser
    user --> webview
    browser -- "HTTP + WebSocket<br/>loopback only" --> srv
    mac -. "✗ no run-time connections" .- internet
```

Key points:
- **Three processes, one library.** Each entry point is its own OS process that links the same `chess-app` crate. There is no shared daemon. They coordinate only through the data directory: the SQLite file and the per-game journal locks (§7.3, §7.6).
- **One `chess` binary** provides both the CLI (`chess`) and the web server (`chess serve`, which prints the URL and opens it in the default browser). It ships inside `Chess.app` and is put on `PATH` by `chess install-cli`, which makes a symlink (PLAT-6, §8.2), so there is one thing to install and one version (PLAT-1, NFR-5, D-16).
- **Three engine boxes, one block.** The diagram draws `stockfish` once per process because each process starts its own child. It is the same bundled binary. Requirements and functions allocate to the one block named `stockfish`. In the desktop process the binary is packaged as a Tauri "sidecar", which here means only a helper binary bundled with the app. It is still spawned by `chess-engine` from `<exe dir>/stockfish`, not by the Tauri shell, so there is one spawn path in all three processes (AMD-9, FG-2). A shell-spawn path would be a G2 design change and is not planned.
- **The desktop adapter is a block.** `Tauri shell and IPC adapter` (`desktop/src-tauri`) sits between the webview and the core. It maps one `invoke("core", envelope)` call onto the protocol dispatcher and forwards events. It holds no rules (principle 1). Functions that carry the protocol over Tauri IPC are allocated to it, not to the process boundary (FG-2, D-32).
- **Stockfish runs as a native child process.** It is pinned to a specific release (Stockfish 19, `sf_19`, D-9), shipped for both arm64 and x86_64 (a universal build, Q46), and bundled once inside `Chess.app`, next to the `chess` CLI binary that also lives in the bundle (§8.2, D-16). The WASM build is not needed, because the engine always runs in Rust, never in the browser. Install locations and how each process finds its engine are covered in §8.2 (D-18).
- **Trust boundary.** The only boundary inside the Mac is loopback. Any local process can reach the `chess serve` port, and the browser is treated as untrusted input to the core (§5.2, R-5, D-22). Everything else stays inside one OS user account, used by one person (ASM-1, CONFIRMED).

### 2.1 Build and release view (enabling system, D-31)

**Question this diagram answers:** which blocks and which external parties produce a release of Chess, and where do the release-time functions (build, engine acquisition, licence audit, platform matrix, install documentation) live? The run-time view in §2 has no block for any of them, and UC-29 is the use case for this scope (FG-1, FG-10, H-18).

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    rel(("Release engineer<br/>(project owner in the build role)"))
    sfsrc(["Stockfish release source<br/>(build time only)"])
    subgraph envb["Build and CI environment (build time, may use the Internet)"]
        direction TB
        tp["third_party/stockfish/<br/>pinned tag, fetch script, SHA-256,<br/>COPYING, source offer"]
        pkg["packaging/<br/>universal build, bundle config,<br/>ad-hoc signing"]
        chk["CI checks<br/>cargo-deny, npm licence checker,<br/>lipo test, offline E2E, scheduled (nightly) calibrate"]
        doc["Release checklist and install docs<br/>README install section,<br/>platform-matrix record"]
    end
    app[["Chess.app (.dmg)<br/>the run-time system of section 2"]]
    user(("User"))
    rel --> doc
    rel --> pkg
    sfsrc -. "download once, checksum" .-> tp
    tp --> pkg
    pkg --> app
    chk -. "audits and tests" .-> app
    chk -. "audits dependencies of" .-> pkg
    doc -. "records the results of" .-> chk
    app -- "installed by the User (UC-1)" --> user
```

| Element | Kind | Role | Functions allocated (functional_decomp) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Release engineer` | External actor | The project owner in the build role. The same person as the User under ASM-1, acting in a different role. Starts the release procedure, records the platform matrix, signs off the checklist (the sign-off is the owner's, never claimed here) | none: outside the boundary |
| `Stockfish release source` | Supporting external entity | The published `sf_19` release and its checksum. Reached at build time only. Running the app never uses it (PLAT-3, NFR-4) | none |
| `Build and CI environment` | Supporting external entity (boundary) | Runs builds, audits and tests. May use the Internet (§8.1, "Build vs. run") | none: it hosts the blocks below |
| `packaging/` | Block | `Chess.app` build (universal, Tauri target), `Info.plist` minimum system version, ad-hoc signing of the app and its executables, `.dmg`, licence notices | F-8.6 |
| `third_party/stockfish/` | Block | Pinned engine source or asset, `fetch.sh` with the SHA-256, the `lipo -info` and `uci` checks, `COPYING`, source offer | F-8.7 |
| `CI checks` | Block | `cargo-deny`, npm licence checker, the `lipo -info` packaging test, bundle scan, offline E2E, and the scheduled (nightly) `chess calibrate` job | F-8.9, F-3.9 |
| `Release checklist and install docs` | Block | `release/CHECKLIST.md` (platform matrix, FIDE Laws edition check, engine facts, licence result) and the README install, update and uninstall text (PLAT-6, NFR-5) | F-8.8, F-8.10 |

Notes:
- **Calibration (F-3.9; Addendum A, D-57).** The function is the nightly job, so F-3.9 stays allocated to `CI checks` (the functional decomposition is unchanged; the systems engineer may add the owner there). **The run itself, meaning the matches, the SPRT and the report, is owned by `chess-app core :: Calibration run`.** The `chess calibrate` subcommand inside `CLI front-end` only parses its arguments, opens a `Core` with `Core::open_for_calibration` and calls the run. It is dev tooling, is not reachable through the protocol (§4.5), and refuses to run on the default data directory (R-17).
- **Ownership.** One person plays every role in v1 (ASM-1). The roles are still separate so that a release does not depend on the person remembering what to check: the checklist is the control, not memory (UC-29, RISK-1, NFR-6).
- **Out of scope here:** hosting or distribution, an installer package, notarization, and automatic updates (requirements Out of Scope, PLAT-6).

### 2.2 Allocation blocks (normative names, D-32)

**Why this section exists.** The §2 diagram shows `chess-app core` as one box, so an allocation to it cannot tell the services apart (FG-3). The §4.1 diagram already shows the services. This table gives every block that functions and requirements may be allocated to one exact name. An allocation cell in functional_decomp uses these names verbatim. A name of the form `chess-app core :: X` means block X inside the core, in whichever process runs the core.

| Allocation name | Kind | Crate or path | Runs in | Owner role |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `User`, `Internet` | External | — | outside the Mac | — |
| `CLI front-end` | Front-end block | `chess-cli` (TUI, plain, headless, `serve`, `install-cli`, `calibrate`, `export`, `import`) | Process: chess; Process: chess serve | CLI developer |
| `axum HTTP + WebSocket` | Transport block | `chess-server` | Process: chess serve | Core developer |
| `Browser tab` | Front-end block | `ui/` | the browser | GUI developer |
| `WKWebView` | Front-end block | `ui/` | Process: Chess.app | GUI developer |
| `Tauri shell and IPC adapter` | Transport block | `desktop/src-tauri` | Process: Chess.app | GUI developer |
| `chess-app core` | Block (whole) | `chess-app` and the crates it links | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: Protocol dispatcher` | Core part | `chess-app::protocol`, event stream | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: PlayerService` | Core part | `chess-app` | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: GameSetupService` | Core part | `chess-app` | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: SessionRegistry` | Core part | `chess-app` | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: GameSession` | Core part | `chess-app` (one actor per live game) | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: Policy` | Core part (pure) | `chess-domain` | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: Game domain` | Core part (pure) | `chess-domain` (`Game`, `Clock`, `EloCalc`, `RosterGen`, `VariationTree`, `EcoTable`, `BoardGrid`) | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: chess-rules` | Core part (pure) | `chess-rules` (moves, SAN, FEN, PGN) | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: EngineGateway` | Core part | `chess-app` | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: chess-engine` | Core part | `chess-engine` (UCI driver, pool, personalities) | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: AnalysisService` | Core part | `chess-app` | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: AnalysisWorker` | Core part | `chess-app` | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: StatsService` | Core part | `chess-app` | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: ImportExportService` | Core part | `chess-app` | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: chess-store` | Core part | `chess-store` (store thread, `MoveJournal`, migrations, backups) | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: Diagnostic log` | Core part | `chess-app` (tracing setup, rotation) | every process | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: Calibration run` | Core part | `chess-app` (`calibration`: matches between classes and reference settings, SPRT, report; *Addendum A*, D-57; F-3.9 stays allocated to `CI checks`, which starts it through `chess calibrate`) | the `chess calibrate` process only | Core developer |
| `chess-app core :: JournalCodec` | Core part (pure) | `chess-app` (conversion between a domain `Fact` and a journal record, §7.6; added by amendment 7, D-41; no function is allocated to it yet) | every process | Core developer |
| `chess.db + journals` | Data store | `~/Library/Application Support/Chess/` | the data directory | Core developer |
| `stockfish` | Supporting block | `third_party/stockfish/` build output | child of each process | Core developer |
| `Process: chess (one binary on PATH)`, `Process: chess serve (same binary, started by the user)`, `Process: Chess.app (Tauri desktop, ad-hoc signed local build)` | Process boundaries | — | used for process-level functions only | — |
| `packaging/`, `third_party/stockfish/`, `CI checks`, `Release checklist and install docs` | Enabling-system blocks | §2.1 | build time | Release engineer |

The `User's Mac` boundary is used only for the top function F-0. A cell that names two or three front-end blocks (for example `Browser tab; WKWebView`) means one behaviour realised once per front-end codebase (FG-4); no split of responsibility is implied.

## 3. Code organisation

### 3.1 Repository layout (baseline; a two-way door that may change during implementation)

```
chess/
├── Cargo.toml                 # Rust workspace
├── crates/
│   ├── chess-rules/           # board, move gen, SAN/UCI parse+format, FEN, PGN,      (pure)
│   │                          #   ECO table + position lookup, built from third_party/eco (D-20, D-75)
│   ├── chess-domain/          # game state machine, policy, clock, Elo, roster,       (pure)
│   │                          #   variation tree, stats aggregation, ASCII renderer,
│   │                          #   ECO classification (deepest named position) over rules' eco::lookup (D-20, D-75)
│   ├── chess-engine/          # Engine trait, UCI driver, Stockfish process pool,
│   │                          #   computer personalities, FakeEngine
│   ├── chess-store/           # SQLite schema, migrations, repositories, export/import
│   ├── chess-app/             # THE shared core: services, game session actors,
│   │                          #   engine gateway, protocol types (→ TS via ts-rs)
│   ├── chess-server/          # axum: static GUI assets + WebSocket protocol endpoint
│   └── chess-cli/             # bin "chess": ratatui TUI, plain mode, flags, `serve`
├── desktop/
│   └── src-tauri/             # Tauri shell: links chess-app, exposes commands/events
├── ui/                        # TypeScript GUI (Vite + React), shared by web & desktop
│   └── src/
│       ├── protocol/          # generated TS types (do not edit)
│       ├── transport/         # CoreClient interface + ws.ts + tauri.ts
│       ├── board/             # Board contract, BoardModel reducer, AccessibleOverlay,
│       │                      #   chessground adapter + Reconciler, PromotionPicker
│       ├── screens/           # register, home, setup, play, watch, analysis, history,
│       │                      #   stats, computer players, players, import/export, settings, help
│       └── themes/            # ≥10 board themes, ≥5 piece sets
├── e2e/
│   ├── scenarios/             # front-end-neutral scenario files (setup + moves + expected)
│   ├── cli/                   # PTY runner (vt100 screen parsing for the TUI)
│   └── gui/                   # Playwright runner (web + desktop)
├── third_party/stockfish/     # pinned version, build script, LICENSE, source offer
├── packaging/                 # Chess.app/.dmg build, Info.plist, ad-hoc signing (§8.2). `install-cli` is a subcommand of `chess`, not a script
├── ci/                        # cargo-deny config, npm licence check, lipo/bundle tests, scheduled (nightly) calibrate job (§2.1)
├── release/                   # CHECKLIST.md: platform matrix, FIDE Laws edition, engine facts, licence result (§2.1)
└── docs/
```

### 3.2 Crate dependency graph

*Amended 2026-10-31 (AMD-1, D-38). The known open issue of the SDD triage is resolved here: 5.7 already named three `chess-app` services as consumers of `chess-rules`, and the graph had no `chess-app -> chess-rules` edge. The edge is added, and one rule keeps the front-ends from reaching below `chess-app`.*

Question this view answers: which crate may call which. Arrows point from a crate to the crates it depends on. Pure crates have no I/O dependencies. Dotted arrows are third-party or generated-code relations.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    cli["chess-cli<br/>(bin: chess)"]
    server["chess-server"]
    tauri["desktop/src-tauri"]
    app["chess-app<br/>shared core + protocol<br/>the only facade"]
    engine["chess-engine"]
    store["chess-store"]
    domain["chess-domain<br/>(pure)"]
    rules["chess-rules<br/>(pure)"]
    ui["ui/ (TypeScript)"]

    cli --> server
    cli --> app
    server --> app
    tauri --> app
    app --> engine
    app --> store
    app --> domain
    app --> rules
    engine --> rules
    store --> domain
    domain --> rules

    app -. "ts-rs generates<br/>protocol types (D-7)" .-> ui
    server -. "embeds built assets" .-> ui
    tauri -. "bundles built assets" .-> ui

    subgraph ext["Third-party dependencies (versions in 3.4, [UNV] = pending lookup)"]
        direction LR
        shak["shakmaty + pgn-reader<br/>(D-3)"]
        tui["ratatui + crossterm<br/>(D-6)"]
        axum["axum + rust-embed"]
        sql["rusqlite (bundled SQLite)"]
        tokio["tokio<br/>(features per crate)"]
        uni["unicode-segmentation +<br/>unicode-normalization<br/>(PLY-6)"]
        tomlLib["toml<br/>(config.toml, line numbers)"]
        tauriLib["Tauri"]
        sfbin["Stockfish binary<br/>(pinned, bundled, D-9)"]
        react["React + Vite<br/>(D-5)"]
        cgLib["@lichess-org/chessground<br/>(D-4)"]
        serdeL["serde + serde_json<br/>[UNV]"]
        uuidL["uuid, v5 feature<br/>[UNV]"]
        tsrsL["ts-rs<br/>(D-7)"]
        shaL["SHA-256 crate<br/>[UNV]"]
        zipL["ZIP crate<br/>[UNV]"]
        clapL["clap"]
    end

    rules -.-> shak
    cli -.-> tui
    server -.-> axum
    store -.-> sql
    app -.-> tokio
    app -.-> uni
    cli -.-> tomlLib
    cli -.-> clapL
    tauri -.-> tauriLib
    engine -.-> sfbin
    domain -.-> serdeL
    store -.-> serdeL
    domain -.-> uuidL
    store -.-> uuidL
    domain -. "feature ts,<br/>enabled by chess-app" .-> tsrsL
    app -.-> shaL
    app -.-> zipL
    ui -.-> react
    ui -.-> cgLib
```

**Edge rule (D-38).**
1. **`chess-app` is the only facade.** `chess-cli`, `chess-server` and `src-tauri` depend on `chess-app` (and `chess-cli` on `chess-server`, for `serve`). They have **no** edge to `chess-rules`, `chess-domain`, `chess-store` or `chess-engine`. `chess-engine` has no edge to `chess-domain`, and `chess-store` has no edge to `chess-rules`.
2. **`chess-app -> chess-rules` is an architecture correction, not a new need.** A direct edge is clearer than a re-export through `chess-domain`, which would make the pure domain crate carry the API of another crate. `chess-domain` and `chess-store` re-export no `chess-rules` type. The store keeps its own small stored-side enums in its journal records.
3. **What a front-end may use from below `chess-app`** is exactly the curated list of named `pub use` items in the `chess-app` `lib.rs`: no glob re-exports, and every item names its consumer in the app design. At the start that list holds the board-rendering types from `chess-domain` (`BoardGrid`, `BoardStyle`, `BoardView`, `Cell`, `Shade`, `Highlight`, `Orientation`; *Addendum A, FIND-JR-005:* `Orientation` was missing, and "the cell types" is replaced by names so FF-1 can compare the list), the calibration items for `chess-cli` (the run function, `CalibrationPlan` and `CalibrationReport`; names proposed, D-57), and, behind the Cargo feature `test-support`, `Seams`, `Core::open`, `Core::open_for_calibration` and the `FakeEngine` factory (item 4). `DataDir` is not re-exported. A fitness function checks the list (FF-1, §9).
4. **Production wiring is inside `chess-app`.** `Core::open_default(CoreConfig)` builds the production engine factory, system clock and OS random source. `Core::open(cfg, Seams)` stays as the test and calibration entry, with `Seams = { engine: Arc<dyn EngineFactory>, time: Arc<dyn TimeSource>, rng: Arc<dyn Rng>, data_dir: DataDir }` (D-39). *Addendum A (FIND-JR-006; D-61):* **`chess-app` defines the `EngineFactory` trait and also writes the production factory**, over the concrete public spawn API of `chess-engine` (the pool and handle constructors). `chess-engine` supplies no factory, needs no change and gains no edge. `FakeEngine` implements the same `Engine` trait as the production engine, and the test factory in `chess-app` implements `EngineFactory` over it. The feature `test-support` of `chess-app` exposes that test factory (an `EngineFactory` that returns scripted `FakeEngine`s) and a helper that opens a `Core` over a temp-directory store, so the tests of `chess-cli`, `chess-server` and `src-tauri` can build a `Core` without an edge to `chess-engine` or `chess-store`. The feature turns on `chess-engine`'s own `test-support` feature; `chess-app -> chess-engine` already exists, so this adds no edge. Names and shapes are fixed in the app design (G1). `chess_app::resolve_data_dir(flag, env)` is a thin wrapper over the store's resolver, so the data-directory logic has one owner (the store). `Core::build_info()` returns `{build_version, protocol_version}`, and `chess_app::new_game_uuid()` serves the CLI. The GUI keeps Web Crypto for `game_uuid` (§5.6).
5. **Typed moves** are parsed by `chess-rules` alone, reached from front-ends through `Query::ParseMove` (§5.1, D-45). The CLI therefore needs no `Position`.
6. **Wire types.** The few domain value types that appear in commands (`GameSetup`, `Mode`, `Matchup`, `ClockSpec`, `Category`, `Preferences`) derive `ts-rs` behind the Cargo feature `ts`, which `chess-app` enables. The domain stays free of I/O. The domain `Command` for `Game::decide` is renamed `GameCommand` so it cannot be confused with `protocol::Command`. No exposed `i64` or `u64` field can exceed 2^53 (millisecond timestamps are fine). `serde` is a dependency of `chess-domain` and `chess-store` only; `chess-rules` and `chess-engine` stay free of it.

Gate: G2 (a dependency edge and the public surface of `chess-app`). Door: two-way before code, harder after (D-38, §11.3).

### 3.3 Crate responsibilities

| Crate | Responsibility | Key types | Requirements |
|---|---|---|---|
| `chess-rules` | Positions, legal-move generation, check/mate/stalemate, draw detection, SAN and coordinate parsing (lenient: either case, optional `+ # x`, `0-0`), SAN formatting, FEN, PGN with variations. Wraps `shakmaty` + `pgn-reader` (GPL-3.0, already perft-verified) rather than writing move generation from scratch (D-3, AGREED). *Amd 7 (AMD-1, AMD-3, D-45):* `Position::parse_move(text, Typed)` is the **only** typed-move parser; there is no CLI or TypeScript matcher. `Fen` gains `as_str`/`Display`. `canonical_form` (tags and SAN movetext) is the input of the content hash (D-42); the hash itself is computed in `chess-app`. Termination detection (mate, stalemate, dead position, repetition, fifty-move) is a query of this crate that the domain invokes (AMD-10). *Amd 8 Addendum A (D-75, readiness C-1):* the **ECO table and the position lookup `eco::lookup(&PositionKey) -> Option<EcoEntry{code, name}>`** live here: the table is generated at build time by a `build.rs` from the pinned snapshot in `third_party/eco/` (D-20). The lookup knows positions only; the game-level rule is in `chess-domain`. | `Position`, `Move`, `San`, `Fen`, `PgnGame`, `MatchedMove` (renamed from `ParsedMove`, D-52), `PositionKey`, `EcoEntry` (D-75) | RULE-1, CLI-3, RULE-6/7/8, IF-1..3, DAT-3 (position lookup) |
| `chess-domain` | Game state machine; `Policy` (mode × matchup × state → allowed actions); `Clock` with an injected `TimeSource`; Elo calculator (K 40→32, no clamp, exclusions); class bands; seeded roster and personality generator; variation tree; stats aggregation; ECO classification of a game: the deepest-named-position rule over the `eco::lookup` of `chess-rules` (D-20, D-75); terminal-independent board renderer that produces a grid of styled cells (glyph, square shade, highlight) for each style (plain, Unicode, ANSI, no-color), used by both the TUI and plain mode. *Amd 7 (AMD-1, AMD-10, D-38 to D-44):* `GameId(Uuid)` and `PlayerId(Uuid)` newtypes (D-40); `Personality` is the stored and identity type with `error_permille: u16` (D-43); the `TimeSource` and `Rng` traits live here and their implementations in `chess-app`; `SplitMix64` is duplicated in `chess-engine` with a shared test-vector file, and `error_pick_seed(GameId, ply) -> u64` is the one seed function; `BoardGrid::from_fen(fen, &BoardView, style, orientation)` with `BoardView.last_move` as UCI text, so no front-end needs a `Position`; `aggregate`, `rating_series`, `check_history` and `counts_in_aggregates` own the statistics predicate and the empty-state rule (D-44); the domain `Command` is `GameCommand`; `ts-rs` derive behind the feature `ts` on six value types; `Fact` is the vocabulary of state changes (§7.6). The domain owns the precedence of end reasons: checkmate, stalemate, dead position, threefold repetition, fifty-move rule, with checkmate on the move that completes the fifty-move count standing (RULE-1); only that rule changes a result, the others change the label (SE interpretation Q-SE-4, confirmed). The TUI Elo chart renderer belongs to `chess-cli` (D-37); the domain exports series data only. | `Game`, `GameSetup`, `Policy`, `Action`, `Clock`, `EloCalc`, `RosterGen`, `VariationTree`, `EcoTable`, `BoardGrid`, `BoardStyle` | PLY-1/5, GAME-1/3, RULE-2/3/4, ELO-1/2, PLY-4, ENG-2, ANA-2, DAT-3 (ECO), UI-2/5, CLI-1/2, UI-3 |
| `chess-engine` | `Engine` trait; UCI driver over async stdin/stdout; process pool; timeouts, crash detection, restart; engine binary discovery (D-18); `EnginePersonality` → UCI options and search limits, plus seeded "random error" move selection; `FakeEngine` for tests. *Amd 7 (AMD-3, AMD-7, D-43, D-46):* the engine takes `search(SearchRequest)` and hands out `EngineHandle`s from an `EnginePool`; `EnginePersonality` carries `error_rate: f64` (= permille / 1000), converted by one function in `chess-app`; there is **no** edge to `chess-domain`; the `UCI_Elo` range is the constant `PINNED_UCI_ELO_RANGE` of the bundled binary (value unknown until ASM-3 is closed). | `Engine`, `UciEngine`, `EnginePool`, `EngineHandle`, `SearchRequest`, `SearchLimits`, `EnginePersonality`, `FakeEngine` | ENG-1/2/3, NFR-3 |
| `chess-store` | SQLite access (`rusqlite`, bundled SQLite), WAL, busy timeout, versioned migrations with a pre-migration backup, schema-version guard (D-16), repositories, game leases, analysis job queue, bulk row read/write for export and import (formats are owned by `ImportExportService`), `MoveJournal` (append + `F_FULLFSYNC`, `flock`, replay; §7.6). Runs on its own blocking thread (D-13). *Amd 7 (AMD-2, AMD-5, D-39, D-40, D-44):* `Store` is one cheap clonable **concrete handle** to the store thread, not a trait, and `chess-app` holds it directly (no `Arc<dyn Store>`, no generic `Core<S>`). The store API takes and returns `GameId`/`PlayerId` (UUID); integer row ids never cross the store boundary. The store returns **rows** for statistics, filtered only by structural keys (Player, reset window, side, limit); the SQL views `player_stats` and `head_to_head` are dropped. `roster_indexes() -> Vec<u32>` (every stored `roster_index`) serves the roster (§4.6); the store holds a flat `roster_index` and knows nothing of the interleaved scheme (Addendum A, ruling 3, OQ-21). | `Store`, `PlayerRepo`, `GameRepo`, `RatingRepo`, `JobQueue`, `Lease`, `MoveJournal` | DAT-1/2/3, RULE-5, NFR-2 |
| `chess-app` | **The shared core.** Name validation (PLY-6: trim, NFC, grapheme-cluster count, control-character check, using `unicode-segmentation` and `unicode-normalization`). The one `ConfirmationRequired` mechanism (UI-12). The diagnostic log writer (NFR-10). Application services that combine domain, store, and engine. One actor per live game. `EngineGateway` (the only path to the engine, policy-checked). Background analysis worker. The protocol: `Command`, `Query`, `Event`, `Snapshot` DTOs and the error catalogue (§5.5). *Amd 7 (AMD-1, AMD-3, AMD-6, D-38 to D-46):* the **only facade** for the front-ends (§3.2): `Core::open_default`, `Core::open(cfg, Seams)`, `resolve_data_dir`, `build_info`, `new_game_uuid`, the curated `pub use` list, `FrontEndSession`. Owns the calibration run (matches, SPRT, report; *Addendum A*, D-57), `JournalCodec` (pure conversion between a domain `Fact` and a journal record, §7.6), `personality_to_engine` (the one conversion from the stored `Personality` to `EnginePersonality`), the content hash function (SHA-256, §5.7), and the production seams. | `Core`, `FrontEndSession`, `GameSession`, `EngineGateway`, `AnalysisWorker`, `JournalCodec`, `Command`, `Event`, `GameSnapshot` | PLY-1..5, GAME-1..4, RULE-2..8, ENG-3/5, DAT-2/3, ELO-1/2, UI-1/2/5, ANA-1..3 (allocation in §10) |

Holds no rules. Controls come from `allowed_actions`. | `CoreClient`, `BoardProps`, `BoardModel`, `AccessibleOverlay`, `Screen`s | GUI-1..4, UI-1..5, GAME-3 (view cursor), PLAT-2 |

### 3.4 Baseline versions (CR-7.3, FIND-006)

The user's rule: use the latest version of every open-source component and language as of the baselined requirements. "Baselined" is taken as the date of the last baseline amendment, **2026-10-28**. Each version below is the highest stable (not pre-release, not yanked) release published on or before that date, read on 2026-10-30 from crates.io, the npm registry, the Rust release channel (`channel-rust-stable.toml`), nodejs.org and the Stockfish GitHub releases `[RAN]`. `Cargo.lock` and `package-lock.json` record the exact resolved set at implementation; a dependency is raised later only through a normal change, with a CVE scan (§8). A version that needs a newer toolchain than the one installed is a reason to update the toolchain, not to pick an older version.

**Toolchain and test-tool policy (D-76, Amendment 8 Addendum B; Brian Prascher, G2, approved 2026-11-04).** The project deploys on many machines and has no nightly capability, so **no nightly Rust toolchain is used, ever**, and no document, CI job or script may require one. ("Nightly" in this document's CI jobs, for example the nightly calibration run, means a scheduled overnight run on the stable toolchain.) **Admitted:** `cargo-llvm-cov` **0.9.1** as the coverage tool of the 90 % line-coverage gate in test-strategy: published 2026-09-06, Apache-2.0 OR MIT, MSRV 1.87, no advisory on the tool itself, installed on the development Mac with the stable `llvm-tools` component `[RAN 2026-11-04, dependency-lookup-step1.md E3, E4, E9]`; a developer and CI binary, not linked into the product. **Not admitted:** `cargo-fuzz` 0.13.2 (it needs a nightly compiler `[OBS dependency-lookup-step1.md F4]`); `libfuzzer-sys` 0.4.13 (its only consumer is a `cargo-fuzz` target crate; licence `(MIT OR Apache-2.0) AND NCSA`); `arbitrary` 1.5.0 (it only structures fuzz-target input; the `arbitrary` feature of `shakmaty` is not enabled). **`cargo-nextest` is not adopted for now (D-81, Addendum D, 2026-11-04).** The copy on the development Mac, 0.9.128, is in the affected range of RUSTSEC-2026-0412 `[RAN dependency-lookup-step1.md E3, E9; F3]`, so no document, CI job or script may require it, and the test suite runs with plain `cargo test` on the stable toolchain (`cargo llvm-cov` wraps `cargo test`). Adopting it later needs a fresh lookup of a patched version and a G2 decision. The lookup report of 2026-11-04 recorded the advisory as patched from 0.9.150 `[OBS lookup F3]`; this document does not re-verify that `[UNV]` and pins no nextest version. **Not decided, and therefore not admitted:** `cargo-insta` (not looked up). NFR-3 is verified by stable-only means (§9).

No version is stated here.** Admitted in principle (user decision 11, with the changes after the table). ## 4. The shared core (`chess-app`)

### 4.1 Internal structure

```mermaid
flowchart TB
    subgraph fe["Front-end adapters"]
        direction LR
        cliA["CLI adapter<br/>TUI · plain · headless<br/>(direct calls)"]
        wsA["WebSocket adapter<br/>(chess-server)"]
        tauriA["Tauri adapter<br/>(src-tauri)"]
    end

    subgraph core["chess-app :: Core"]
        api["Protocol dispatcher<br/>Command / Query → Result<br/>Event stream (per-game subscription)"]
        players["PlayerService<br/>register (self-assessment → Elo), name rules, rename,<br/>archive, active Player, preferences, computer roster"]
        setup["GameSetupService<br/>validate GameSetup"]
        sessions["SessionRegistry<br/>one GameSession actor per live game<br/>clocks · leases · allowed_actions · resume list"]
        analysis["AnalysisService<br/>Training live analysis,<br/>post-game analysis, what-if"]
        stats["StatsService<br/>aggregates (self-play excluded),<br/>Elo history, any Player"]
        io["ImportExportService<br/>PGN / FEN (review only) / full-data backup"]
        gateway["EngineGateway<br/>purpose-tagged, policy-checked, audited"]
        worker["AnalysisWorker<br/>background per-move evals<br/>(finished games only)"]
        codec["JournalCodec<br/>domain fact to journal record<br/>and back (pure)"]
        ctxp["GameContextProvider (trait, D-74)<br/>live session snapshot,<br/>else the GAME row"]
    end

    subgraph pure["chess-domain (pure)"]
        direction LR
        policy["Policy<br/>mode × matchup × state → allowed actions"]
        game["Game state machine<br/>Clock · EloCalc · RosterGen"]
    end

    subgraph edges["Edges (traits only where more than one implementation exists)"]
        direction LR
        eng["Engine pool<br/>(Stockfish | Fake)"]
        clk["TimeSource<br/>(system | fake)"]
        rng["Rng<br/>(seeded)"]
    end

    st["Store (chess-store)<br/>one concrete handle, SQLite file<br/>(a temp directory in tests)"]

    cliA --> api
    wsA --> api
    tauriA --> api
    api -. "events" .-> cliA
    api -. "events" .-> wsA
    api -. "events" .-> tauriA
    api --> players
    api --> setup
    api --> sessions
    api --> analysis
    api --> stats
    api --> io
    setup --> policy
    sessions --> policy
    sessions --> game
    gateway --> policy
    sessions --> gateway
    analysis --> gateway
    worker --> gateway
    gateway --> eng
    players --> st
    sessions --> st
    stats --> st
    io --> st
    worker --> st
    sessions --> clk
    sessions --> rng
    sessions --> codec
    players --> sessions
    setup --> sessions
    analysis --> st
    gateway --> st
    gateway --> ctxp
    sessions -. "implements,<br/>publishes snapshots" .-> ctxp
    ctxp --> st
```

**Amendment 7 (AMD-2, AMD-10, D-39).** The store is drawn outside the "edges" box because it is **not** a trait: `chess-app` holds the concrete `chess_store::Store` handle, and tests use a real store in a temp directory with fault injection (`FaultPlan`) instead of a fake. A SQLite `:memory:` database cannot use WAL, so the earlier "in-memory store" wording could not be met anyway `[INF]`. If the core integration suite becomes too slow (FF-2, §9), a trait and a fake are introduced then; the change is mechanical. Four intra-core edges the design needs were missing from this diagram and are added: `PlayerService -> SessionRegistry` (void of a live game on reset, §7.2), `GameSetupService -> SessionRegistry` (slot and live-game checks), `AnalysisService -> Store` (`VARIATION`), and `EngineGateway -> Store` (`analysis_used`). `APP_SETTING` has its writer named: `PlayerService` (§7.4). `JournalCodec` (§7.6) is the one place where a domain `Fact` becomes a journal record. *Amd 8 Addendum A (D-74, CONFIRMED by Brian as a G2 decision on 2026-11-04, Addendum D):* **OQ-13 is answered.** The gateway gets the game state it checks through `GameContextProvider`, a trait private to `chess-app`. `SessionRegistry` implements it (the live session's last published snapshot, else the `GAME` row read through the store), and the gateway never names the registry or a session, so there is no cycle with `SessionRegistry -> EngineGateway`. The diagram gains one node and three edges (`EngineGateway -> GameContextProvider`, `SessionRegistry -. implements .-> GameContextProvider`, `GameContextProvider -> Store`). They are intra-crate, so §3.2 is unchanged (no crate edge); they carry the tier G2 as a precaution, and Brian confirmed D-74 as a G2 decision (Addendum D).

**Allocation names.** The boxes in this diagram are the normative sub-blocks of `chess-app core` (§2.2). A function or requirement allocated to `chess-app core :: PlayerService` is the service's job, whichever process runs it. The three front-end adapters are `CLI front-end`, `axum HTTP + WebSocket` and `Tauri shell and IPC adapter` (D-32).

**Cross-cutting duties of the core** (they have no box of their own because every service uses them):
- **Confirmation.** A command that needs a user decision returns `ConfirmationRequired` until it carries `confirmed: true` (§5.5). The services that own the actions listed in UI-12 are the only callers (D-33). The check is one shared helper in `chess-app`, not a box of its own, so the functional decomposition allocates F-2.5.7 to `chess-app core`, not to the Protocol dispatcher; the dispatcher only routes the command.
- **All-or-nothing.** Each store-changing command is one store-thread transaction or, for restore and migration, a copy-then-rename. A failure leaves the store as it was and returns a catalogue error whose message states the next step (DAT-7, NFR-9, D-35).
- **Diagnostic log.** `chess-app core :: Diagnostic log` writes identifiers and error detail, never names or move lists (NFR-10, §10.2).

### 4.2 Game sessions as actors

Each live game is one `GameSession`: a tokio task that owns that game's `chess_domain::Game` value. Commands reach it over an `mpsc` channel, and it publishes `Event`s on a `broadcast` channel. Every front-end — including two hot-seat humans on one screen — subscribes to the same stream.

- Commands are handled one at a time, so a game cannot race with itself: a human move, a flag falling, and an engine reply can never interleave.
- **Optimistic concurrency guards rewind (GAME-3).** Every move command carries the `ply` the client believes is current. If a client is rewound, or a computer move landed meanwhile, the `ply` does not match and the command is rejected with `StalePosition`. Rewind itself is purely client-side view state: the snapshot includes the FEN and SAN of every ply, so stepping back needs no engine and no server round trip, and it cannot touch the record.
- **Computer turns** are spawned as cancellable engine tasks whose results return to the actor as messages. The actor stays responsive while the engine thinks: a human can resign, a watcher can pause or abort, and a flag can fall (ENG-5, NFR-8, GAME-2).
- **Clocks** are domain values driven by an injected `TimeSource`. The actor arms a single timer for the active side's flag deadline. **Every state that waits for a decision stops both clocks**: `Paused` and `DrawOffered` (RULE-2, GAME-2). The `Clock` value then has `running_side = None`, the flag timer is disarmed, and the next `Clock` journal record carries the stopped values. Clients receive `{white_ms, black_ms, running_side, as_of}` and count down locally, so there is no stream of tick events. The core alone decides when a flag falls.

### 4.3 Game state machine

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    [*] --> Setup
    Setup --> InProgress: StartGame (setup validated by Policy)

    state InProgress {
        [*] --> AwaitingMove
        AwaitingMove --> EngineThinking: side to move is computer
        EngineThinking --> AwaitingMove: engine move applied (CancelEngine = play best so far)
        EngineThinking --> EngineError: engine crash / timeout / missing binary
        EngineError --> EngineThinking: retry (engine restarted)
        AwaitingMove --> AwaitingMove: human move applied (ply checked)
        AwaitingMove --> AwaitingMove: Takeback (Training only), undoes the human move and the computer reply
        EngineThinking --> AwaitingMove: Takeback (Training only), search cancelled and the human move undone
        AwaitingMove --> DrawOffered: OfferDraw (own turn, none pending, not blocked after a decline), all clocks stop
        DrawOffered --> AwaitingMove: declined, clocks restart with the same side to move
        DrawOffered --> AwaitingMove: engine failure drops the offer to a computer (ENG-4)
        AwaitingMove --> Paused: Pause (computer–computer watcher)
        EngineThinking --> Paused: Pause (engine search cancelled)
        Paused --> EngineThinking: Step (exactly one move)
        Paused --> AwaitingMove: Resume
    }

    note left of InProgress
        Paused and DrawOffered freeze both
        clocks (GAME-2, RULE-2). EngineError does
        not: the side to move keeps its clock
        running (D-15, Q53). An offer to a human
        survives a suspend and resume. An offer to
        a computer is dropped by a suspend.
    end note

    note right of InProgress
        Rewind is client-side view state only:
        it never changes this machine, and clocks
        keep running while a human is rewound.
    end note

    InProgress --> Suspended: app closed / crash / power loss / tab closed or disconnected (D-14)<br/>clocks frozen at last recorded value (Q54). Not an abort: no Elo or stats effect
    Suspended --> InProgress: ResumeGame (journal lock acquired, journal replayed)

    InProgress --> Completed: mate · stalemate · threefold · 50-move ·<br/>insufficient material · flag · draw agreed (computer accepts only if eval ≤ 0) ·<br/>Resign (confirmed, h–h asks which side resigns)
    Suspended --> Completed: Resign from the resume prompt (human games, frees the slot)
    InProgress --> Aborted: Abort (c–c watcher only, confirmed)
    Suspended --> Aborted: Abort from the resume prompt (c–c only, confirmed)
    InProgress --> Voided: confirmed reset of a human Player in this game (D-26, D-30)
    Suspended --> Voided: confirmed reset of a human Player in this game (D-26, D-30)
    Suspended --> Voided: user voids a game whose journal and store are unreadable (DAT-7, D-35)
    Completed --> Voided: replay only, a journal that holds Ended then Voided (D-67 v, D-87), never produced live

    Completed --> Finalized: one transaction: result + times + W/L/D,<br/>Elo on the game's track (Training or Strict) for both sides,<br/>unless self-play or custom FEN
    Finalized --> [*]
    Aborted --> [*]
    Voided --> [*]

    note right of Aborted
        c–c only. No result, no Elo, no stats
        for either computer. Kept in history
        and can be analysed.
    end note

    note right of Voided
        h–h and h–c only (only humans are reset).
        No result, no Elo, no W/L/D, no rating
        history point for any side. Every slot
        of the game is released. Kept in the
        opponent history and can be analysed.
        Not a resignation (D-30). An unreadable
        game (DAT-7) is voided by the user with
        termination voided: unreadable.
    end note

    note right of Finalized
        Post-game analysis (ANA-3) and the
        background per-move evaluations (DAT-3)
        start only after this point.
    end note
```

### 4.4 Mode policy as a single table

`Policy::check(action, &GameContext) -> Result<(), Denied>` is a pure function and the single source of truth. `GameContext` holds the mode, matchup, game state, whose turn it is, whether that side is human or computer, and whether the game is still in progress.

| Action | Training (h–c) | Strict (h–c, h–h, c–c) |
|---|---|---|
| Takeback | ✅ to the human's turn. Stops at the human's first turn. During a search, undoes one move; after the computer's reply, undoes both (D-33). Moves taken back leave the record. Nothing to undo: `NothingToTakeBack` | ❌ |
| Live analysis / what-if on the current game | ✅ human only | ❌ until the game ends |
| Rewind (client view) | ✅ human | ✅ human (either hot-seat human); ❌ c–c has no human player, but watchers may scroll the move list |
| Clock selection | ❌ always untimed | ✅ presets + custom |
| Offer draw (humans only; a computer never offers, Q42). Only on the offerer's own turn, never during a search, never while an offer is pending, and not again after a decline until the offerer has moved (D-33). All clocks stop while pending | ✅ to the computer, which accepts only if its eval ≤ 0. The decision runs within the ENG-3 maximum think time and cannot be withdrawn | ✅ h–h: to the other human, no time-out, moves blocked until answered. h–c: to the computer (eval ≤ 0 rule). ❌ c–c |
| Pause / step / delay | ❌ | c–c only |
| Resign (always confirmed) — the only way a human ends a game early. Also from the setup or resume offer (`Suspended` games) | ✅ human: counts as a loss for Training Elo and W/L/D | ✅ h–c, h–h: loss for Strict Elo and W/L/D. In h–h the front-end asks which side resigns, then confirms, from the game and from the resume offer. A self-play resignation has no Elo or W/L/D effect (self-play is unrated). Resigning a custom-start game is recorded in the game record only (H-17). ❌ c–c |
| Abort (always confirmed) | ❌ | c–c watcher only. Neither computer's Elo or stats change |
| Start a new game while a slot-holding Player has an unfinished game in this mode (D-27) | ❌ `UnfinishedGameExists`: resume it, or resign it. Only the human holds a Training slot. The computer is never blocked | ❌ same. h–h: each human holds a slot (one for self-play). h–c: only the human; the computer's slot is neither used nor checked, so any Player can always play any computer. c–c: each computer holds its Strict slot, and the setup offers resume or abort |
| CancelEngine ("move now", ENG-5). Front-end forms: command `movenow`, key F2, GUI "Move now" button (D-33) | ✅ only in `EngineThinking` in an h–c game | ✅ only in `EngineThinking` in an h–c game; ❌ c–c (use Pause); shown unavailable in every other state |
| Elo track | **Training Elo** for both sides, human and computer (computers have a Training Elo too) | **Strict Elo** for both sides, including c–c between different computers. Never for self-play or a custom FEN start |
| Counts in W/L/D | ✅ (filterable by mode) | ✅. Self-play is excluded in both modes |
| Post-game analysis | ✅ | ✅ |

**Player-management and setup rows (AMD-14, D-38).** The domain function `check_player_action` and the slot function `slot_holders` own these rules, and `chess-app` calls them and maps the result to `Denied` (SE desk answer Q-SE-3, interpretation confirmed). The `WhatIf` precondition is the domain's too and adds "a human is to move".

| Action | Rule | Refusal |
|---|---|---|
| Rename, archive or reset a computer Player | Refused (PLY-4) | `Denied{computer_player}` |
| Archive the Player this front-end session has selected | Refused (PLY-4) | `Denied{active_player}` |
| Archive a Player with an unfinished game | Refused; the message names the games | `PlayerHasUnfinishedGames{game_ids}` (its own code, §5.5). The triage proposal of a `game_open` reason is **not** added, because this situation already has this code |
| Choose an archived Player as a side in a new game | Not offered in any list (PLY-2: archived Players are hidden). The core still validates it, because another front-end can archive between listing and starting | `Denied{archived_player}` (additive, §5.6) |
| Switch the active Player (`SelectProfile`) while a game is open in this front-end session (PLY-2: "only while no game is open"; *Addendum A*, D-56) | Refused by `PlayerService`, not by the domain, because the check needs the `FrontEndSession`. A game is **open** in a session when the session holds a subscription to it or the handle of its `GameSession` (a live game on its screen). A suspended game that no session has attached does not count, and neither does a game open in another front-end. The first selection at launch passes (nothing is open) | `Denied{reason: state_not_allowed}` with `details.game_ids`. **No `game_open` reason is needed** (D-56) |

**In-play action rules (D-33).** The rules below are the architecture's reading of requirements RULE-2, RULE-3, ENG-5, GAME-1 and GAME-4 (CR-4.4, CR-4.6, CR-4.7, CR-4.8).
- **Takeback.** The earliest position reachable is the human's first turn: the start position when the human has White; the position after the computer's first move when the human has Black; from a custom FEN, the first position at which it was the human's turn. In `EngineThinking` the search is cancelled and exactly one move, the human's, is undone, so it is the human's turn again. After the computer's reply, both moves are undone. When there is nothing to undo (the earliest position, or the computer is making its opening move), the command returns `NothingToTakeBack` ("Nothing to take back"). **Taken-back moves are removed** from the `MOVE` rows, so from the stored move list, the exported PGN, the per-move times and any evaluation; the append-only journal keeps the `Takeback{to_ply}` record, so replay reaches the same state. A takeback does not change the game's rating track: it stays rated on the Training scale. `analysis_used` is unaffected.
- **Draw offer.** Allowed only on the offerer's own turn, not during a search, and not while an offer is pending. After a decline the same side may offer again only after it has completed another move (`Game` holds `offer_blocked_for: Option<Side>`, cleared when that side moves). While an offer is pending all clocks are stopped, and on a decline they restart with the same side to move. An offer to a human has no time-out and blocks moves until it is answered. An offer to a computer starts `ComputerDrawDecision`, which must finish within the ENG-3 maximum think time, shows the thinking indicator, and cannot be withdrawn. An engine failure drops the offer (ENG-4). Acceptance ends the game 1/2-1/2 with termination "draw agreed". Rejected attempts return `Denied` with the reasons `draw_not_own_turn`, `draw_engine_busy`, `draw_offer_pending`, `draw_repeat_blocked`, `draw_not_available` (c–c, or a Strict-only or human-only rule).
- **Move now.** `CancelEngine` is accepted only in `EngineThinking` of an h–c game. The command word is `movenow`, the key is F2 (TUI and GUI), and the GUI thinking indicator carries a "Move now" button that is keyboard reachable, has an ARIA label, and activates on Enter or Space. The core sends UCI `stop`, plays the best move found so far, and the move must land within 200 ms (TBD-12, §4.8). Elsewhere the control is shown unavailable.
- **Resume list.** `ListUnfinishedGames{player_id}` returns every unfinished game in which the active Player plays either side (the second side of a hot-seat game included; self-play appears once) **plus every unfinished c–c game**. It reads `GAME` for `status = 'in_progress'`, not the slot table, so a game held by a live session in another process is listed and marked "open in the CLI" (`GameLeased`). Resigning from the offer in a hot-seat game asks which side resigns, then confirms.

Every `GameSnapshot` includes `allowed_actions`, computed from this table. Front-ends render controls from that list and never re-derive the rules themselves. The table drives (a) table-driven unit tests over the full mode × matchup × state product, and (b) per-front-end E2E checks that the visible controls match `allowed_actions` exactly.

### 4.5 Engine gateway and Strict-mode integrity

Every engine call passes through `EngineGateway::request(purpose, …)`, where:

```rust
enum EnginePurpose {
    ComputerMove { game_id, player_id },          // a computer side choosing its move
    ComputerDrawDecision { game_id, player_id },  // the "eval ≤ 0" check (RULE-2); result never shown
    LiveAnalysis { game_id },                     // Training only
    WhatIf { game_id },                           // Training live, or any finished game
    PostGameAnalysis { game_id },                 // finished games only
    Calibration,                                  // dev/nightly tooling
}
```

The gateway looks up the game's state and mode and calls `Policy::check` before it touches the engine. *Amd 8 Addendum A (D-74, confirmed by Brian, G2):* it gets the state from `GameContextProvider` (§4.1) and never from its caller, and when no context can be produced (unknown game, store error) it fails closed: `Denied` or `Internal`, and the engine is not touched. Only `FreeAnalysis` and `Calibration`, which name no stored game, skip the lookup. `LiveAnalysis` or `WhatIf` on an in-progress Strict game returns `Denied`, even if a modified client sends it directly over the WebSocket. Every call is recorded in an in-memory audit log, which is the "spy on engine calls" required by ANA-1's verification. `Calibration` cannot be sent through the protocol. Only the calibration run of `chess-app` (D-57), which the `chess calibrate` dev subcommand calls, builds it, and it never names a user game.

The gateway also validates engine output. Every `bestmove`, including a seeded "error" pick, is checked for legality with `chess-rules` before the session applies it. An illegal or unparsable move counts as an engine failure (§4.7), so the game can never enter an illegal state (NFR-3).

### 4.6 Computer Players

- **Roster generation** (pure, `chess-domain`): `RosterGen::new(seed, uci_elo_range)` takes the pinned range as a plain value (below). The default roster is 5 Players per class. Each gets a human-readable name and a `Personality { skill_level, depth_limit, movetime_ms, uci_elo: Option<u32>, error_permille: u16, error_multipv }` (an integer permille, so the unique-parameter check of ENG-2 compares integers and never floats; the engine receives `error_rate = permille / 1000.0` from one conversion in `chess-app`, D-43) drawn within the class bounds, plus **two starting ratings, Strict Elo and Training Elo, both equal to its class baseline** (Beginner 400, Novice 600, Intermediate 1200, Master 2200, Grand Master 2500). That is the same rule as a human's self-assessment (Q41). From then on both ratings are **dynamic**: every rated game updates both sides on that game's track by the Elo formula, with the same K-factor rule as humans: 40 for the first 30 rated games **on that track**, then 32. The two tracks count separately (`strict_rated_games`, `training_rated_games`; Q78). The change is written to `RATING_HISTORY`. Its **engine parameters stay fixed** forever, so its rating converges toward its real strength. The **category label follows the current Strict Elo** (Q79) through the ELO-1 bands, never the Training Elo, while the engine settings stay those of the class it was created in. Generation rejects duplicate parameter sets across all computers (ENG-2). The result is persisted once and never regenerated (PLY-4, ENG-2). **One request creates N Players, 1 to 25, default 5 (TBD-31), in the chosen category** (`GenerateComputerPlayers{category, count}`). Each Player is a position in the seed's stream, numbered by the interleaved index `5*k + class` (`k` is the next free position within the class): `roster_index` fixes its personality and its UUID, which is derived deterministically as a UUIDv5 over the name string `chess-roster/v1/{seed}/{index}` (D-34, D-46), so an archive from another install matches by identity and DAT-2 prompts only for real conflicts. The **name** is the first unused candidate from that position's candidate names: the generator skips any name already used by any Player, human or computer, active or archived, so no collision prompt exists and no request can fail on a name. All N are inserted in one transaction or none (DAT-7). **Next index per class (AMD-7, amended by Addendum A, ruling 3, D-46):** the store holds a flat `roster_index` and **knows nothing of the interleaved `5*k + class` scheme** (Brian's option C, OQ-21). `StoreApi::roster_indexes() -> Vec<u32>` returns every stored `roster_index` (at most a few hundred integers, so it is cheap).
