(The name `next_roster_index` is dropped: the store no longer computes a "next".) `PlayerService` passes the list to the domain function `first_k_after(class, &stored) -> k`, which returns 0 when no stored index belongs to the class and otherwise one more than the largest *k* among the stored indices of the class (an index `5*k + c` belongs to class ordinal `c`); this widens the domain design's `highest_stored_index: Option<u32>` argument to a slice (G1 for the domain design). `PlayerService` then calls `RosterGen::generate(class, first_k, count, uci_range)`, and inserts the batch with one `insert_computers` transaction. The read and the insert are two store calls, not one transaction: `UNIQUE(roster_seed, roster_index)` is the arbiter. On `RosterIndexTaken` `PlayerService` repeats the whole sequence once (a fresh read) and then returns `StoreBusy`. A change to the interleaving therefore touches `chess-domain` only. Pools, bounds and the default seed stay editable until the first release and are permanent afterwards (D-34).
- **Playing a move:** limits = min(personality movetime, the active profile's max-think-time preference (UI-1), a clock-derived budget). Classes whose target strength lies inside the engine's `UCI_Elo` range use `UCI_LimitStrength`. *Amended 2026-10-31 (AMD-7, D-46).* The `UCI_Elo` range is a **pinned constant of the bundled engine**, `chess_engine::PINNED_UCI_ELO_RANGE`, set from the engine build step (ASM-3). It is not read from the handshake to generate Players, because that would make a permanent Player depend on the binary found at run time, and two machines (or an engine bump) would generate different Players from the same seed and index, which breaks D-34. The engine integration test EI-EN-002 compares the constant with the `min` and `max` of `option name UCI_Elo type spin …` in the real binary's `uci` handshake, fails CI when the engine pin changes without the constant (FF-7), and asserts that every stored `COMPUTER_PROFILE.uci_elo` lies inside the range. `chess-app` passes the constant to `RosterGen` and to the calibration run. At run time `EngineGateway` also clamps `UCI_Elo` into the handshake range for the **search only**, never for the stored value, and logs a mismatch with the constant as an installation defect. The first-launch roster no longer waits for the engine, so the lazy engine start and NFR-1 stay intact. **The value of the range is not written in any document**: it is unknown until the Stockfish 19 build is run (ASM-3) `[UNV]`. If a later engine changes the range, calibration is re-run before release. Beginner and Novice use a low `Skill Level`, shallow depth, and a random error: with probability `error_rate`, the core picks a non-best line from a MultiPV search. The seed is `error_pick_seed(game_id, ply)`, one function in `chess-domain` (it owns the `SplitMix64` generator and `GameId`), passed to the engine in `SearchRequest.seed`; the engine keeps its own copy of `SplitMix64` (about ten lines, shared test vectors, no `engine -> domain` edge, FF-8). Replays are therefore reproducible.
- **Calibration** is owned by `chess-app` (Addendum A, ruling 4, D-57). `chess_app::calibration` plays the matches between classes and reference settings, runs the SPRT and builds the report, on a `Core` from `Core::open_for_calibration(cfg)`, which refuses the default data directory (R-17). It plays through `EngineGateway` with `EnginePurpose::Calibration` (§4.5). The `chess calibrate` dev subcommand of `chess-cli` only parses its arguments, opens that `Core`, calls the run and prints the report; it holds no match, SPRT or report logic. The nightly CI job (F-3.9) starts the subcommand. The run is not a protocol command. It reports measured Elo and SPRT results against the class baselines, on the baseline machine: M4, 16 GB, macOS Tahoe (Q47). Because ratings are now dynamic (Q41), calibration is used to set the generator bounds before release and as a regression check. It never rewrites a released Player's parameters (R-2). Strength depends on `Threads`, `Hash`, and hardware, so computer-Player instances use fixed `Threads`/`Hash` values (placeholders in §4.7, set before the first calibration run). Those values are recorded with each calibration run. Changing them means recalibrating.
- **Whose max-think time applies (UI-1, user 2026-10-26).** It is always the **active profile's** setting, meaning the Player chosen in the profile chooser for that front-end session. In h–c it caps the computer's moves. In c–c it caps both computers' moves for the watching profile. In h–h there are no engine moves, so it caps only the engine analysis that profile requests: post-game analysis, deeper lines, and what-if. It can only shorten a limit, never lengthen it. The session passes the active profile to `EngineGateway` with each request.

### 4.7 Engine process management

| Use | Engine instances | Priority |
|---|---|---|
| Computer side(s) of a live game | One per computer side. Self-play c–c (same Player on both sides) gets two instances with identical options, so neither side shares the other's hash table. Placeholder `Threads` 1, `Hash` 16 MB (`ESTIMATE`) | Interactive lane |
| Training live analysis / what-if | One shared analysis instance per process, `MultiPV 3`. Placeholder `Threads` 2, `Hash` 64 MB (`ESTIMATE`) | Interactive lane |
| Post-game analysis + background per-move evals | The same analysis instance; the background worker yields to interactive requests | Background lane |

*Amended 2026-10-31 (AMD-12).* **Priority is by preemption lanes, not by `nice`.** Interactive requests (computer moves, the draw decision, live analysis, what-if) run in the interactive lane. The background worker runs one ply per request in the background lane: when an interactive request arrives for the shared analysis instance, the worker's current search is stopped (UCI `stop`), the interactive request runs, and the ply is repeated afterwards (the job resumes from its last stored ply, §7.4). The `Threads` and `Hash` values in the table are placeholders to be fixed before the first calibration run, then recorded with each run (§4.6).

Instances start lazily, which keeps CLI startup under 1 s (NFR-1). They are health-checked with `isready` and killed on timeout. **Restart rule (SE interpretation Q-SE-12, confirmed; the "restart once" of F-3.7 is read this way):** an instance found dead while idle is restarted transparently before the next request. A failure **in flight** is reported and not retried by the engine layer: the core emits `EngineUnavailable`, leaves the game in a resumable state (ENG-1), and lets the user retry (`RetryEngine`, the next request, which restarts the engine and asks again) or leave (the game is suspended). Engine failures are: process exit, no `uciok`/`readyok` within the handshake timeout, no `bestmove` within the search deadline (§4.8), or an illegal or unparsable move (§4.5). While the engine is unavailable, the side to move's clock keeps running (D-15, decided by Q53). A computer that can't recover in time can lose on time.

### 4.8 Concurrency and threading model (D-13)

Nothing slow ever runs on a UI thread: the browser main thread, the WKWebView main thread, or the TUI's `select!` loop. Engine searches run in a separate OS process. SQLite calls run on a dedicated store thread. The UI only sends commands and receives events.

| Execution context | Runs | Must not |
|---|---|---|
| tokio multi-thread runtime, one per process | Protocol dispatcher, `GameSession` actors, engine I/O tasks (async stdin/stdout), `AnalysisWorker`, axum (web), Tauri command handlers (desktop), TUI event loop (CLI) | Block. `rusqlite` is synchronous, so no SQLite call runs on a runtime worker |
| Store thread, one per process | Owns the single write connection. Requests arrive over a channel, and every transaction in §7.4 runs here. Read queries (stats, history) use a small pool of read connections through `spawn_blocking`. WAL lets readers run during a write | Hold a transaction across an `await` or an engine call |
| Stockfish child processes | Searches (§4.7) | Be reached by anything but `chess-engine` |
| `GameSession` actor, one tokio task per live game | All state changes of its game, one message at a time (§4.2) | Wait on the engine inline. Results come back as messages |
| GUI main thread (JS) | Rendering, `BoardModel` reducer, local clock countdown | Compute rules. Legal moves arrive in the snapshot, and what-if moves use `LegalMoves` (D-11) |
| TUI loop (`tokio::select!`) | Key, tick, signal, and core-event handling, plus `render` | Wait on a core future inline. Effects are spawned, and results come back as `Msg`s |

**Ordering and durability.** The actor applies a command to a copy of its `Game`, then appends the resulting record to the game's move journal and flushes it with `F_FULLFSYNC` (§7.6). Only after that does it swap in the new state and broadcast the event (principle 5). Writing to SQLite happens afterwards, on the store thread, so a busy or locked database never delays a move. If the journal write fails (disk full or read-only), the old state stays and the command returns `JournalWriteFailed` (§5.5).

**Backpressure.** Event channels are bounded `broadcast` channels. A subscriber that lags (`RecvError::Lagged`) is sent a fresh `GameSnapshot` instead of the missed events, so a slow WebSocket client can never stall a game.

**Timeouts and periods.** Values are `ESTIMATE`s for the baseline machine (M4, 16 GB, macOS Tahoe; Q47, Q53), to be confirmed by the engine integration benchmarks. Tests set them through config. Intel Macs use the same wall-clock values, so they get less search in the same time (R-14).

**Budget allocation is a Should (CR-7.4, FIND-007).** Splitting the NFR-8 100 ms budget across flush, core, transport and render, and setting a memory ceiling for the engine pool, are Should items of the `chess-store` and `GameSession` design. The design proceeds without them; the NFR-8 latency test (Must) still measures the whole path. They are done if the ASM-2 measurement shows the flush is a large share of the budget.

| Item | Value | On expiry |
|---|---|---|
| UCI handshake (`uci`→`uciok`, `isready`→`readyok`) (ENG-4, TBD-13) | 5 s (the accepted EST in ENG-4). Startup is expected to be well under 1 s on the baseline | Kill and restart once, then `EngineUnavailable` |
| Search deadline (ENG-4, TBD-13) | at the search's own limit + 500 ms, send `stop` | No `bestmove` by the limit + 2 s (the accepted EST in ENG-4) → kill, engine failure |
| `CancelEngine` → move played (ENG-5, TBD-12) | ≤ 200 ms (the accepted EST). Design target 100 ms (`stop` → `bestmove`) | Treated as a search-deadline failure |
| Computer move, top class (ENG-3) | ≤ 3 s by default, capped by the clock budget and the max-think preference | — |
| Computer draw decision (`ComputerDrawDecision`, RULE-2) | within the same ENG-3 maximum think time as a move. All clocks are stopped while it runs | Engine failure drops the offer (ENG-4). A suspend drops it |
| Web server stop (SIGINT or SIGTERM, PLAT-4) | ≤ 5 s `ESTIMATE` to stop accepting, notify pages, suspend open games (journal flush each), then exit 0 | Exit anyway: every acknowledged move is already durable, so nothing is lost |
| Training live analysis (ANA-1) | MultiPV 3, streamed, refined until the user leaves, capped at 10 s per position | Shows the deepest result so far |
| Background per-move evaluation (DAT-3) | 200 ms per ply at low priority (about 30 s for an 80-move game) | Job resumes from the last ply (§7.4) |
| On-demand deeper line / what-if (ANA-2/3) | 2 s per request | Shows the best line so far |
| Journal flush (`F_FULLFSYNC`) per record | a few ms per move. Measured in the NFR-8 latency test | `JournalWriteFailed` |
| Journal clock checkpoint (RULE-5, Q54, TBD-7) | every 1 s while a clock runs, plus on every move, pause, and suspend | After a crash, the clock is restored to the last checkpoint: at most 1 s is lost |
| Journal → SQLite apply | after every journal record, retried with backoff (0.1 s → 5 s) while the DB is busy or locked | Play continues. The backlog is shown in the status line if it exceeds 30 s |
| SQLite `busy_timeout` | 5 s (§7.1) | `StoreBusy` for non-game writes. Game writes are retried from the journal |
| Game lock (journal `flock`) and lease row heartbeat (DAT-4, TBD-28) | the lock is released by the OS when the owner exits or crashes. Heartbeat every 2 s, used only for display | Another process can take over immediately after a crash (§7.3) |
| `PROCESS_HEARTBEAT` refresh (AMD-13, XC-16) | every 10 s (`ESTIMATE`). Unit tests inject the clock. The staleness threshold is a store-design value, not set here | A row not refreshed past the threshold is treated as a dead process |
| Analysis-job claim (`ANALYSIS_JOB.heartbeat_at`) | claimed with a 10 s lease (`ESTIMATE`, the same value as the process heartbeat) | Another process can claim the job and resume it from the last ply |
| Lease heartbeat, display only | 2 s (the game lock row above) | `LeaseLost` is raised only when the journal lock is held by another pid or the journal file was replaced; the registry acquires the lock and the session holds it |
| Web server limits (UR-44, AMD-13; values from the server design, all `ESTIMATE`) | at most 8 connections (a ninth gets `503`); the first frame must be `hello` within 10 s; one message at most 32 MiB, because file contents travel in frames (§5.2); an outbound queue of 64 frames per connection. Ping, pong and write time-outs are set in the server design | Close the socket with a protocol code; a slow reader gets a fresh `GameSnapshot` (backpressure above) |
| Import size limits (AMD-6, decision 9) | PGN file: 10 MB (TBD-25). Archive file through a frame: about 24 MiB (`ESTIMATE`, from the 32 MiB message cap and the 4/3 base64 growth `[INF]`; proposed TBD-35). A CLI import by path has no frame limit | `ValidationFailed{field: file, reason: too_large}` naming the limit, the file size and, for an archive, the CLI alternative. The store is unchanged (DAT-2) |
| Client command timeout (GUI) | 10 s | Show "core not responding", resubscribe (§5.6) |
| Last front-end detaches (tab closed, WebSocket dropped; D-14) | immediately, no grace | `Suspend` is journalled and the clocks freeze at the recorded value. When a client resubscribes, the game resumes |

---

## 5. Protocol (front-end ↔ core)

One set of Rust types in `chess-app::protocol`, `serde`-serialized, with TypeScript types exported by `ts-rs` into `ui/src/protocol/`. A CI check fails if the generated files are stale. *Addendum F (OQ-26, decided by Brian, G2):* no generated type changes. `NewGame`, the input of the store's `start_game`, now carries `started_at: i64`; it is a `chess-store` Rust type, is not part of `chess-app::protocol`, has no `ts-rs` derive and never reaches a front-end. No front-end supplies the start stamp.

### 5.1 Shape

| Kind | Examples |
|---|---|
| **Command** (changes state, returns `Result`) | `RegisterPlayer`, `RenamePlayer`, `ArchivePlayer`, `UpdatePreferences`, `GenerateComputerPlayers{category, count}`, `StartGame{game_uuid, GameSetup}`, `ResumeGame`, `DetachGame`, `MakeMove{game_id, ply, mv}`, `Takeback{game_id, ply}` (`ply` is a stale-command guard, §5.6), `OfferDraw`, `RespondDraw{accept}`, `Resign{side, confirmed}` (h–h: the side chosen in the prompt), `Pause`, `Resume`, `Step`, `SetMoveDelay`, `Abort{confirmed}` (c–c watcher only), `CancelEngine` ("move now", ENG-5), `RetryEngine`, `VoidUnreadableGame{game_id}` (offered when a journal and the store are both unreadable, DAT-7; the offer dialog is the confirmation, so no extra step is added to UI-12), `ImportPgn{file, on_duplicate}`, `ImportFen`, `PreviewDataImport{file}` → conflicts, `ApplyDataImport{plan, confirmed}`, `CreateFreshStore`, `RestoreBackup{confirmed}`, `SaveVariation`, `DeleteVariation` (both carry `game_id`; payloads are defined in the app design), `StopAnalysis{game_id}`, `SelectProfile{player_id}`, `ArchivePlayer{confirmed}`, `ResetPlayerData{player_id, confirmed, void_game_ids}` (humans only. Unconfirmed, it returns `ConfirmationRequired` listing the unfinished games that will be voided, and the confirmed command echoes that list back as `void_game_ids`, §7.2) |
| **Query** (no change to the record) | `ListUnfinishedGames{player_id}` (the resume list, §4.4), `ListPlayers{kind, include_archived}`, `GetPreferences{player_id}`, `GetPlayerStats{player_id}`, `GetRatingHistory`, `ListGames{filter}`, `GetGame`, `GetGameAnalysis{game_id}` (stored `MOVE_EVAL` rows, job state, and `evaluated` and `total` ply counts so a client can show "n of N plies evaluated"), `ExportPgn`, `ExportData`, `LegalMoves{fen}`, `ParseMove{game_id, ply, text}` (typed move text, read-only; reply shapes in the bullet "Typed-move reply shapes" below), `Analyze{purpose, fen, multipv}` (policy-checked; may set `analysis_used`) |
| **Event** (pushed, per game subscription) | `GameSnapshot` (full state on subscribe), `MoveApplied{ply, san, uci, fen, clocks, allowed_actions}`, `EngineThinking{side}`, `ClockSync`, `TakebackApplied{to_ply}`, `DrawOffered`, `DrawDeclined`, `PauseChanged{paused}`, `GameEnded{result, reason, elo_changes}`, `GameVoided{game_id, reason, reset_player_id?}` (the game was voided by a reset of one of its human Players, D-30, or by the user after an unreadable journal, `reason = unreadable`, DAT-7; the client leaves the game screen, shows why, and sends no more commands for it), `RatingsUpdated`, `GameSuspended`, `StoreBacklog{records}`, `AnalysisProgress`, `EngineUnavailable`, `EngineRecovered`, `LeaseLost`, `ServerStopping` (the web server is stopping; the page shows "server stopped" and does not retry, D-36) |

`GameSnapshot` carries everything a client needs to render and rewind without asking the server again: setup, players, every ply (SAN, UCI, FEN, timestamp, clock after the move), the current ply's legal moves (for highlighting and keyboard input), clocks, state, and `allowed_actions`.

**Amendment 7 additions (AMD-6, decision 10 accepted as a package; the app design governs the exact shapes).** Evidence `[INF]` unless tagged.
- **Game-scoped addressing.** Every game command and query carries `game_id`. Events carry `game_id` and a per-game `seq`.
- **Typed moves: `Query::ParseMove{game_id, ply, text} -> ParsedMove{uci, san} | IllegalMove{reason} | ParseError{kind: move, ...} | StalePosition{live_ply}` (D-45; shapes in the next bullet, D-51, D-52).** `chess-rules` is the only parser. The query parses against the position at `ply` of the live or viewed game, and returns `StalePosition{live_ply}` when the game is live and `ply` is not the live ply. The CLI calls it in process, and the GUI over the WebSocket or Tauri IPC. Typed text is **not** accepted in `MakeMove.mv`: that would mix parse and execute and weaken the UCI-only idempotency of `MakeMove` (§5.6). No separate latency requirement exists for typed input; NFR-8 (100 ms feedback to a key press, `ESTIMATE`) is the budget, and the local echo of a typed character never waits for the query. The query is sent on Enter or after a debounce, not per keystroke. FF-6 (§9) measures the loopback round trip.
- **Typed-move reply shapes (Addendum A, D-51, D-52).** Success: `ParsedMove{uci, san}` on the wire, where `uci` is the lowercase long-algebraic text of the move (a promotion letter in lower case) and `san` is the canonical SAN for that position. The Rust type of `chess-rules` that carries the matched `Move` is renamed `MatchedMove{mv, uci, san}`, so the crate no longer has two types called `ParsedMove`. `mv` stays inside `chess-rules` and `chess-app`; it never crosses the protocol, because `chess-rules` has no `serde` and a `Move` has no wire form. A client that wants to play the move sends `MakeMove{mv: uci}`. Failure: the text was understood but names no legal move (rules `NoMatch`, or an illegal move) gives `IllegalMove{reason}`. Text that could not be read as a move gives `ParseError{kind: move, reason, ...}`. **`ParseError` is one code with a `kind` tag:** `Pgn` and `Fen` keep `{game_index, line, col, reason}` (§5.5), and `move` carries `reason` and the extra fields of that reason only, in `details`:

  | `reason` | Rules variant | Extra fields |
  |---|---|---|
  | `empty` | `Empty` | none |
  | `too_long` | `TooLong` (over 16 bytes) | none |
  | `syntax` | `Syntax` (including a non-ASCII byte) | none |
  | `lowercase_piece` | `LowercasePiece` | `suggestion`: the text from `chess-rules`, passed through unchanged |
  | `promotion_required` | `PromotionRequired` | `choices`: the list of strings from `chess-rules`, passed through unchanged |
  | `ambiguous` | `Ambiguous` | `candidates`: the canonical SAN of every matching legal move, in the order `chess-rules` returns them |

  The `message` is an English sentence built from `reason` (NFR-9). The mapping is made once, in `chess-app`, so the CLI and the GUI show the same text for the same input. The TypeScript client narrows on `code` and `kind`.
- **Sessions and subscriptions.** A `FrontEndSession` is one attached front-end (a CLI run, one WebSocket connection, the Tauri window). It carries the active Player and a trust level (in-process or remote). `subscribe(game)` gives the per-game stream. **`subscribe_global()`** gives the process-wide stream: `ServerStopping`, `StoreBacklog`, `AnalysisProgress` of stored games, `RatingsUpdated`. These four events are therefore not part of a game subscription.
- **Envelope kinds.** `hello` (first frame, carries `protocol_version`), `command`, `query`, `subscribe`, `unsubscribe`, `subscribe_global`, `result`, `error`, `event`. A frame that does not decode (bad JSON, unknown kind or field, or any frame before `hello`) gets the error `MalformedFrame{reason}` and the transport closes (D-54, §5.5). **Which frames carry an `id`, and what an event frame holds, is fixed in §5.6 (D-53).**
- **Export reply and write failure (Addendum A, D-55).** The reply to `ExportPgn` and `ExportData` is `ExportedFile{name, media_type, content}` where `content` is either `Bytes{base64}` or `Saved{path}`. The core returns `Bytes` to a remote or Tauri session, and a browser saves it as a Blob. The Tauri shell writes the bytes to the Downloads folder and **rewrites `content` to `Saved{path}` before the reply reaches the UI**, so the UI has one reply type for both transports and shows the path. An in-process CLI export that names an output file gets `Saved{path}` from the core. A write that fails gives `ExportWriteFailed{cause}` (§5.5).
- **Analysis.** `Analyze` stays a query. `StopAnalysis{game_id}` ends a live analysis stream (ANA-1 "unless the user stops it"); the analysis also stops on a new request for that game, a move or takeback, the game leaving `InProgress`, and the last subscriber detaching. An analysis board for an imported FEN has no game; its purpose name (`FreeAnalysis`) is fixed in the app design.
- **Recovery mode.** If the store cannot open because it is missing or corrupt (`StoreMissing`, `StoreCorrupt`), `Core::open` returns a `Core` in recovery mode that accepts only the recovery commands (`CreateFreshStore`, `RestoreBackup`, `ApplyDataImport`), so that these commands have a core to run in. `SchemaTooNew`, `BackupFailed`, `DurabilityUnsupported` (Amd 8, D-71, approved 2026-11-04) and a version refusal return no `Core`: the front-end shows the message and exits, or "retry" means calling `open` again.
- **Variations.** `SaveVariation` is refused from the **final position** of a game, because a variation needs a move to branch from and PGN has no place for a branch after the last move (user decision 13, which limits the "optionally saves the branch" of ANA-2 to branches that start before the last ply; the requirement wording is the systems engineer's to adjust). The reply is `ValidationFailed{field: from_ply, reason: no_move_after_position}` (§5.5). A what-if branch can still be explored and discarded.


### 5.2 Transports

| Front-end | Transport | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| CLI | In-process: `core.execute(cmd).await`, `core.subscribe(game_id)` | No serialization. |
| Web | One WebSocket at `ws://127.0.0.1:<port>/api`, JSON frames `{id?, kind, body}`; `id` is on requests and their replies only (§5.6) | Bound to loopback only. Rejects any `Host`/`Origin` other than `127.0.0.1:<port>` / `localhost:<port>` (blocks DNS rebinding and drive-by pages). `Host` must match on every request. `Origin`, when present, must match. A request without `Origin` is allowed (browsers send none on a same-origin page load), but a WebSocket upgrade without `Origin` is rejected; a test client must set `Origin`. Both loopback spellings count as the server's own origin (SE interpretation Q-SE-13, confirmed). |
| Desktop | Tauri `invoke("core", envelope)` + `listen("core-event")` | Same JSON envelope as the web, so the TS client differs only in its send/receive functions. No TCP port opened. |

In the UI, one `CoreClient` interface hides the difference:

```ts
interface CoreClient {
  execute<C extends Command>(cmd: C): Promise<ResultOf<C>>;
  query<Q extends Query>(q: Q): Promise<ResultOf<Q>>;
  subscribe(gameId: GameId, onEvent: (e: Event) => void): Unsubscribe;
  subscribeGlobal(onEvent: (e: Event) => void): Unsubscribe;   // process-wide events, §5.1
}
// ui/src/transport/ws.ts    → WebSocketCoreClient
// ui/src/transport/tauri.ts → TauriCoreClient   (chosen at startup: window.__TAURI__ ?)
```

**File transfer (decision 9, XC-20, UR-35; AMD-6).** The protocol carries file contents inside frames as base64 bytes (the message cap is 32 MiB, §4.8). No HTTP upload or download route is added, so the server keeps one kind of endpoint and one set of Host/Origin checks, and no native dialog plugin is added.
- **Browser.** An import is read with a file input and sent in the frame. An export is received in the frame and saved as a Blob download.
- **Desktop.** An import uses the same file input in the webview. The shell writes an export to a fixed folder, the user's Downloads folder, and shows the path. It reports the path by rewriting the core's `ExportedFile` reply to `Saved{path}` (D-55); it does not open a dialog. A write failure returns `ExportWriteFailed{cause}`.
- **CLI.** A path (`FileRef::Path`), honoured only for in-process sessions. A remote (WebSocket) session may send bytes only, because a path chosen by a browser client would be arbitrary file access. Files are written to a temporary file and renamed.
- **Limits.** The PGN file limit stays 10 MB (TBD-25, `ESTIMATE`). The archive file limit is an `ESTIMATE` of about 24 MiB through a frame, derived from the 32 MiB cap and the 4/3 growth of base64 `[INF]` (proposed TBD-35, to be registered by the systems engineer; this restricts DAT-2 and IF-5 for the GUI path only). A file over its limit is rejected with `ValidationFailed{field: file, reason: too_large}` whose message names the limit, the file size and, for an archive, the CLI alternative. The store is unchanged. An import by path from the CLI has no frame limit.

### 5.3 Example: human move then computer reply (Strict, timed)

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    actor H as Human (GUI)
    participant B as Board (chessground + BoardModel)
    participant UI as TS GUI (screen + client state)
    participant T as Transport (WS or Tauri)
    participant S as GameSession actor
    participant P as Policy + Domain (pure)
    participant J as Move journal (fsync)
    participant DB as Store (SQLite)
    participant G as EngineGateway
    participant SF as Stockfish

    H->>B: drag or click e2→e4 / keyboard on overlay / types "e4"
    B->>UI: onMove(e2, e4)
    UI->>T: MakeMove{game, ply: 12, mv: "e2e4"}
    T->>S: command
    S->>P: check(MakeMove, ply) + apply(move, now)
    alt legal and ply current
        P-->>S: new Game state, clocks updated (+increment)
        S->>J: append MoveRecord{seq, ply, uci, clocks} + F_FULLFSYNC
        J-->>S: durable
        S-)DB: apply record (async, retried while busy#59; §7.6)
        S-->>T: Event MoveApplied{ply 13, san "e4", fen, clocks, allowed_actions}
        T-->>UI: event → re-render
        UI-->>B: BoardProps{fen, lastMove, interactive: false}
    else IllegalMove or StalePosition
        S-->>T: error {code, message}
        T-->>UI: show message
        UI-->>B: BoardProps{fen unchanged} → chessground reverts the piece
        Note over B,S: No engine request follows a rejected move
    end
    opt move was accepted and Black is a computer
        S-->>T: Event EngineThinking{black}
        S->>G: request(ComputerMove, fen, limits from personality ∧ clock ∧ max-think)
        G->>P: check(purpose, game context) ✓
        G->>SF: position fen … / go wtime … movetime …
        Note over UI,S: Actor stays responsive: Resign, flag fall,<br/>or pause can arrive and cancel the search
        SF-->>G: bestmove e7e5 (or a seeded "error" pick from MultiPV)
        G->>P: legal? (NFR-3, §4.5)
        G-->>S: engine result message
        S->>P: apply(move, now)
        S->>J: append MoveRecord + F_FULLFSYNC
        S-)DB: apply record (async)
        S-->>T: Event MoveApplied{ply 14, ...}
        T-->>UI: event → re-render
        UI-->>B: BoardProps{fen, lastMove, interactive: true} → animated move
    end
```

### 5.4 Example: game end, rating, and background analysis

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    participant S as GameSession actor
    participant P as Domain (pure)
    participant J as Move journal
    participant DB as Store
    participant W as AnalysisWorker
    participant G as EngineGateway
    participant A as AnalysisService
    participant UI as Front-end

    S->>P: apply(move) → Terminal(Checkmate, 1-0)
    S->>J: append GameEnd record + F_FULLFSYNC (the result is now durable)
    S->>DB: finalize(game) on the store thread
    Note over DB,P: One BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction: re-read both Players' current Elo<br/>(a computer may have finished another game in another process),<br/>EloCalc per side on the game's track (each Player's own K on that track: 40 for the first 30 rated games, then 32),<br/>write result + reason + times + rating_history for both sides, enqueue analysis_job
    DB->>P: EloCalc(current ratings, K, rated flag)
    P-->>DB: deltas on the game's track (Training or Strict), or none (self-play / custom FEN)
    DB-->>S: committed, elo_changes
    S-->>UI: GameEnded{result, reason, elo_changes, offer_analysis: true}
    Note over S,UI: If the DB is busy, GameEnded is sent with elo_changes pending,<br/>and RatingsUpdated follows once the journal record is applied
    Note over W,G: Per-move evaluation runs only after the game ends,<br/>never during play (DAT-3, ANA-1)
    W->>DB: claim job (lease)
    loop each ply, low priority
        W->>G: request(PostGameAnalysis, fen)
        G-->>W: eval, best line
        W->>DB: upsert move_eval (cp, mate, best, cp_loss, flag)
    end
    W->>DB: mark job done (accuracy is derived from MOVE_EVAL by query)
    UI->>A: (optional) open post-game analysis → GetGameAnalysis reads stored evals,<br/>Analyze / LegalMoves for deeper lines and what-if on demand
    A->>G: request(PostGameAnalysis or WhatIf, fen) — allowed, game is finished
```

### 5.5 Error catalogue

Every `Result` error crosses the protocol as `{code, message, details}`. `code` is a stable string from this table. Front-ends branch on `code` and show `message`, and new codes may be added without breaking clients (§5.6). Rust crates use `thiserror` and are mapped to these codes in `chess-app`. No internal error text (paths, SQL) reaches `message`. Internal detail goes to the log.

| Code | Raised by | Meaning | Client behaviour | Retry? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ConfirmationRequired{action, voids}` | services | A destructive command (resign, c–c abort, archive, reset player data, apply an import after its preview, restore a backup) was sent without `confirmed: true` (Q48, Q52, UI-12). The core enforces this, so no front-end can skip the prompt. The seventh UI-12 action, quitting the TUI with Ctrl-C, changes no data and is confirmed by the TUI's own dialog, which is the same dialog widget as the others. For a reset, `voids` lists the Player's unfinished games (opponent, mode, moves played) that the reset will void (D-30). It is also returned when, at commit time, the Player has an unfinished game that is not in the confirmed reset's `void_game_ids`, so a game the user never saw listed is never voided. A listed game that ended normally in the meantime is simply not voided | Show the confirmation dialog. For a reset, the dialog says that each listed game will be voided: no result, and it counts for neither player | After confirming |
| `UnfinishedGameExists{game_id, mode, player_id}` | `GameSetupService` | A slot-holding Player in the new game (a human, or a computer in a c–c game) already has an unfinished game in that mode (GAME-4, D-27). Since 2026-10-27 it is **not** a reset outcome: a reset voids unfinished games instead (D-30) | Offer Resume, or Resign (human games) or Abort (c–c), each confirmed | After resume, resign, or abort |
| `JournalWriteFailed{cause}` | `GameSession` | The move journal can't be written (disk full, read-only) | The move isn't made. Show the cause. After a single failed append whose cleanup succeeded (D-82) the clock keeps running and the player may try again. *Addendum E, JR5-003 (text only):* if the same code comes back for the next command, or the code follows a step in which an earlier record was written (D-65 part 2), the session is in the degraded state of D-80 (clocks hold, only queries, `Detach` and `Shutdown` are accepted): the front-end says that this game cannot be saved from this session and how to leave it (`Detach`, then resume), and does not say that the clock is running. No new field or code is used to tell the two apart (a field would be G2 and is not proposed) | User-initiated |
| `StoreMissing` | `chess-store` on open | The data dir shows an earlier store existed (marker or backups), but `chess.db` is gone (Q51) | Choose: start fresh, or import from a file | — |
| `ImportConflicts{list}` | `ImportExportService` | Preview found Players or games that already exist (Q51) | Full-data import: for each one, choose Replace, or Keep both as distinct identities. **PGN import:** a duplicate game (identical tags and moves to a stored game or an earlier game in the same file) offers only **Skip this game (default)** and **Keep both**, plus "apply to all remaining collisions". Replace is not offered (D-34) | After choosing |
| `IllegalMove{reason}` | `GameSession` / `chess-rules` | Move not legal in the current position. *Addendum A, D-51:* also the reply of `Query::ParseMove` to text that is understood but names no legal move (rules `NoMatch`) | Show reason, keep input editable (CLI-3), revert the piece (GUI) | No |
| `StalePosition{live_ply}` | `GameSession` | `ply` ≠ live ply (rewound, or a move landed meanwhile) | Show the live-position badge, refresh from the snapshot | After refresh |
| `Denied{reason}` | `Policy` (via services and gateway) | Action not allowed in this mode, matchup, or state. Reasons include the draw-offer set (§4.4), `state_not_allowed` (a domain reason for an action the current state does not allow; *Addendum A, D-56:* also `SelectProfile` while a game is open in the session, with `details.game_ids`), `computer_player` (rename, archive or reset of a computer Player), `active_player` (archiving the Player that this front-end session has selected), `archived_player` (an archived Player chosen as a side in a new game, §4.4; additive, Amd 7), and `voided` | Should not happen for a correct client (controls come from `allowed_actions`). Show reason | No |
| `ValidationFailed{field, reason}` | `PlayerService`, `GameSetupService`, preferences | Bad name, bad setup or setting. A name is trimmed of leading and trailing whitespace, then NFC-normalised (no other folding). The rule that failed is named in `reason` and in the message: `empty`, `too_long` (over 128 user-perceived characters, i.e. extended grapheme clusters, UAX #29), `character_not_allowed` (control characters, Unicode category Cc, and line or paragraph separators, Zl and Zp), or `already_used`. `already_used` is **case-sensitive against every Player**, human or computer, active or archived, and the message says "by an archived Player" when so (PLY-6, CR-4.1). *Amd 7 (additive):* an import file over its size limit gives `field: file`, `reason: too_large`, and the message names the limit, the file size and, for an archive, the CLI alternative (§4.8). A `SaveVariation` from the final position of the game gives `field: from_ply`, `reason: no_move_after_position`, because a variation needs a move to branch from (decision 13) | Highlight the field and show the rule | After fixing input |
| `ParseError{kind: Pgn\|Fen, game_index, line, col, reason}` | `ImportExportService` | Malformed PGN/FEN (RULE-7, RULE-8, IF-2, IF-3) | List errors per game. The other games in the file still import | No |
| `ParseError{kind: move, reason, ...}` | `AnalysisService` (`Query::ParseMove`) | *Addendum A, D-51.* Typed move text that cannot be read as a move. `reason` is `empty`, `too_long`, `syntax`, `lowercase_piece` (with `suggestion`), `promotion_required` (with `choices`) or `ambiguous` (with `candidates`, the canonical SAN of each matching move) (§5.1) | Keep the input editable and show the message (CLI-3). For `ambiguous`, list the candidates | No |
| `MalformedFrame{reason}` | transport adapters (WebSocket, Tauri), using the shared envelope helpers | *Addendum A, D-54.* A frame did not decode. `reason` is `bad_json`, `unknown_kind`, `unknown_field` or `before_hello`. A correct client never sends one. The transport then closes. The WebSocket close code is the server's choice (server design ASM-SRV-1), not fixed here | Show "protocol error" and offer a reload | After reload |
| `ExportWriteFailed{cause}` | Tauri shell (Downloads folder), `ImportExportService` (a CLI `--output` or `FileRef::Path`) | *Addendum A, D-55.* An export file could not be written. `cause` is `folder_missing`, `permission_denied`, `disk_full` or `io_error`. No partial file is left at the final name, and the game and the store are unchanged. The message names the cause and the next step (free space, check the folder permission, try again) (NFR-9) | Show the message | User-initiated |
| `NotFound{entity, id}` | services | Unknown Player, game, or variation | Refresh the list | No |
| `GameLeased{owner_kind}` | `SessionRegistry` | The game is live in another process ("open in the CLI") | Show the owner, offer read-only view | When the owner releases it or its lease goes stale |
| `LeaseLost` (event) | `GameSession` | This process lost the lease (another process took it over after a stall) | Drop to read-only, explain | No |
| `EngineUnavailable{cause}` | `chess-engine` / gateway | Binary missing, crash, timeout, illegal output | Offer Retry / Leave (ENG-1). *Amd 7 (additive):* `cause` is one of `spawn_failed` (binary missing), `crashed`, `handshake_timeout`, `search_deadline`, `illegal_move`; the final names are fixed in the app design. A failure in flight is reported, not retried (§4.7) | `RetryEngine` |
| `StoreBusy` | `chess-store` | `busy_timeout` exceeded | Transparent retry once in the core, then show | Yes, once |
| `StoreUnavailable{cause}` | `chess-store` | I/O error, disk full, read-only file. **Every store-changing command** (register, rename, archive, preferences, generate, reset, import, restore) is all-or-nothing and, on any failure, returns this or `StoreBusy` with a message naming the failure and the next step (DAT-7, NFR-9) | For non-game writes: show. A live game keeps playing from the journal, and the backlog is applied later (§7.6) | User-initiated |
| `StoreCorrupt` | `chess-store` on open | `quick_check` failed (§7.1) | Restore-from-backup flow | — |
| `SchemaTooNew{db_version, app_version}` | `chess-store` on open | The database was migrated by a newer build (D-16) | Tell the user to update this entry point. Nothing is written | — |
| `DurabilityUnsupported{path, errno}` | `chess-store` on open | *Amd 8 (D-71, additive; APPROVED by Brian, 2026-11-04).* The data directory is on a volume where `fcntl(F_FULLFSYNC)` fails with `ENOTSUP` or `ENOTTY`, so "no completed move is lost" (NFR-2) cannot be promised there. The store refuses to open it and writes nothing | Show the path and the reason, tell the user to choose another folder (`--data-dir`, `CHESS_DATA_DIR`) and exit (CLI: exit class 3 in the TUI, in plain mode and in subcommands, TBD-36 not narrowed; desktop: an in-window screen, not a native dialog, with its Quit button as OQ-TAU-005 decides). No "continue anyway" (§7.1) | After the user picks another folder |
| `Busy{reason}` | `ImportExportService` | Import or restore needs exclusive access, but another process is live (§7.4) | Ask the user to close the other entry points | Yes |
| `ProtocolMismatch{server, client}` | WebSocket adapter | A browser tab from an older build (D-16). *Amd 7 (superseded by Addendum A, D-54):* a frame before `hello` or one that does not decode used to get this code with `details.reason`. It now gets `MalformedFrame` | Hard reload | After reload |
| `NothingToTakeBack` | `GameSession` | Takeback at the earliest reachable position (§4.4). Message: "Nothing to take back" | Show the message, change nothing | No |
| `PlayerHasUnfinishedGames{game_ids}` | `PlayerService` | `ArchivePlayer` on a Player with an unfinished game (PLY-4, GAME-4). The message names the games | Offer Resume or Resign for each. Reset is the only action that voids games (D-30) | After resume or resign |
| `BackupFailed{cause}` | `chess-store` on upgrade | The pre-upgrade backup could not be written (DAT-6, D-35). Nothing was migrated | Show the cause. Offer Retry or Exit. A newer front-end never opens an old-version store without the upgrade | User-initiated |
| `JournalUnreadable{game_id, recovered_moves}` | `SessionRegistry` at resume | The journal could not be read in full (DAT-7). `recovered_moves` is the length of the longest intact run held by the journal or the store. *Amended 2026-10-31 (AMD-14, UR-36):* this is an **error** only when nothing is readable, and the client then offers `VoidUnreadableGame`. With partial damage the `ResumeGame` reply succeeds and carries `recovered_moves` as a field; the client tells the user the count and play continues from it | Show the count, or offer the void | No |
| `OtherVersionRunning{version, kind}` | `chess-store` / `Core::open` | *Amd 7 (additive, UR-36).* Startup is refused because a live process of another build version is registered in `PROCESS_HEARTBEAT` (PLAT-5, §7.5). The message names the version and the front-end kind and says to quit it first | Show the message and exit, or call `open` again after quitting | After the other process exits |
| `ImportInvariantFailed{check}` | `ImportExportService` | *Amd 7 (additive, UR-36; the name is fixed in the app design).* After an import plan was built, a post-check failed (for example the Elo invariant per Player, §7.4 step 3). Nothing was written and the backup is untouched | Show which check failed | No |
| `ConfigInvalid{file, line, reason}` | `chess-cli` (`serve`) | `config.toml` is unreadable, unparsable, or `server.port` is outside 1 to 65535 (PLAT-4). Never a silent fallback | Print the message, exit 1 | After fixing the file |
| `Internal{id}` | any | Bug. Logged with a correlation id | Generic message with the id | No |

### 5.6 Protocol reliability, idempotency, and versioning

- **Envelope (Addendum A, D-53; replaces "every frame has an `id`").** `{id?, kind, body}`. `id` is a client-chosen request id. It is present on `command`, `query`, `subscribe`, `unsubscribe` and `subscribe_global` frames and is echoed on the `result` or `error` that answers them. `hello` and `event` frames carry **no `id`**, because a pushed frame answers no request. An `error` for a frame that could not be decoded has no `id` unless one could be read from it. The body of an `event` frame is `EventEnvelope{game_id, seq, event}`. For a game subscription, `game_id` is the game and `seq` is that game's gap-free counter (the first item is the `GameSnapshot`). For the global stream (`subscribe_global`), `game_id` is null and `seq` is a per-subscription counter that increases strictly but **may show a gap** after the receiver lagged: global events are notifications (`ServerStopping`, `StoreBacklog`, `AnalysisProgress` of stored games, `RatingsUpdated`), the client re-queries on them, and no snapshot is needed to repair a gap. An event about one stored game, such as `AnalysisProgress`, names the game inside `event`, not in the envelope. The web and desktop transports have a per-request client timeout (§4.8).
- **Idempotency.**
  - `MakeMove`, `Takeback{ply}` (`ply` is only a stale-command guard: the command is rejected with `StalePosition` when it differs from the live ply, and the core derives the target from RULE-3, §4.4; there is no multi-step takeback in one command), `Step`, `OfferDraw`, `RespondDraw`, `CancelEngine`, `VoidUnreadableGame`, and `Resign` are guarded by the game state. A duplicate after a reconnect gets `StalePosition` or `Denied` and changes nothing. A command for a voided game gets `Denied{reason: voided}`.
  - A duplicate confirmed `ResetPlayerData` is harmless: the games it listed are already voided, so it voids nothing, adds a second reset point at the same rating, and moves `reset_at` past no game, which changes no view. Pending requests are never replayed blindly anyway (see Reconnect).
  - `StartGame` carries a client-generated `game_uuid`. A repeat with the same uuid returns the existing game instead of creating a second one. *Addendum F (OQ-26):* the command is unchanged. The start stamp is not in it and no front-end supplies one: the app takes it and passes it to the store in `NewGame.started_at` (D-84, §5.7).
  - Imports are all-or-nothing per game and dedupe on a content hash, so re-importing the same PGN reports "already imported".
  - `RegisterPlayer` relies on unique names.
- **Reconnect (web).** If the WebSocket drops, the client reconnects with backoff (0.5, 1, 2, 4 s, capped), sends `ResumeGame` and then `subscribe` for the game it held, and treats the new `GameSnapshot` as authoritative. A new or resumed game session with no subscriber suspends after a **5 s attach window** (`ESTIMATE`); a detach after attach suspends at once (D-14). Pending requests fail with a "connection lost" message and are never replayed blindly. While disconnected, inputs are disabled and a banner is shown. If the session gets no subscriber back, D-14 decides what the game does.
- **Versioning.** The bundle and the core always come from the same build: the bundle is embedded in `chess` or bundled in `Chess.app`. The handshake sends `protocol_version` (the build version) both ways. A mismatch, such as a stale tab after an upgrade, returns `ProtocolMismatch` and asks for a reload. Within one version, unknown event kinds are ignored by clients, which keeps tests tolerant of additive changes. There is no external API, so no deprecation policy is needed beyond this.

### 5.7 Internal interface contracts

| Interface | Provider → consumer | Style | Contract essentials | Failure behaviour |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Protocol (`Command`/`Query`/`Event`) | `chess-app` → CLI, `chess-server`, `src-tauri`, `ui/` | Async request/response plus a per-game event stream | §5.1–§5.6. TS types generated (D-7). `allowed_actions` in every snapshot | §5.5 codes, §5.6 reconnect |
| `Engine` trait, `EnginePool` | `chess-engine` → `EngineGateway` | Async, cancellable | *Amended 2026-10-31 (AMD-3; shapes from the engine design, `[OBS]` draft SDD).* `search(SearchRequest) -> SearchResult{best, pv, lines, score, depth}`, `stop()`, `health()`. `SearchRequest{position, limits, multipv, new_game, seed, updates}`; `SearchPosition` is `Fen` or `Moves{start, moves}` (so repetition and the fifty-move count are visible to the engine); `updates` is an optional throttled stream of `SearchInfo` (depth, multipv, score, pv) that feeds `AnalysisProgress` and live analysis; `seed` is the error-pick seed (§4.6). `EnginePool` hands out `EngineHandle`s (`computer_side(key, personality)`, `analysis(priority)`). `EnginePersonality` is the engine's own type: the stored `Personality` is converted by `personality_to_engine` in `chess-app` (D-43). Scores come back from the side to move's point of view and `chess-app` normalises them to White's point of view (§7.2). Only the gateway holds a handle | Typed `EngineError` → §4.7 |
| `Store` handle and repositories | `chess-store` → `chess-app` services | Calls to the store thread over a channel (D-13). *Amended 2026-10-31 (AMD-2, D-39):* `Store` is one cheap clonable **concrete handle**; there is no `dyn Store` and no fake store. `StoreApi` in the store design is a documentation contract, not a seam | One method per transaction in §7.4. The API takes and returns `GameId`/`PlayerId` (UUID); integer row ids never cross it (D-40). No SQL outside `chess-store`. Statistics queries return rows filtered by structural keys only (D-44). *Addendum A:* `roster_indexes() -> Vec<u32>` replaces `next_roster_index` (ruling 3, D-46); `insert_computers` carries each Player's `roster_index`; `abort_game`, `void_unreadable_game` and `reset_player(..., voids: Vec<VoidSpec{game_id, content_hash}>)` take the `content_hash` computed by the caller, because the store has no `chess-rules` (ruling 1, D-42). *Addendum F (OQ-26, D-84; decided by Brian, G2, 2026-11-04):* `start_game(NewGame)`, where `NewGame` carries `started_at: i64`, the app's one start stamp, written unchanged to `GAME.started_at`; `NewGame` is a store input type, not a protocol type | `StoreBusy` / `StoreUnavailable` / `SchemaTooNew` |
| `MoveJournal`, `JournalWriter` (Amd 7, AMD-3) | `chess-store` → `GameSession`, `SessionRegistry`, `PlayerService` (void of a suspended game) | Concrete types, not a trait. Shapes from the store design (`[OBS]` draft SDD 3.7.3) | `MoveJournal::create(game_uuid)` creates `journal/<uuid>.jnl`, takes the exclusive `flock`, writes the header and syncs file and directory (`Locked{owner}` if held). `open_for_resume(store, game_uuid)` takes the lock, scans, reconciles with the store (§7.6) and returns the writer and a `Recovered{records, moves, clocks, report}`. `probe(game_uuid)` reports whether the lock is held, without keeping it. `JournalWriter::append(record)` returns only after `F_FULLFSYNC`, runs its blocking I/O on the blocking pool as one task that completes even if the future is dropped, and cleans up after a failure by truncating to the last acknowledged length or poisoning the writer (*Addendum E, D-82; replaces "is cancel-safe"; the `JournalError` list is unchanged, `Poisoned` is a cause of `WriteFailed`*). `close_final()` moves the file to `journal/done/` and releases the lock | `JournalError`: `Locked`, `WriteFailed`, `Unreadable`, `VersionMismatch`, `ForeignStore` → `GameLeased`, `JournalWriteFailed`, `JournalUnreadable` (§5.5) |
| `JournalCodec` (Amd 7, D-41) | `chess-app` (pure), between `chess-domain` `Fact` and `chess-store` `JournalRecord` | Pure functions | `fact_to_record(&Fact, &RecordCtx) -> JournalRecord`, `record_to_fact(&JournalRecord) -> Fact`, *(Amd 8 Addendum D, D-79:)* `record_to_replay_item(&JournalRecord) -> ReplayItem`, which is `{fact: record_to_fact(r), check}` with `check = Some(MoveCheck{san, fen_after})` from the record's payload for a `Move` and `None` for every other kind (`ReplayItem` and `MoveCheck` are `chess-domain` types and the input of `Game::replay`), the `Start` assembler (adds `rated` and `self_play`), and the `GameEnd`, `Abort` and `Voided` assemblers that add the finalize inputs, `content_hash` included (Addendum A, rulings 1 and 2). One `Fact` maps to exactly one record kind, and the exact payload fields of each are the table in §7.6. `fen_after` and `wall_ms` come from the post-state and stamp that `Game::decide` returns, so the domain `Fact::Move` does not change | Not applicable. Conformance: a property test over every `Fact` variant, `record_to_fact(fact_to_record(f)) == f`, for a `Move` the `check` of `record_to_replay_item` equals the `san` and `fen_after` that were put in the `RecordCtx` (D-79), and, for the three finalize records, `decode_finalize(assemble_*(f, inputs)) == inputs` (FF-3) |
| Calibration run (Addendum A, D-57) | `chess-app` (`calibration`) → `chess-cli` (`chess calibrate`) | In-process async call | `Core::open_for_calibration(cfg)` gives the `Core` (it refuses the default data directory). The run function takes the `Core` and a `CalibrationPlan` and returns a `CalibrationReport`. It plays the matches through `EngineGateway` with `EnginePurpose::Calibration`, runs the SPRT, and builds the report. The CLI parses arguments, calls it and prints. Not a protocol command. The plan and report shapes belong to the app design | A typed error from the run; the user's store is never touched |
| Test support (Addendum A, FIND-JR-006) | `chess-app`, Cargo feature `test-support` → the tests of `chess-app`, `chess-cli`, `chess-server`, `src-tauri` | Rust items | `Core::open(cfg, Seams)`, the test `EngineFactory` over `FakeEngine` (the production factory is written in `chess-app`, D-61) and a temp-directory-store helper, so a front-end crate builds a `Core` without an edge to `chess-engine` or `chess-store`. Not compiled into a release build | Not applicable |
| `TimeSource`, `Rng` | traits in `chess-domain`; implementations in `chess-app` → `chess-domain`, `GameSession` | Sync, injected | Monotonic time for clocks, wall time only for timestamps. `Rng` seeded by the roster seed or by `error_pick_seed(game_id, ply)`, which `chess-app` passes to the engine as `SearchRequest.seed` (§4.6) | Not applicable |
| PGN/FEN I/O | `chess-rules` (wrapping `pgn-reader`/`shakmaty`) → `ImportExportService`, `GameSetupService`, `AnalysisService` | Pure functions. *Amd 7 (AMD-3, D-45):* `Fen::as_str`/`Display`; `Position::parse_move(text, Typed) -> MatchedMove{mv, uci, san}` is the only typed-move parser (the wire type is `ParsedMove{uci, san}`, D-52); a `UciMove` becomes a `Move` with `Move::from_uci`. *Amd 8 Addendum A (D-75):* `eco::lookup(&PositionKey) -> Option<EcoEntry{code, name}>` is a provided interface of this crate, used by `EcoTable` in `chess-domain` (the table is generated at build time from `third_party/eco/`) | **Parse:** FEN must be a legal position, or it's rejected with a reason. PGN is streamed game by game, and every SAN is validated by replay. **Export (IF-1), checked against the PGN Standard on 2026-10-30 (TBC-6 and TBD-24 closed):** the Seven Tag Roster first, in the standard order (`Event`, `Site`, `Date`, `Round`, `White`, `Black`, `Result`; unknown values `?`, `Round "-"`, `Date` as `YYYY.MM.DD`), then every other tag in **ASCII order of the tag name** (`BlackClass`, `BlackUUID`, `EndStatus`, `FEN`, `GameUUID`, `Mode`, `Rated`, `SetUp`, `Termination`, `TimeControl`, `WhiteClass`, `WhiteUUID`; the UUID tags only in the archive). `SetUp "1"` and `FEN` for a custom start, `TimeControl` (`seconds` or `seconds+increment`) when timed. One tag pair per line, one space between name and value, a blank line after the tags, `\"` and `\\` escaped in values. Movetext in canonical SAN with `1.` and `1...` numbers, a single space between tokens, lines under 80 characters, and a final token equal to `Result`. `Termination` uses the standard's values: `normal` for checkmate, stalemate, resignation and draws; `time forfeit` for a loss on the clock; `abandoned` for voided and aborted games; none for an unfinished game. The writer is one function in `chess-rules` so every front-end gets the same bytes. The custom tags are `Mode`, `Rated`, `WhiteClass`, `BlackClass`, `EndStatus`. `Mode` also names the rating scale. **Games that ended without a result** (watcher-aborted c–c, and voided by a reset, D-30) are exported the same way: `[Result "*"]` with the movetext ending in `*`, `[Termination "abandoned"]` (a standard PGN termination value, so other tools read it as a game that stopped without a result rather than one still in progress), and the custom tag `[EndStatus "aborted"]` or `[EndStatus "voided"]`, which tells the two apart and lets the full-data import restore `GAME.status`. `EndStatus` is written only for those two statuses. **An unfinished game** (`in_progress`, suspended or not) is exported with `[Result "*"]`, the movetext ending in `*`, **no `Termination` tag and no `EndStatus` tag**, and the moves completed so far (RULE-6, IF-1). Exporting changes nothing in the game. Importing that file gives a review-only game (`status = 'imported'`, no result) that can never be resumed. Only the full-data import restores an unfinished game as unfinished (below). Also imported `GAME_TAG`s, saved variations from `VARIATION`, UTF-8, and deterministic output (byte-identical from every front-end). **Import (IF-2):** multi-game files; UTF-8, falling back to Latin-1 per game; comments, NAGs, and variations kept; files over 10 MB rejected (TBD-25, `ESTIMATE`; the archive has its own, separate limit, §5.2). **Duplicates (CR-4.2):** a game is a duplicate when its tags and moves are identical to a stored game or to an earlier game in the same file, found by a `content_hash`. *Amended 2026-10-31 (AMD-5, D-42; SE interpretation Q-SE-1, confirmed):* "identical tags" means the **Seven Tag Roster values plus `SetUp`, `FEN` and `TimeControl` where present, plus the SAN movetext with the result**. Custom tags (`GameUUID`, `WhiteUUID`, `BlackUUID`, `Mode`, `Rated`, `WhiteClass`, `BlackClass`, `EndStatus`), `Termination`, `ECO` and any unknown tag are ignored, so an export re-imports as a duplicate whichever custom tags a build writes, and a PGN from another tool matches. The value is `"sha256:" + hex(SHA-256(canonical_form))`, where `canonical_form` comes from `chess-rules` (IFP-RUL-5) and the one hash function lives in `ImportExportService`; finalize and import dedupe call the same function. The algorithm prefix keeps the value versionable. A stored game has a hash only once it is terminal (completed, aborted or voided); an unfinished game has none, so it never matches an imported copy (FF-4 compares the hash of a live game with the hash of its re-imported export). The collision prompt offers **Skip this game (default)** and **Keep both**, plus "apply to all remaining collisions". "Replace" is not offered, because the contents are identical and a replacement could overwrite a rated, played game. A skipped duplicate is not a `ParseError`; the report counts imported, skipped and failed. The importer can tell a played game from an imported one by `GAME.status` and `matchup`, but it never needs to, since nothing is replaced. **FEN (IF-3):** six fields, legality checked. **Full-data export (IF-5, DAT-2):** one zip archive (D-29) holding `manifest.json` (`format_version`, build version, export time), `games.pgn` (every game, with custom tags `GameUUID`, `WhiteUUID`, `BlackUUID` and `[%clk]`/`[%emt]` comments so clocks and think times round-trip), `players.json` (including `reset_at`), `ratings.json` (both tracks, full history, both rated-game counters), `settings.json` (preferences, `APP_SETTING`), and **`games.json`**. `games.json` is keyed by `GameUUID` and holds, per game, the stored fields that neither PGN tags nor PGN comments carry: `status`, `mode`, `rated`, `self_play`, `matchup`, `clock_spec`, the clock checkpoints of an unfinished game, `*_elo_before` and `*_elo_after` on the game's scale, `analysis_used`, the engine parameters each computer side used (a copy of its `COMPUTER_PROFILE` values), `termination` text, `voided_by` as a Player UUID, and `content_hash`; and **the analysis results**: per ply `cp`, `mate`, `best_uci`, `cp_loss`, `flag`, `depth` and `engine_version` (a new `MOVE_EVAL` column, e.g. "Stockfish 19"). Clocks and think times stay in PGN `[%clk]` and `[%emt]` comments, and saved variations stay PGN variations. The archive also carries archived Players and unfinished games. **Import accepts an archive without `games.json`** (older or hand-made): the stored fields then come from the PGN custom tags and defaults, there are no analysis results, an unfinished game takes its clocks from its last `[%clk]` comments, and the background evaluation queues every finished game (F-5.5). **`format_version` stays 1**, because nothing has been released; this is an amendment of D-29, not a new format. Import accepts older format versions and rejects newer ones. **Unfinished games on import (CR-4.3):** an unfinished game in the archive is restored as unfinished, with its slot, unless that would give a Player a second unfinished game in a mode (or a computer a second c–c game). Then it is imported review-only with no result, and the preview says so. A game that is live in a front-end of the importing process is never replaced: the import reports it and keeps the stored game. (Another live process refuses the whole import with `Busy`, §7.4.) FEN export is not in v1 (IF-3). Round-trip: `parse(export(g))` gives the same move list (RULE-6, RULE-7) | `ParseError` with game index, line, and column. One bad game never aborts the rest of a file |
| CLI data commands (D-36) | `chess-cli` → user, scripts | Command line | **Single game (UC-22, RULE-6):** `chess export game <game> [--output <file>]`, where `<game>` is a game UUID or the number shown in `chess games`; the PGN goes to the file, or to standard output without `--output`. **Full data (UC-24, DAT-2):** `chess export data --output <file.zip>`. **PGN import (UC-23, RULE-7):** `chess import pgn <file> [--on-duplicate skip\|keep]`. **Full-data import (UC-25, DAT-2):** `chess import data <file.zip> [--yes]` (preview first, then the confirmation; `--yes` answers it for scripts). The bare `chess export` and `chess import` print the four forms and exit 1. The TUI and plain-mode Import/export screens offer the same four actions | Exit 1 usage, 3 store error; messages on standard error |
| FEN uses | — | — | **Start position** in `GameSetup` (GAME-1, unrated) vs. **Import FEN** on the Import screen, which opens an analysis board and creates no game ("review only") | — |
| `BoardGrid` renderer | `chess-domain` → TUI `BoardWidget`, `PlainRunner`, reached through the curated `chess-app` re-exports (§3.2) | Pure | Grid of `{glyph, shade, highlight}` per `BoardStyle` and orientation (CLI-2, UI-3). *Amd 7 (D-38):* built by `BoardGrid::from_fen(fen, &BoardView, style, orientation)`; `BoardView.last_move` is UCI text; `BoardStyle` is `Plain`, `Unicode`, `Ansi`, `NoColor`. No front-end needs a `Position` | Not applicable |
| `Board` contract (`BoardProps`/`onMove`) | `ui/board` → GUI screens | React props | §11.1. Screens never touch chessground | Rejected move → `fen` re-set, piece reverts |
| `CoreClient` | `ui/transport` → GUI client state | Promise plus subscription | §5.2. Two implementations with identical behaviour (GUI-1) | §5.6 |

### 5.8 Two-step PGN import with an ECO mismatch question (D-59, D-60, AGREED)

**Status: AGREED 2026-10-31 (D-59) and refined 2026-11-01 (D-60); user, Brian Prascher, G2/G3.** Requirement CR-9.12 (DAT-3, IF-2) says that when an imported game's `ECO` tag differs from the table's classification of its position, the app flags it and asks the player to choose: (1) **keep the PGN's ECO** and ignore the position classification, or (2) **keep the position classification** and override the PGN's ECO. This section gives the protocol and service changes. Evidence `[INF]`: nothing was built or run.

Question this section answers: how can an import pause for an answer, per game, without a half-written store, in the core, for the CLI, the GUI and a script?

**When a question exists.** `chess-domain` classifies each imported game (the deepest named position, CR-9.11 rules for custom starts and FENs equal to the standard start). **Positions looked up: the start position (ply 0) as well as every position reached by a move**; this matters for a custom start that is itself a named position. The result is one of: *agree*, *mismatch* (both exist and differ), *tag only* (the table finds nothing: the file's code is kept, CR-9.12), *table only* (no tag, or a malformed tag: the table supplies code and name), *neither*. Only *mismatch* asks. The ECO tag is ignored by the duplicate hash (D-42), so the answer never changes whether a game is a duplicate.

**Normalising the tag (D-60).** The tag text is trimmed and upper-cased. It is **well-formed** when it is one letter from A to E followed by two digits (A00 to E99). A malformed tag is treated as **no tag**. The comparison with the table uses the normalised code, and the **stored `GAME.eco` is the normalised code**, never the raw text.

**Shapes (additive).**
- `ImportPgn{file, on_duplicate, eco?}` keeps its name. `eco` is an optional `EcoResolutions{default: keep_pgn | keep_table | none, per_game: {game_index: keep_pgn | keep_table}}`. The protocol values are the underscore forms. A file with no mismatch behaves exactly as today.
- If a mismatch is not covered by `eco`, nothing is written, and the reply is the existing code `ImportConflicts{import_id, list}` with one entry per mismatching game of kind `eco_mismatch`: `{game_index, white, black, tag_eco, table_eco, table_name, ply, moves_san}`.
  - `tag_eco` is the normalised code. `ply` is where the table matched (0 for a named start position). **`moves_san`** is the SAN of the moves that lead from the start to that position, as one text with move numbers (for example `1. e4 e5 2. Nf3 Nc6`; empty when `ply` is 0). The TUI and the dialog show it.
  - **The entry has no `tag_name`**, which an earlier draft had. When the PGN's ECO is kept the stored name is empty (below), the rules crate exposes only the raw tag, and no other tag is read, so there is no source for a tag name and none is needed.
  - The core keeps the parsed games in memory under `import_id`, bound to the calling `FrontEndSession`. **Duplicates stay a separate step (D-62).** With `on_duplicate = Ask`, a duplicate game is answered first by the existing `ImportConflicts{list}` with `duplicate_game` entries and **no `import_id`**; the client sends `ImportPgn` again with its per-game choices (D-34), and only then can the ECO question come, with an `import_id`. A PGN `ImportConflicts` reply therefore holds one kind of entry. (The archive preview is different: it has no second pass and lists Player and game conflicts and `eco_mismatch` entries together in one `ImportConflicts{list}`.) The held state keeps the `on_duplicate` policy of the `ImportPgn` call that created it.
- `ResolveImport{import_id, eco}` completes the import and returns the usual report, which gains the counts `eco_kept_pgn` and `eco_kept_table`. `CancelImport{import_id}` drops the state and writes nothing. Both are idempotent. **`CancelImport` and `ResolveImport` exist for PGN imports only.**
- **Multi-game files.** One reply lists every mismatching game. Answers are per game, with "apply to all remaining" setting `default` (the pattern of the duplicate prompt, D-34). Writing stays all-or-nothing per game (IF-2); a game with a parse error is reported and does not block the others.
- **Archives are stateless (D-60).** `PreviewDataImport{file}` returns `ImportConflicts{list}` with `eco_mismatch` entries and **no `import_id`**; the core holds nothing. The choices travel in `ApplyDataImport`, which gains an optional `eco: EcoResolutions` (additive). As the app design already has it, the core re-reads the archive from the file reference when applying, so a remote client sends the archive twice. An unanswered mismatch in `ApplyDataImport` returns `ImportConflicts{list}` again and writes nothing. Cancelling an archive import means not sending the apply. The question applies only to a game whose stored fields come from PGN tags (an archive without `games.json`, or a game missing from it); a game with stored `eco` and `opening_name` in `games.json` keeps them and asks nothing.
- **Cancel.** The player closes the dialog or sends `CancelImport`: nothing was written.
- **Stale or lost state (PGN imports).** The held state is dropped on `CancelImport`, on session end, when the same session starts another import, and after an **idle time-out of 10 minutes (`ESTIMATE`, a design value and not a requirement; measure or revise with use)**. `ResolveImport` with an unknown, expired or foreign `import_id` returns the existing `NotFound{entity: import, id}`, and the client starts again from `ImportPgn`. The file is not stored on disk. Duplicates and Player conflicts are checked again when games are written, and a game that has become a duplicate follows the `on_duplicate` policy held with the state. A `ResolveImport` that leaves a mismatch unanswered is refused with `ImportConflicts` again.
- The file-size limits (10 MB for PGN, about 24 MiB for an archive) are checked on the first call, before parsing.

**`ImportExportService` responsibilities (added).** Parse with `chess-rules` (which exposes the raw `ECO` tag); normalise and classify through the domain; hold one pending PGN import per `FrontEndSession`; build the mismatch list with `moves_san`; apply the resolutions; write each game in its own transaction; count the outcomes. It never asks anything itself; the question is the reply. `GameSession`, the journal and the engine are not involved.

**Journal and store impact.** None. An imported game has `status = 'imported'`, no journal, and writes the same `GAME.eco` and `GAME.opening_name` columns; only the values depend on the answer, and the answer is not stored (no `PREFERENCES` key, OQ-ECO-3).

**Presentation constraints.**
- *TUI (80x24, keyboard only, no mouse):* one mismatch per screen: game number and players, the file's code, the table's code and name, and `moves_san`. Keys: `1` keep the PGN's code, `2` keep the table's, `a` apply this choice to all remaining, `Esc` cancel the import, `n`/`p` for next and previous game.
- *Plain mode:* **when standard input is a terminal, plain mode asks** the same question (prompt on standard error, answer from standard input; end of input cancels, nothing written). Only a run whose **standard input is not a terminal, and a headless run, are non-interactive** (D-60).
- *Non-interactive runs:* the import command takes the explicit option **`--eco keep-pgn`** or **`--eco keep-table`**. The CLI maps these hyphenated values to the protocol values `keep_pgn` and `keep_table` (the `default` of `EcoResolutions`); the protocol, the app and the TypeScript client keep the underscore forms. With a mismatch and no option, the command writes nothing, prints the mismatching games on standard error and exits with code 1. There is no silent default. The same applies to `chess import data`. The numeric exit value follows TBD-36.
- *GUI:* a dialog with two radio choices per game and "apply to all remaining", fully keyboard reachable, with an ARIA description that states both values (UI-4, GUI-2). It is not a `ConfirmationRequired` action.

**Rules decided by the user (OQ-ECO-1 to OQ-ECO-3 on 2026-10-31; the items above marked D-60 on 2026-11-01).**
- **Bulk and non-interactive answers (OQ-ECO-1).** Interactive screens ask per game and offer "apply to all remaining". A non-interactive run requires the explicit option; without it, exit 1 with the list.
- **Stored name (OQ-ECO-2).** When the player keeps the PGN's ECO, the stored `opening_name` is empty and `eco` is the normalised file code. Choosing the table stores the table's code and name.
- **Remembered (OQ-ECO-3).** Not in v1. Every import asks again; "apply to all remaining" holds within one import.

---

## 6. Front-ends

### 6.1 CLI (`chess`): ratatui terminal UI

The CLI is a full-screen terminal UI built with **`ratatui`** (rendering) and **`crossterm`** (terminal I/O, keys, resize). It is **keyboard only** (CLI-1/3, AGREED): moves are typed in algebraic notation, there is no board cursor, and mouse capture is never enabled — which also leaves the terminal's normal text selection and copy working. It links the core in-process like the other front-ends and follows the same rules as the GUI: it renders `GameSnapshot`s, shows only the controls in `allowed_actions`, keeps rewind as local view state, and counts clocks down locally from `ClockSync`.

#### Run modes

| Mode | Selected when | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| **TUI** (default) | stdin and stdout are terminals and no scripting flags are given | Alternate screen, raw mode, full-screen layout, live clocks and spinner |
| **Plain** | `--plain`, or stdin/stdout is not a terminal | Line-oriented transcript: board, last move, clocks, and prompt printed after each change. For screen readers (UI-4) and pipes. |
| **Headless** | scripting flags (`--matchup cvc --white … --black … --clock 5+0`) | **Computer–computer, Strict only** (CR-4.12). No UI. Runs to completion, prints the PGN, and exits with a status code. The game is stored and rated like any c–c game (D-36). Drives the c–c scenario tests |

**Selection rule (D-19).** Rules are checked in order:
1. `--headless`, or top-level setup flags with no subcommand (`chess --matchup cvc --white … --black …`, as in CLI-4), selects headless. Headless needs a fully specified setup of two computer Players. **Naming a human Player, or a matchup other than `cvc`, is a usage error: exit 1, nothing on stdout, a message on stderr.** A computer that already has an unfinished c–c game is a setup error (exit 1): a headless run never offers resume (D-27). *(Earlier text let a human side read moves from stdin in headless mode. That is withdrawn: scripted human play uses plain mode, below.)*
2. `--plain`, or stdin/stdout not a TTY, selects plain.
3. Otherwise TUI. `chess play [setup flags]` opens the TUI pre-filled with the setup.

**Scripted human play** (the h–c and h–h scenario tests, PLAT-1) runs in plain mode with moves piped to standard input: `chess play --plain --player <name> [setup flags] < moves.txt`. It uses the same `InputParser` and the same stored games as the interactive modes.

**Data directory (CLI-4, D-36).** The global option `--data-dir <path>` selects another data directory. It **overrides `CHESS_DATA_DIR`**, which overrides the default `~/Library/Application Support/Chess/` (precedence stated again in §7.1). A directory that does not exist is created (mode `0700`) and given the seeded roster, as on a first launch. Scripted tests and calibration use it, so they never touch the user's ratings (RISK-2, R-17).

Exit codes (CLI-5, TBD-21): 0 game reached a result, 1 usage or setup error, 2 engine unavailable, 3 store error (*Addendum E: this class includes `DurabilityUnsupported`, D-71; Brian accepted exit class 3 on 2026-11-04, G3, beads chess-a0r.1.30; no new code number*), **4 stopped by a signal** (architect-chosen, D-36; accepted by CR-5). A headless run stopped by SIGINT or SIGTERM leaves an **unfinished** game, which the journal already holds (D-14, §7.6), prints no PGN, and exits 4. PGN goes to stdout, and messages go to stderr. **`save` (CLI-4)** is a confirmation: it makes a round trip through the game's session actor (so it sees every earlier command), checks that the last move is durable, and prints "Game saved" with the stored move count. It changes nothing. **`movenow`** (ENG-5) is the in-game command for "move now" (F2 does the same, §4.4).

**Plain mode and screen readers.** Plain mode never prints clock ticks. It prints the clocks with each move and as warnings at fixed thresholds (e.g., 60 s, 10 s), so a screen reader isn't flooded. Opponent moves are printed as they arrive, even while the user is typing.

All three modes share `InputParser` (command words, SAN, and coordinate moves) and the board renderer in `chess-domain`. That renderer produces a terminal-independent `BoardGrid`: per square, a glyph, a light/dark shade, and highlights (last move, check, rewound). The TUI's `BoardWidget` maps that grid to ratatui styles. Plain mode prints it as text lines. One snapshot suite therefore covers both modes (CLI-1/2, UI-3).

#### Play screen layout (80×24 minimum)

```text
┌ Chess ─ Strict · 5+0 · Rated ───────────────────────────────────── F1 help ┐
│    A  B  C  D  E  F  G  H        Black  Viktor Rook (Master 2310)   04:51  │
│ 8  ♜  ♞  ♝  ♛  ♚  ♝  ·  ♜  8     ⠿ thinking…  (F2 move now)                │
│ 7  ♟  ♟  ♟  ♟  ·  ♟  ♟  ♟  7     ──────────────────────────────────────    │
│ 6  ·  ·  ·  ·  ·  ♞  ·  ·  6      1. e4      e5                            │
│ 5  ·  ·  ·  ·  ♘  ·  ·  ·  5      2. Nf3     Nf6                           │
│ 4  ·  ·  ·  ·  ♙  ·  ·  ·  4      3. Nxe5    …                             │
│ 3  ·  ·  ·  ·  ·  ·  ·  ·  3                                               │
│ 2  ♙  ♙  ♙  ♙  ·  ♙  ♙  ♙  2     ──────────────────────────────────────    │
│ 1  ♖  ♘  ♗  ♕  ♔  ♗  ·  ♖  1     White  Brian (1243)                04:38  │
│    A  B  C  D  E  F  G  H                                                  │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Last: Nxe5 (White) · ←/→ rewind · End live · Tab focus · Esc clear         │
│ > _                                                                        │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

- The board panel sits on the left; the right panel holds the opponent, clocks, engine-thinking indicator, scrollable move list, and the bottom player. `flip` (or Black at the bottom) turns the board and its A–H/1–8 labels (CLI-2).
- The status line shows the last move, errors (e.g., "Illegal: Nf3 — no knight can reach f3"), the "live position has changed" badge while rewound (GAME-3), and context-sensitive key hints built from `allowed_actions`.
- The input line has editing, Up/Down history, and paste.
- **Minimum size (CLI-1, Q55): fixed at 80×24.** Every screen is designed to fit 80×24. If the terminal is smaller:
  1. At startup or on resize, the TUI asks the terminal to resize itself to 80×24 with the xterm window-manipulation sequence `CSI 8 ; 24 ; 80 t`. Terminal.app and iTerm2 are expected to honour it; this is checked in both (R-16, and iTerm2 has a setting that can block it). It records the original size first (`CSI 18 t`) and restores it on exit as part of terminal hygiene.
  2. If no resize event of at least 80×24 arrives within 300 ms (`ESTIMATE`), the screen is replaced by a "please enlarge the terminal to 80×24" message. Inside tmux or screen the sequence is ignored, so the message appears.
  3. The TUI never asks again until the user resizes, so it doesn't fight a user who shrinks the window on purpose.

#### Screens

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    pick["Choose profile<br/>(every launch: registered Players or Register new, Q48)"] --> home["Home menu"]
    pick --> reg["Register<br/>(name ≤ 128 user-perceived characters + self-assessment)"] --> home
    home --> setup["New Game<br/>matchup · mode · players ·<br/>colour · clock (Strict only) · start FEN"]
    setup --> play["Play<br/>(h–c, h–h hot-seat)<br/>rewind · takeback (Training)"]
    setup --> watch["Watch<br/>(c–c: pause · step · delay · abort)"]
    home --> resume["Resume game<br/>(your games, either side,<br/>and every c–c game)"] --> play
    play --> result["Result dialog"]
    watch --> result
    result --> post["Post-game analysis<br/>eval per move · flags · what-if"]
    play -- "analyze (Training only)" --> live["Live analysis / what-if<br/>overlay"]
    live --> play
    home --> stats["Statistics<br/>any Player · tables +<br/>Elo chart in character cells"]
```
