```mermaid
    home --> roster["Computer Players<br/>class · Elo · W/L/D ·<br/>generate 1 to 25"]
    home --> games["Game history<br/>→ replay / analysis / PGN export"]
    home --> data["Import / export<br/>PGN · FEN (review only) · full-data backup"]
    home --> settings["Settings"]
    home --> players["Players<br/>switch active · rename · archive"]
    home --> help["Help (F1, any screen)"]
```

Each screen is a `Screen` implementation with its own state, key handling, and `render`. Forms (setup, settings, registration) use focusable fields where Tab/Shift-Tab moves and Enter confirms. Fields the policy disallows (e.g., Training for h–h, a clock in Training) are not offered (GAME-1). Statistics use ratatui's `Table`, `Chart`, and `Sparkline` widgets (UI-2, UI-5).

**Active Player (PLY-2, CR-4.9).** Picking a profile, or registering a Player at launch, makes that Player the active Player of this front-end session (`SelectProfile`). Registering a Player later (from the Players screen) does not change the active Player. The active Player can be switched at any time when no game is open in this front-end, by returning to the profile chooser. A front-end session has exactly one active Player; two front-ends may differ.

**Players screen (PLY-4, CR-4.10).** Rename and archive are offered for human Players only, and are not offered for computer Players. A human with any unfinished game cannot be archived (`PlayerHasUnfinishedGames` names the games); the active Player cannot archive itself. There is **no un-archive in v1**, and nothing is deleted. An archived Player's name stays taken (PLY-6).

**Empty and partial states (UI-2, ANA-3, CR-4.15).**
- With no games, statistics show zero counts, "—" for every average, the text "no games yet", and each Elo chart shows its baseline point.
- Analysis of a game whose background evaluation has not finished shows "evaluating…" in place of each missing value, raises no blunder flag for it, shows "n of N plies evaluated" from the `evaluated` and `total` counts, and fills in values as `AnalysisProgress` events arrive.
- Games started from a custom position are excluded from W/L/D and from every aggregate, as self-play is, and are listed in the game history marked "custom start" (H-17).

**CLI Elo chart (D-37).** The TUI draws each Elo series with ratatui's `Chart` using a Braille marker, with a table of the latest points beside it. The ASCII style (and any terminal without Unicode) draws the same series on a character grid of `*` and `.` from a small pure renderer, so the snapshot tests cover both. Plain mode prints the table and a one-line sparkline (block glyphs, or the ASCII ramp `_.-^` in the ASCII style) per series. The GUI chart library stays deferred (D-21).

#### Event loop (Elm-style)

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    keys["crossterm EventStream<br/>keys · resize · paste<br/>(no mouse events)"] --> msg
    coreev["Core event subscription<br/>MoveApplied · ClockSync · GameEnded · …"] --> msg
    tick["Tick timer ~10 Hz<br/>clock display · spinner"] --> msg
    sig["tokio::signal::unix<br/>SIGTERM / SIGHUP / SIGCONT<br/>(raising SIGTSTP: file-lock spike, OQ-14)"] --> msg
    msg(["Msg"]) --> update["update(state, msg)<br/>→ new state + effects"]
    update --> state["TuiState<br/>screen stack · snapshot · view cursor ·<br/>input buffer · focus · status"]
    update --> effects["Effects<br/>core.execute / core.query"]
    effects --> core["chess-app Core<br/>(in-process)"]
    core --> coreev
    state --> view["render(state, frame)<br/>pure, ratatui widgets<br/>(resize request, then enlarge message below 80×24)"]
    view --> term["Terminal<br/>(alternate screen, restored on quit,<br/>Ctrl-C, panic)"]
```

- **One `tokio::select!` loop** over terminal events, core events, and a tick. There are no blocking input threads, so engine moves, flag falls, and clock updates appear while the user is typing.
- **`update` is separate from `render`.** `update` can be unit-tested with synthetic `Msg`s and no terminal. `render` draws into a ratatui `TestBackend` for snapshot tests at fixed sizes.
- **Key routing (`KeyMap`, CLI-4).** Keys that can never start a move are bindings: ←/→ back/forward, End live, PgUp/PgDn move list, F1 help, **F2 move now** (only while a computer thinks in an h–c game, otherwise shown unavailable), Tab focus, Esc clear/cancel. F1 and F2 are in the help screen generated from `KeyMap` (UI-9), and the `KeyMap` test checks that both are bound. On a Mac laptop F-keys may need Fn, so the command `movenow` always works as well. Every printable character (letters, digits, `-`, `=`) goes to the input line. On Enter, `InputParser` resolves the text to a command word or a move. A unit test checks that no binding consumes a character that can begin SAN or coordinate notation. Because there is no board cursor, the arrow keys are reserved for rewind. The Watch screen has no move input, so it uses plain keys (Space, `s`, `+`/`-`, `a`).
- **Terminal hygiene.** A guard object enters raw mode and the alternate screen and restores both on `Drop`. A panic hook restores the terminal before printing the panic. Ctrl-C arrives as a key in raw mode and always asks for confirmation before quitting (Q52). Every move is already journalled (§7.6), so quitting is always safe. Resign (in hot-seat h–h, after asking which side resigns), c–c abort, archive, "reset player data", applying an import (after its preview) and restoring a backup also always ask, and the core refuses them without `confirmed: true` (§5.5). The Ctrl-C quit dialog is the TUI's own and uses the same dialog widget (UI-12). Cancel and Esc change nothing. Ctrl-Z restores the terminal, suspends via `SIGTSTP`, and redraws on `SIGCONT` (signals through `tokio::signal`; raising `SIGTSTP` follows the file-lock spike, OQ-14; `signal-hook` is rejected, D-47).
- **Styles.** `BoardStyle` = plain ASCII, Unicode glyphs, ANSI colour, light/dark squares. With `NO_COLOR` or `--no-color`, no colour is emitted. The plain ASCII style also switches panel borders to an ASCII border set.
- **Startup (NFR-1).** The first frame is drawn before the engine starts; engines start lazily.

#### Subcommands

`chess` (profile chooser, then TUI home), `chess play [--player <name>] [setup flags]` (straight into a game), `chess serve [--port <n>] [--no-open] [--config <path>]` (port precedence in §8.2), `chess install-cli [--remove]` (§8.2), `chess stats [player]` / `chess players` (open the TUI screen, or print tables in plain mode), `chess games` (lists stored games with the numbers `export game` accepts), `chess export game <game>` and `chess export data` (UC-22 and UC-24), `chess import pgn <file>` and `chess import data <file.zip>` (UC-23 and UC-25) with the exact forms in §5.7, `chess calibrate` (dev). The global option `--data-dir <path>` applies to every subcommand. `install-cli` and `--help` run before the core is created, so they never open the store or start the engine.

#### `chess serve` lifecycle (PLAT-4, D-36)

- **Configuration.** A missing `config.toml` means defaults. A file that is unreadable, not valid TOML, or whose `server.port` is outside 1 to 65535 stops the command with exit 1 and a message naming the file, the line and the reason (`ConfigInvalid`). There is no silent fallback to a default. Unknown keys are warned about (the file is read-only, see §8.2). `--config <path>` and `CHESS_CONFIG` are **architect-added** (tests use them; the requirements do not ask for them) and are kept.
- **Port in use.** Exit 1 with a message naming the port, and the URL of the other `chess serve` when `PROCESS_HEARTBEAT` shows one (§8.2).
- **Browser will not open.** The server keeps running, the URL is printed with a notice that the browser could not be opened, and the exit status is not affected.
- **Stop.** Ctrl-C (SIGINT) or SIGTERM, with no confirmation (the server has no user at the keyboard to ask). The server stops accepting connections, sends `ServerStopping` to every connected page (the page shows "server stopped" and does not retry), suspends each open game as an interruption (D-14: `Suspend` journalled, clocks frozen), and exits 0. Stopping takes at most 5 s (`ESTIMATE`, §4.8); every acknowledged move is already durable, so the limit loses nothing.

### 6.2 GUI (web and desktop, one codebase)

```mermaid
flowchart TB
    subgraph ui["ui/ (TypeScript, React + Vite) — one bundle for web and desktop, all assets local (CSP: self)"]
        screens["Screens<br/>Register · Home · New Game · Play · Watch (c–c) ·<br/>Analysis · Game history · Stats (any Player) ·<br/>Computer Players · Players · Import/Export · Settings · Help"]
        board["Board component (D-4)<br/>chessground (visuals, mouse/touch, aria-hidden)<br/>+ AccessibleOverlay (ARIA grid, keyboard)<br/>+ BoardModel reducer (one selection state)"]
        san["Typed SAN input<br/>+ PromotionPicker"]
        controls["Controls rendered from<br/>allowed_actions (Policy)"]
        store["Client state<br/>game snapshot + local view cursor (rewind)<br/>+ local clock countdown"]
        themes["Themes<br/>≥10 board CSS · ≥5 audited piece sets"]
        client["CoreClient interface"]
        ws["WebSocketCoreClient"]
        tc["TauriCoreClient"]
        gen["protocol/ (generated from Rust by ts-rs)"]
        screens --> board
        screens --> san
        screens --> controls
        screens --> store
        board --> themes
        store --> client
        client --> ws
        client --> tc
        ws -.-> gen
        tc -.-> gen
    end
    ws -- "ws://127.0.0.1" --> server["chess-server"]
    tc -- "Tauri IPC" --> tauri["src-tauri"]
```

- **Framework:** React + TypeScript + Vite (AGREED, D-5): the largest ecosystem, mature testing tools (Testing Library, Playwright, axe), and good accessibility support.
- **Board (AGREED, D-4):** `@lichess-org/chessground` (pinned 10.x, GPL-3.0-or-later) renders the board and handles mouse, touch, and animation, wrapped by our own React component. Our shared layer adds the keyboard model (GUI-2) and screen-reader support (UI-4): a transparent ARIA-grid overlay and a `BoardModel` reducer that owns the one selection state. Full design in §11.1. Themes are chessground CSS classes for boards plus license-audited SVG piece sets; contrast ratios are checked by a unit test.
- **Rewind** is a local view cursor over the snapshot's ply list. While rewound, move input is disabled. The "live position has changed" badge appears when a `MoveApplied` arrives with the cursor behind live (GAME-3).
- **Elo chart:** a small charting dependency or hand-built SVG (D-21, DEFERRED). Either way it has a text or table alternative for screen readers (UI-4).
- **Settings take effect in the client (UI-1, GUI-3).** `PREFERENCES` drive:
  - `BoardProps`: theme, piece set, orientation, coordinates on/off (GUI-3, default on), legal-move hints;
  - app colour theme (UI-10): System (default, follows `prefers-color-scheme` and switches live when macOS appearance changes), Light, Dark, or another bundled theme. It is applied as CSS custom properties on the root element. Every theme, the high-contrast one included, meets WCAG AA (UI-7), and nothing more is required of the high-contrast theme;
  - the default opponent class and colour in New Game.
  Changes are saved with `UpdatePreferences` and apply immediately. The other entry points pick them up the next time they read preferences.
- **Move commit rules (GUI-2, Q52).** All input methods are active during play, and there is no confirm-move setting and no "default input method" setting. A move commits:
  - mouse or touch: when the piece is dropped on a legal square (click-click: on the second click);
  - keyboard overlay: when the destination square is selected with Enter/Space;
  - typed SAN: on Enter.
  A promotion opens the `PromotionPicker` first, and the move commits when a piece is chosen.
- **No sound in v1** (Q52). There are no sound assets, no sound setting, and no terminal bell.
- **Language.** English only in v1 (Q52). UI strings still live in one module per front-end, which keeps the help content (UI-9) and messages (NFR-9) in one place.
- **Layout (Q55).** There is no minimum window size. The layout is fluid (CSS grid with `clamp()` sizing), and the board is a square sized from its container. A `ResizeObserver` triggers chessground's `redrawAll()`, which keeps the `AccessibleOverlay` aligned at every size. Below a narrow breakpoint, panels stack under the board instead of beside it.
- **Profiles (Q48).** Each GUI session starts at the profile chooser: registered Players, or Register new. The chosen Player is session state (`SelectProfile`) and becomes the **active Player**: picking or registering at launch sets it, registering later does not change it, and it can be switched whenever no game is open in this front-end (§6.1). Two tabs or apps can use different profiles. `APP_SETTING.last_active_player` only pre-selects the list.
- **Game feedback (GUI-4).** Last move, check, and legal destinations come from `BoardProps`. The move list comes from the snapshot's plies. Captured pieces are derived in the client from the snapshot (material difference at the viewed ply). The result dialog is shown on `GameEnded`, and the thinking indicator on `EngineThinking`. While a computer thinks in an h–c game the indicator carries a **"Move now" button** (keyboard reachable, ARIA label "Move now", activated by Enter or Space, and bound to F2); it sends `CancelEngine` and is not rendered as available in any other state or matchup (ENG-5, GUI-4).
- **Connection loss (web).** §5.6: banner, inputs disabled, then reconnect and resnapshot.

### 6.3 Desktop (Tauri)

- `src-tauri` creates the `Core` at startup, using the same data-directory resolution as the CLI. It registers one `core` command and forwards events.
- It bundles the Stockfish binary as a Tauri helper binary (Tauri's word is "sidecar", which here means only a bundled helper; the Rust core spawns it from `<exe dir>/stockfish`, never the shell, AMD-9, UR-37), the `chess` CLI binary beside it (symlinked onto `PATH`, §8.2), and the license notices. There is one bundle, so CLI and desktop are always the same version (D-16).
- Packaging: a universal (arm64 + x86_64) `.app`/`.dmg` (PLAT-1, Q46), **ad-hoc signed** (no Developer ID, no notarization; Apple silicon will not run unsigned code), built with `cargo tauri build --target universal-apple-darwin`. See §8.2.
- **No single-instance mechanism** (user decision 11, 2026-10-31; *amended*: the earlier sentence "a second launch focuses the existing window" is struck). It is not a requirement (SE desk answer Q-SE-21), and a second desktop instance is valid under DAT-1 and DAT-4. Concurrent launches, including the first-launch roster race against the CLI or `chess serve`, are settled by the store's own locking, not by a UI rule, and no plugin dependency is added.
- **Main binary name (AMD-9, UR-38).** The Tauri main executable is renamed in the bundle configuration so that `Contents/MacOS/` never holds two names that differ only in case (the Tauri main binary and the CLI binary `chess` would collide on a case-insensitive volume). The new name is chosen when packaging is set up (OQ-11).
- Closing the window quits the app, which suspends any live game (RULE-5). All of this is the block `Tauri shell and IPC adapter` (§2.2).

---

## 7. Persistence

### 7.1 Technology

- **SQLite** through `rusqlite` with the `bundled` feature (no system dependency). Pragmas: `journal_mode=WAL`, `synchronous=FULL`, `fullfsync=ON` and `checkpoint_fullfsync=ON` (on macOS a plain `fsync` does not flush the drive's cache, so this makes SQLite use `F_FULLFSYNC`), `foreign_keys=ON`, `busy_timeout=5000`.
- **Durability (NFR-2, RULE-5, Q54: decided).** No completed move is ever lost, whatever happens: app crash, kill, disconnect, OS crash, or power loss. Every move is written to the game's move journal and flushed with `F_FULLFSYNC` before it is acknowledged. The journal is then applied to SQLite, whose own commits are also fully synced (§7.6). After any interruption, the clock comes back at the last recorded value: the last move or 1 s checkpoint, so at most 1 s of clock is lost (TBD-7 proposed: 1 s). TBD-23 (moves lost on power loss) is 0. The flush costs a few ms per move (`ESTIMATE`, measured by the NFR-8 latency test), which is negligible at chess move rates.
- **Volume check: refuse, do not downgrade (Amd 8, D-71; APPROVED by Brian, 2026-11-04; his decision at G-1, BP-014).** On open, before the store accepts any write, it probes the data directory with `fcntl(F_FULLFSYNC)` on a file it owns there (`store.id` or a scratch file in the directory). If the call fails with `ENOTSUP` or `ENOTTY`, the store **refuses to open** the directory and returns `DurabilityUnsupported{path, errno}` (§5.5). It does not fall back to `sync_all`: that would weaken NFR-2 silently, and TBD-23 stays closed at 0 moves lost. This replaces the fallback in the store design's ASM-ST-4. A `F_FULLFSYNC` failure of that kind later, at a journal append, is a write failure: the move is not made and the user sees `JournalWriteFailed{cause}` (existing). Consequence: a data directory on a volume without full sync (an external or network volume reached through `--data-dir` or `CHESS_DATA_DIR`, D-36) cannot be used; the default directory on the internal APFS volume is expected to pass `[UNV]` (CT-ST-001 on the development Mac, OQ-14 spike). The front-ends show the path, the reason ("this folder is on a volume that cannot guarantee that a move survives a power loss") and the way out (choose another folder), then exit or, in the desktop shell, show an in-window screen (not a native dialog; Brian, 2026-11-04, CR-12 item 7; the Quit button is OQ-TAU-005); they do not offer "continue anyway". No new exit code is invented here: the CLI uses its existing store-open failure code, **exit class 3** (store error), which Brian accepted for `DurabilityUnsupported` on 2026-11-04 (G3, beads chess-a0r.1.30); **it applies in the TUI, in plain mode and in subcommands, and TBD-36 is not narrowed** (CR-12 item 7); the CLI, UI, Tauri and server designs must carry it (§11.4.13, list of SDD edits). The refusal is an NFR-2 verification case (CR-12 item b, TBR-4): CT-ST-085, CT-APP-33 and CT-ST-001.
- **Location and precedence (D-36):** `~/Library/Application Support/Chess/chess.db`. Precedence: (1) the `--data-dir <path>` option, (2) `CHESS_DATA_DIR`, (3) the default. A directory that does not exist is created with the roster, as on a first launch. Every test and `chess calibrate` isolate state this way.
- **Migrations:** numbered SQL files tracked with `PRAGMA user_version`. Before any migration, the file is copied with `VACUUM INTO chess.db.bak-v<N>`. **If that backup cannot be written the upgrade does not start** (DAT-6, D-35): the store is left unchanged, `BackupFailed{cause}` shows the cause, and the user may retry or exit. A newer front-end never opens an old-version store without the upgrade. Fixture databases for each released schema version are kept in the repo for migration tests (DAT-1).
- **Corruption:** on open, `PRAGMA quick_check`. If it fails, the app explains the problem, offers to restore the latest backup, and never overwrites the file silently (DAT-5). A restore is all-or-nothing and replays **every retained journal**, including those in `journal/done/` for games that finished after the backup was taken; the journal wins on any disagreement, and each such game is finalised again in order of its end time so its Elo change and statistics apply once. Details in §7.6 and D-35.
- **Automatic backups (D-35; stated in DAT-5 by CR-5.1).** Requirements DAT-5 and DAT-6 assume a "most recent backup" exists, but only the pre-upgrade and pre-import copies were defined. The store therefore also writes `backups/chess-<UTC date>.db` with `VACUUM INTO`, followed by `quick_check` of the copy, at start-up when the newest backup is older than 24 h (TBD-33, `ESTIMATE`) and no other process is live. The newest 3 automatic copies are kept, and the pre-upgrade and pre-import copies are never pruned by this rule. Requirements DAT-5 (Amendment 3, CR-5.1) now states the cadence and the retention; a failed automatic backup is logged and never stops start-up (function F-6.15). *Amd 7 (SE interpretation Q-SE-19, confirmed):* any complete backup copy that was verified to open counts as "the newest backup" for the 24 h rule, whichever event wrote it (automatic, pre-upgrade or pre-import); a full-data export is not a store backup and does not count. At an upgrade the order is backup, migrate, replay journals.
- **Missing store (Q51).** When a store is created, a `store.id` file (store uuid, creation time) is written next to it. On open, if `chess.db` is absent:
  - nothing else in the data dir (no `store.id`, backups, or journals) means a genuine first launch. The store is created silently (PLAT-3) and the profile chooser opens on Register.
  - any trace of an earlier store returns `StoreMissing`. The user chooses **start fresh** (`CreateFreshStore`, keeping the traces aside in `missing-<date>/`) or **import from a file** (a full-data export or a backup).

### 7.2 Data model (baseline v1.0)

```mermaid
erDiagram
    PLAYER ||--o| PREFERENCES : has
    PLAYER ||--o| COMPUTER_PROFILE : "if computer"
    PLAYER ||--o{ RATING_HISTORY : "every Player (Q41)"
    PLAYER ||--o{ GAME : "plays white"
    PLAYER ||--o{ GAME : "plays black"
    GAME ||--o{ MOVE : contains
    GAME ||--o{ VARIATION : "saved what-ifs"
    GAME ||--o| ANALYSIS_JOB : "queued after end"
    GAME ||--o| GAME_LEASE : "while live"
    GAME ||--o{ UNFINISHED_SLOT : "occupies until finished"
    PLAYER ||--o{ UNFINISHED_SLOT : "at most one per mode"
    GAME ||--o{ GAME_TAG : "PGN headers"
    MOVE ||--o| MOVE_EVAL : "computed post-game"
    RATING_HISTORY }o--o| GAME : "caused by"

    PLAYER {
        int id PK "internal row key, never leaves chess-store (D-40)"
        text uuid "stable identity for import merge (Q51)"
        text kind "human | computer"
        text name "UNIQUE case-sensitive across every Player, NFC, trimmed, max 128 grapheme clusters, case kept (PLY-6)"
        text start_category "self-assessed (human) or creation class (computer)"
        int strict_elo "dynamic for every Player (Q41)"
        int training_elo "separate track, same baseline (ELO-2)"
        int strict_rated_games "Strict K counter: 40 for games 1-30, then 32"
        int training_rated_games "Training K counter, counted separately"
        datetime reset_at "humans only: hides older games from this Player views"
        bool archived
        datetime created_at
    }
    COMPUTER_PROFILE {
        int player_id PK, FK
        int roster_seed
        int roster_index "flat index; the interleaving is the domain's (D-46)"
        int skill_level
        int depth_limit
        int movetime_ms
        int uci_elo "nullable"
        int error_permille "0 to 1000, integer (D-43)"
        int error_multipv
    }
    PREFERENCES {
        int player_id PK, FK
        text json "versioned settings blob (UI-1)"
    }
    GAME {
        int id PK "internal row key (D-40)"
        text uuid
        int white_id FK "null for imported PGN"
        int black_id FK "null for imported PGN"
        text matchup "hh | hc | cc"
        text mode "training | strict"
        bool rated
        bool self_play
        bool custom_start "1 iff the start differs from the standard start; equals (start_fen is not null) (SF-02, D-64)"
        text start_fen "null = the standard start; never the standard FEN (SF-02)"
        text clock_spec "null | base+inc in whole seconds, e.g. 300+0 (Strict only, SF-11)"
        int white_clock_ms "checkpoint for resume (RULE-5), null if untimed"
        int black_clock_ms "checkpoint for resume (RULE-5), null if untimed"
        text status "in_progress | completed | aborted | voided | imported"
        text result "1-0 | 0-1 | 1/2-1/2, null if aborted or voided"
        text termination "voided by reset (D-30) or voided: unreadable (DAT-7), and the normal reasons"
        int voided_by FK "the reset Player, null for voided: unreadable"
        text eco "code of the deepest named position (D-20)"
        text opening_name "name from the ECO table (D-20)"
        datetime started_at
        datetime ended_at
        int white_time_used_ms
        int black_time_used_ms
        int total_active_ms
        bool analysis_used
        int white_elo_before
        int white_elo_after
        int black_elo_before
        int black_elo_after
        text content_hash "null iff in_progress; sha256:v1:hex over the canonical form as of game end (SF-03, SF-04): PGN duplicate check and import dedupe (§5.7, D-34, D-42, D-68)"
        int journal_seq "last journal record applied (§7.6)"
    }
    MOVE {
        int game_id PK, FK
        int ply PK
        text san
        text uci
        text fen_after
        int think_ms
        int white_clock_ms
        int black_clock_ms
        datetime played_at
    }
    MOVE_EVAL {
        int game_id PK, FK
        int ply PK, FK
        int cp "nullable, from White point of view (D-41)"
        int mate "nullable, signed like cp, positive when White mates"
        text best_uci
        int cp_loss
        text flag "blunder | mistake | inaccuracy | null"
        int depth
        text engine_version "e.g. Stockfish 19 (games.json, ANA-3)"
    }
    RATING_HISTORY {
        int id PK
        int player_id FK
        text track "strict | training"
        int game_id FK "null for an initial or reset point"
        int rating_before
        int rating_after
        int k
        datetime at
    }
    VARIATION {
        int id PK
        int game_id FK
        int from_ply "anchor ply on the main line"
        text pgn "PGN variation text"
        datetime created_at "no player_id: filtered by game (PLY-7, decision 13)"
    }
    ANALYSIS_JOB {
        int game_id PK, FK
        text state "queued | running | done | failed"
        text owner "process id"
        datetime heartbeat_at
    }
    GAME_TAG {
        int game_id PK, FK
        text name PK
        text value
    }
    GAME_LEASE {
        int game_id PK, FK
        text owner "process kind + pid"
        datetime heartbeat_at
    }
    UNFINISHED_SLOT {
        int player_id PK, FK
        text mode PK "training | strict"
        int game_id FK
    }
    APP_SETTING {
        text setting_key PK "e.g. last_active_player, roster_seed"
        text value
    }
    PROCESS_HEARTBEAT {
        text owner PK "process kind + pid"
        text build_version "build version string (D-41)"
        datetime heartbeat_at
    }
```

Notes:
- **Stored-format batch (Amd 7, AMD-5, D-41; G3, decided before any store code exists, nothing is released).** (1) `GAME.content_hash` is `TEXT NULL`, set in the finalize, abort and void transactions. *Addendum A, ruling 1:* `chess-app` computes the value before the transaction and passes it in (finalize and abort inputs, `VoidSpec` for a reset, the unreadable-game void), because the store has no `chess-rules`; the same value is written into the `GameEnd`, `Abort` or `Voided` journal payload, so a restore re-runs the transaction with it (§7.6). (2) `COMPUTER_PROFILE.error_permille INTEGER` replaces `error_rate REAL`, so the unique index of ENG-2 uses integer equality. (3) `PROCESS_HEARTBEAT.build_version` is `TEXT`. (4) `MOVE_EVAL.cp` and `mate` are stored from **White's point of view**; `chess-app` normalises the engine's side-to-move score. `cp_loss` stays the mover's point of view (ANA-3). The start-position evaluation needed for the first move's loss is transient: computed when needed and not stored, so the analysis view shows "evaluating..." for it until computed, and after an archive import (ASM-APP-07; SE interpretation Q-SE-2, confirmed). (5) `VARIATION` gains `created_at` and keeps its anchor ply `from_ply`; it has **no `player_id`**, because PLY-7 hides saved variations through the game they belong to (decision 13). A row is one saved line from its anchor ply; how imported nested variations are stored so that the DAT-2 round trip keeps their nesting is open and deferred by the user (OQ-15). (6) Clock columns of an untimed game are null, and the journal clocks are nullable too (§7.6). `journal_version` and `format_version` stay 1.
- **Stored-format batch, second part (Amd 8, APPROVED by Brian, 2026-11-04; Board-pass-store-journal.md; G3).** The full list is SF-01 to SF-22 in §11.4. The parts that change this table: (1) **`custom_start`** is a new column, defined once (D-64, SF-02): it is 1 exactly when the start position differs from the standard start, and a FEN that equals the standard start is a standard start (RULE-8, CR-9.11); "equals" compares **all six FEN fields** (SF-02, Brian's choice, 2026-11-04). `start_fen` is NULL exactly when `custom_start = 0`, and is never the standard FEN. DDL backstops (approved with the batch): `CHECK (custom_start = (start_fen IS NOT NULL))` and `CHECK (custom_start = 0 OR rated = 0)`. The earlier flag-4 sentence that a custom FEN equal to the standard start still made an unrated game is withdrawn. (2) **`content_hash`** has the form `sha256:v1:<64 lowercase hex>` (SF-03; spelling chosen by Brian, 2026-11-04), is NULL exactly while the game is `in_progress` (`CHECK ((status = 'in_progress') = (content_hash IS NULL))`, SF-17; its premise ASM-9 is still open), and is the hash **as of game end** (D-68, SF-04). (3) `white_time_used_ms`, `black_time_used_ms` and `total_active_ms` are written by finalize from the `GameEnd` record, never recomputed from `MOVE.think_ms` by a finalize (D-63, SF-01); the seed of a resumed game is derived from `Move.think_ms` (D-78). (4) `MOVE` and `GAME` clock columns are both NULL or both non-NULL (SF-14, SF-18). (5) `COMPUTER_PROFILE` gets `CHECK (error_permille BETWEEN 0 AND 1000)` and `CHECK (error_permille = 0 OR error_multipv >= 2)` (SF-19). (6) A non-imported game has both `white_id` and `black_id` (SF-20). (7) The hash of a **voided** game is advisory (D-67). `journal_seq` of a voided game is advanced by applying its `Voided` record (D-69, SF-06). `journal_version` and `format_version` still stay 1 because nothing is released (ASM-6).
- **Identity (Amd 7, D-40).** `PLAYER.id` and `GAME.id` are integer row keys **internal to `chess-store`**. Integer ids never cross the store API, the journal, the protocol or an export. Outside the store a Player is a `PlayerId(Uuid)` and a game a `GameId(Uuid)`. The reason is that a journal must survive a restore, which can reuse integer ids.
- **The `MOVE` rows are the source of truth.** The game state is rebuilt by replaying them from `start_fen`. PGN is produced on export, not stored redundantly. Imported PGN headers go in `GAME_TAG`; imported games may have no registered Players, so `white_id`/`black_id` are nullable for them.
- **Imported games** (`status = 'imported'`) have null `matchup`/`mode`, `rated = 0`, null `think_ms`, and no analysis job. Post-game analysis runs on demand. They are never counted in stats or Elo (RULE-7).
- **`APP_SETTING`** holds machine-wide values: the last active Player (PLY-3) and the roster seed (PLY-4). Per-Player settings stay in `PREFERENCES` (UI-1). **`PROCESS_HEARTBEAT`** lists live entry points and their build versions. It is used by D-16 and the exclusive operations in §7.4.
- **`analysis_used`** is set by the gateway the first time it grants `LiveAnalysis` or `WhatIf` for a Training game (DAT-3).
- **Statistics are computed by `chess-domain` over rows (Amd 7, AMD-1, D-44).** The store returns rows filtered only by structural keys (the Player, the reset window `started_at >= reset_at`, the side, a limit). The domain function applies the predicate `self_play = 0`, completed, not a custom start (custom-start games are excluded as self-play is, UI-2) and the empty-state rule (CR-4.15); `StatsService` calls the store, then the domain, and maps to DTOs without a filter or arithmetic of its own. The SQL views `player_stats` and `head_to_head` are not built. History lists include everything, with custom-start games marked "custom start" (UI-2, UI-5). A resignation in a custom-start game is recorded in the game record only. Computer Players' W/L/D use the same functions. FF-5 (§9) measures the statistics path (the store's rows plus `chess-domain::aggregate`) on a 10 000-game fixture against the NFR budget; if it fails, a SQL fast path may be added behind a differential test against the domain function.
- **Two Elo tracks for every Player (Q41, ELO-1/2).** Each Player, human or computer, has a **Strict Elo** and a **Training Elo**, both starting at the same category baseline. A finished game updates the track of its mode (`GAME.mode`) for both sides, unless it is self-play or started from a custom FEN (`rated = 0`). That includes c–c games between different computers. `GAME.*_elo_before/after` hold the values on that game's track. **Ratings, `k` and the history values are whole integers (CR-7.1):** `EloCalc` computes `Δ = round(K × (S − E))` in `f64`, rounding half away from zero, and stores `R + Δ`, so the history sums exactly. Each track has its own rated-game counter (`strict_rated_games`, `training_rated_games`), which sets that track's K: 40 for its first 30 rated games, then 32 (Q78). Because two processes may finish games against the same computer Player at the same time, finalize re-reads both current ratings inside its `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction (§5.4).
- **Rating history is append-only, one series per track.** The initial row for each track is written when the Player is created. An integration test asserts `elo == initial + Σ deltas` per Player and per track (ELO-1). The displayed category of a computer follows its current Strict Elo (§4.6, Q79).
- **One unfinished game per mode, per Player (GAME-4, D-27; user 2026-10-26).** `StartGame` inserts one `UNFINISHED_SLOT` row, keyed by `(player_id, mode)`, for each slot-holding Player in the game:
  - h–h (Strict): each human, or one row for self-play;
  - h–c (Training or Strict): the human only. A human–computer game never uses or checks the computer's slot, so any Player can always play any computer, even one that has an unfinished game;
  - c–c (Strict): each computer, or one row for self-play.
  The slot limit therefore applies to every human Player, and to a computer only in c–c games. A computer with an unfinished c–c game can't start another c–c game until that one ends or is aborted, but humans can still play it. The primary key makes a second unfinished game impossible across all processes, and the error is `UnfinishedGameExists`. Rows are deleted in the finalize, abort, or reset (void) transaction. `StartGame` is therefore a synchronous DB transaction: it fails with `StoreBusy` if the DB is locked, which is acceptable because no clock is running yet.
- **Ending a game early (GAME-4, RULE-2).** An interruption the player didn't choose (crash, engine or power failure, closed tab) is never an abort. The game stays `in_progress` in the journal and DB, and resumes with no Elo or stats effect. A human ends a game early only by **Resign** (confirmed; h–h asks which side). That is a normal result and counts as a loss for Elo on the game's track and for W/L/D. **Abort** exists only for a watcher ending a c–c game: `status = 'aborted'`, no result, no Elo and no stats for either computer. It stays in history and can be analysed (ANA-4). The only other way a game ends without a result is a **void** by a reset (`status = 'voided'`, D-30, below).
- **Player reset (PLY-7, D-26; user 2026-10-26, amended 2026-10-27).** Humans only; computers can't be reset. `ResetPlayerData{confirmed, void_game_ids}` runs one transaction: it sets `reset_at`, restores default `PREFERENCES`, appends a reset point to `RATING_HISTORY` on each track (`game_id` null, `rating_before` = `rating_after` = the current value), and voids every unfinished game of the Player (below). Both current ratings and both rated-game counters are kept, so the next rated game uses the kept K. Nothing is deleted.
  - **Views of the reset Player** show only what came after `reset_at`: statistics, W/L/D, game history, saved variations, and the rating history and chart. That applies in every front-end and in the "stats for any Player" views (UI-5). The ELO-1 check restarts at the reset point: current = reset point + Σ later deltas.
  - **Opponents' views are unchanged** (Q76): the Player's earlier games stay in every opponent's game history, statistics, W/L/D, and head-to-head, computers included, under the Player's current name.
  - The full-data export still contains every game and `reset_at` (DAT-2), so an import shows the same views.
  - **Unfinished games are voided by the reset (D-30; user 2026-10-27, closes OQ-8).** *Superseded rule (baseline v1.0): a reset was refused with `UnfinishedGameExists` while the Player had an unfinished game.* Now the reset is allowed, and the unfinished game does not count against either player:
    - **Confirmation.** The unconfirmed command returns `ConfirmationRequired{action: reset, voids}`, listing each unfinished game (opponent, mode, moves played). Every front-end's dialog says that these games will be voided: no result, no rating change, not counted for either player. The confirmed command echoes the list as `void_game_ids`. If at commit time the Player has an unfinished game that is not in that list, nothing is written and the confirmation is asked again (§5.5).
    - **Inside the reset transaction**, for each unfinished game of the Player: `GAME.status = 'voided'`, `result` null, `termination = 'voided by reset'`, `voided_by` = the reset Player, `ended_at` = the reset time. No Elo change, no `*_rated_games` change, no W/L/D, and no `RATING_HISTORY` row for any side: the reset Player, a human opponent, or a computer. Every `UNFINISHED_SLOT` row that points to the game is deleted, whichever Player holds it (the opponent human in h–h, both rows of a self-play game), and its `GAME_LEASE` row is deleted. An `ANALYSIS_JOB` is enqueued as for an aborted game, so the opponent can analyse it.
    - **Journal and lock.** Before the transaction, `PlayerService` tries the game's journal `flock`. If it gets the lock (the game is suspended), it first replays unapplied journal records, so every completed move is in `MOVE` (NFR-2). After the commit it appends a `Voided` record (carrying the committed `content_hash`, §7.6) with `F_FULLFSYNC`, closes the journal, and moves it to `journal/done/` (the normal lifecycle of a final game, §7.6). The journal is not deleted by the reset. If the game is live in this process, `PlayerService` sends `Void` to its `GameSession` through the `SessionRegistry` after the commit. If it is live in another process, that process's `GameSession` finds out at its next lease heartbeat (its `GAME_LEASE` row is gone, at most 2 s, `ESTIMATE`) or at its next journal apply, whichever comes first. Either way the session cancels any engine search, appends `Voided`, closes its journal, releases its `flock`, emits `GameVoided`, and stops. A move made in that short window is still journalled and applied to `MOVE` (no completed move is lost), but the status stays `voided`. A `GameEnd` in that window is skipped by finalize, because the status is already final (§7.4). *Amd 8 (D-67, OQ-20 option A, Brian's decision at G-1; text approved, 2026-11-04):* the five conditions are in D-67. In short, the hash of a voided game is advisory; for a game live here, `prepare_void` takes the move list from the session's in-memory `Game` or first calls `sync_backlog`; the session live elsewhere builds its `Voided` record from the `content_hash`, `voided_by` and `ended_at` of the `GAME` row; `void_unreadable_game` hashes whatever moves are readable (zero allowed); a `GameEnd` appended before the session sees `RowGone` is superseded by the committed void, and restore skips that pair.
    - **Which record wins.** The journal wins on moves and clocks (§7.6). The void is a store decision made in the reset transaction, so `status = 'voided'` is final and no journal replay changes it.
    - **Visibility (architect's interpretation, D-30, for the user to correct).** The voided game stays in the opponent's game history, marked "voided by reset", with no result, and can be analysed (ANA-4). It is hidden from the reset Player's own views by the `reset_at` rule (Q76), because it started before `reset_at`. It is not a resignation, so the Q60 loss rule does not apply. It is never counted in W/L/D or Elo for anyone: stats aggregates already filter on `status = 'completed'`.

Reset with unfinished games (D-30). Question this diagram answers: how does a reset void a suspended game and a game that is live in another process, without losing a move or touching any rating?

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    actor U as User (front-end A)
    participant PS as PlayerService (process A)
    participant DB as Store thread + chess.db
    participant J as Journal of suspended game G1
    participant S2 as GameSession of G2 (process B)
    participant UI2 as Front-end B
    U->>PS: ResetPlayerData{player, confirmed: false}
    PS->>DB: list unfinished games of the Player
    DB-->>PS: G1 (suspended), G2 (live in process B)
    PS-->>U: ConfirmationRequired{reset, voids: G1, G2}
    U->>PS: ResetPlayerData{player, confirmed: true, void_game_ids: G1, G2}
    PS->>J: flock G1 (free), replay unapplied records
    PS->>DB: BEGIN IMMEDIATE
    alt an unfinished game is missing from void_game_ids
        PS->>DB: ROLLBACK
        PS->>J: unlock G1 (it stays suspended and resumable)
        PS-->>U: ConfirmationRequired with the current list
    else every unfinished game was listed
        PS->>DB: reset_at, default preferences, reset point per track
        PS->>DB: G1 and G2 voided, their slots and leases deleted, analysis jobs queued
        PS->>DB: COMMIT (no Elo, no W/L/D, no rating history for any game)
        PS->>J: append Voided + F_FULLFSYNC, close, move to journal/done, unlock
        PS-->>U: reset done, G1 and G2 voided
        Note over DB,S2: At the next lease heartbeat (2 s, ESTIMATE) or journal apply
        S2->>DB: heartbeat G2 lease
        DB-->>S2: lease row gone, status voided
        S2->>S2: cancel search, append Voided, close journal, unlock
        S2-->>UI2: Event GameVoided{G2, reset player}
    end
```
- **Names (PLY-6, CR-4.1).** A name is trimmed of leading and trailing whitespace and then NFC-normalised, and nothing else is folded. It is at most 128 user-perceived characters (extended grapheme clusters, UAX #29), contains no control character (category Cc) and no line or paragraph separator (Zl, Zp), and is non-empty. It is unique **case-sensitively** against **every** Player, human or computer, active or archived ("Anna" and "anna" are different names), and an archived Player's name stays taken. The `UNIQUE` index is on the stored (trimmed, NFC) value. Case is kept. The failed rule is named in the message (`empty`, `too_long`, `character_not_allowed`, `already_used`, and "by an archived Player" when so). Players are identified by `uuid`, never by name.
- **Void as unreadable (DAT-7, D-35).** `VoidUnreadableGame` is the second way a game becomes `voided`: `status = 'voided'`, `result` null, `termination = 'voided: unreadable'`, `voided_by` null, `ended_at` = the void time. No Elo, counter, W/L/D or rating-history change for anyone, and every `UNFINISHED_SLOT` row of the game is deleted in the same transaction, so the slot is freed. The game stays in history marked "voided: unreadable".
- **Average game length (UI-2).** Reported both in moves (full moves = ⌈plies / 2⌉) and in time (`total_active_ms`), from the same filtered queries.
- **Time accounting (DAT-3):** *(Amd 8, D-63, approved 2026-11-04: the three totals are carried in the `GameEnd` record and finalize writes those values; a finalize does not recompute them from `think_ms` (only the seed of a resumed game uses `think_ms`, D-78), because a resignation or a flag after a long think has no `Move` record and a Training takeback drops plies whose time was already spent.)* `think_ms` per move and the per-side totals come from the session's `TimeSource` while the game is live. Time while the app is closed is never counted, because a suspended game has no running clock. The session checkpoints both clocks into the journal every 1 s while a clock runs, and on every move, pause, and suspend (§7.6). The checkpoints are applied to `GAME.white_clock_ms`/`black_clock_ms`, so a resumed game restarts from the last recorded remaining time, including the side to move's partial thinking time (RULE-5, Q54). After any crash, disconnect, or reset, at most one checkpoint interval (1 s) is lost. *(Amd 8 Addendum D, D-78, ruled under Brian's delegation and ratified by Brian on 2026-11-04, option A, Addendum F:)* that bound is for the clock. The three totals of a game that is suspended and resumed are seeded from the journal's `Move.think_ms` (every `Move` fact, taken-back plies included) and **may lose up to one turn of time at each stop (the unfinished turn of the side to move); they are not required to**; no journal field carries them before `GameEnd`.

### 7.3 Multi-process coordination

The CLI, web server, and desktop app can all be open at once against the same file.

- **Writes** are short transactions, and WAL plus `busy_timeout` serialise them safely across processes (concurrent-access integration test for DAT-1).
- **Live games** are owned by exactly one process: the one holding an exclusive `flock` on the game's journal file (§7.6). The OS releases the lock the instant the owner exits or crashes, so another process can resume immediately (DAT-4; TBD-28 = 0 s: no wait after a crash). The `GAME_LEASE` row (owner kind, pid, heartbeat every 2 s) is only for display: opening the same game elsewhere shows "this game is open in the CLI". A hung but still-running owner keeps the lock, and the message tells the user which process to quit.
- **The background analysis queue** uses the same lease idea, so whichever process is running picks up pending jobs, and jobs survive restarts.
- Other screens (stats, player lists) re-query when they are opened. No cross-process push is needed in v1.

### 7.4 Data ownership and transaction boundaries

One writer per entity, and every invariant is enforced inside one SQLite transaction on the store thread (D-13).

| Entity | System of record (writer) | Readers | Transaction boundary and invariant |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PLAYER`, `PREFERENCES` | `PlayerService` | all services, front-ends via queries | **Register:** `PLAYER` + default `PREFERENCES` + an initial `RATING_HISTORY` row per track (Strict and Training), all-or-nothing (PLY-2, ELO-1). `name` UNIQUE, case-sensitive (PLY-6). **Reset (confirmed, humans only):** `reset_at` + default `PREFERENCES` + one reset point per track in `RATING_HISTORY` + the void of each unfinished game of the Player (`GAME.status = 'voided'`, every `UNFINISHED_SLOT` and `GAME_LEASE` row of the game deleted, analysis job queued, no rating or W/L/D effect for any side), one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. Ratings and K counters untouched (PLY-7, D-26, D-30). For the void, `chess-app` computes each voided game's `content_hash` from the moves held after the journal replay and passes it as `VoidSpec{game_id, content_hash}`, so the transaction sets it (the store has no `chess-rules`; Addendum A, ruling 1). *Amd 8 (D-67, approved 2026-11-04):* that hash is advisory for a voided game (it may be taken from a shorter move list, or from zero moves for an unreadable game), and `prepare_void` must `sync_backlog` or take the move list from the in-memory `Game` of a game live in this process. `PlayerService` is also a second writer of `GAME.status` and `UNFINISHED_SLOT` beside `GameSession`. The `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` serialisation and the "skip if already final" rule keep the two from conflicting (§7.2). `PlayerService` writes only initial and reset points to `RATING_HISTORY`; every game delta is written by finalize. **Rename and archive** apply to human Players only (`Denied{computer_player}`). Archive is refused while the Player has any unfinished game (`PlayerHasUnfinishedGames`) and is refused for the session's own active Player (`Denied{active_player}`). There is no un-archive in v1. **Register, rename, archive, preferences:** one transaction each, all-or-nothing (DAT-7) |
| `APP_SETTING` | `PlayerService` (Amd 7, UF-APP-16: the writer was unnamed) | all services | One transaction per key (`last_active_player`, `roster_seed`) |
| `COMPUTER_PROFILE` | `PlayerService` (via `RosterGen`) | `EngineGateway`, stats | Roster batch of N Players (1 to 25), with `roster_indexes()` read first and the batch inserted in one transaction, `UNIQUE(roster_seed, roster_index)` arbitrating a race (§4.6, Addendum A), together with each computer Player's two baseline rating rows. Computer Players are never renamed, archived, deleted or reset. Profile rows are never updated after insert: engine parameters are fixed (PLY-4, Q41) |
| `GAME` (header), `MOVE`, `UNFINISHED_SLOT` | `GameSession` (live), `GameSetupService` (start), `ImportExportService` (imported) | stats, analysis, export | **Start (Amd 7, AMD-4, UR-04):** in one synchronous store transaction, claim one `UNFINISHED_SLOT` per slot-holding Player (every human; computers only in c–c, D-27; the primary key is the arbiter) and insert the `GAME` row (GAME-4). Then create the journal with its `Start` record, then acknowledge. A crash between the two leaves an `in_progress` row with no journal; resume treats that as a game with zero moves replayed from `start_fen`, and no new state exists for it. **Move:** journal record + `F_FULLFSYNC` first (the durability point). Then `MOVE` row + `GAME` clock + `journal_seq`, one transaction per applied batch (NFR-2, §7.6). **Takeback:** a journal record, then delete the trailing `MOVE` rows in one transaction |
| `GAME` result, `RATING_HISTORY`, `PLAYER.*_elo` / `*_rated_games`, `ANALYSIS_JOB` | `GameSession` finalize | stats | **Finalize** (completed, including resignation): `GameEnd` journal record first. Then one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction: re-read both Players' ratings on the game's track (Training or Strict), compute result + Elo deltas for both sides on that track + history rows, write the three time totals taken from the `GameEnd` record (Amd 8, D-63: never recomputed), delete the `UNFINISHED_SLOT` rows, enqueue the analysis job and set `content_hash` (ELO-1/2, §5.4, D-42). *Amd 8 (D-72, confirmed by Brian, 2026-11-04):* when several pending `GameEnd` journals are finalised after a crash, they run ascending by `ended_at`, ties by `game_uuid`, as in a restore. **c–c abort:** status, slot deletion, job and `content_hash`, with no ratings. Both are idempotent on replay: skipped if the status is already final (`completed`, `aborted`, or `voided`) |
| `MOVE_EVAL`, `ANALYSIS_JOB` state | `AnalysisWorker` | `AnalysisService`, stats | One upsert per ply. A partial run resumes from the last ply |
| `VARIATION` | `AnalysisService` | export | Per save or delete. A save from the final position of the game is refused (§5.1). `created_at` is set at save |
| `GAME_TAG`, imported `GAME`/`MOVE` | `ImportExportService` | export, history | One transaction per imported game |
| Journal files (`journal/<game_uuid>.jnl`) | the `GameSession` holding the file's `flock` | recovery in any process | Append-only, one writer. Truncated only after its records are applied and the game is final (§7.6) |
| `GAME_LEASE`, `PROCESS_HEARTBEAT` | `SessionRegistry`, `Core` startup | all processes | Display and liveness only. Compare-and-set on `heartbeat_at` |
| Whole database (`ImportData`, `RestoreBackup`, migrations) | `ImportExportService` / `chess-store` | — | **Exclusive:** refused with `Busy` if `PROCESS_HEARTBEAT` shows another live process. Pending journals are replayed first. Runs under `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE` after a `VACUUM INTO` backup. Data import merges (D-17, see below) |

**Data import merges (D-17, decided by Q51).**
1. `PreviewDataImport` matches Players by `uuid` (computers by `(roster_seed, roster_index)`) and games by `uuid`. A different Player with an identical name is also a conflict, because names are unique. It returns `ImportConflicts`.
2. For each conflict the user picks **Replace**: the imported record, with its rating history, replaces the local one. Or **Keep both**: the imported record gets a new `uuid` and a suffixed name such as "Brian (imported 2)", so it stays a distinct identity. Non-conflicting records are added.
3. `ApplyDataImport{plan, confirmed}` is confirmed after the preview (UI-12) and writes everything in one transaction, then re-checks the Elo invariant per Player.
4. If any step fails, nothing is written and the backup is untouched.
5. **Unfinished games** in the archive are restored as unfinished (with their `UNFINISHED_SLOT` rows and the clocks from `games.json`, or the last `[%clk]`) unless that would give a Player a second unfinished game in a mode, or a computer a second c–c game. In that case the game is imported **review-only with no result**, and the preview lists it. A game that is live in a front-end of the importing process is never replaced: it is reported and the stored game is kept.
6. **PGN import** (`ImportPgn`) is not a merge: duplicates raise the Skip (default) or Keep both prompt of D-34, and nothing is ever replaced.

Multi-game PGN files are supported by `ImportPgn` (IF-2).

A suspended game is `in_progress` with no journal lock held, so the stored status needs no separate `suspended` value.

Cross-process consistency is eventual only for screens that are open in another process: they re-query when opened (§7.3). Live game state is never shared, because the lease gives each live game one owner.

### 7.5 One version everywhere (D-16; one-way for data, accepted by the user)

The CLI and the desktop app must never be different versions. They ship and update together as one bundle (§8.2), so v1 has **no** machinery for mixed versions working together. It only detects a mismatch and refuses, with a message.
- **One build version** is compiled into the bundle and covers `chess`, `Chess.app`, the GUI bundle, the journal format, and the schema version.
- **Against the database.** On open, a `user_version` newer than the build's schema returns `SchemaTooNew`, writes nothing, and says "this data was written by a newer Chess. Update the app." An older `user_version` is a normal upgrade: the build migrates after a `VACUUM INTO` backup (DAT-6), but only when no other process is live. Before migrating, it replays all pending journals.
- **Against running processes.** Every process records its build version in `PROCESS_HEARTBEAT`. If a live process of a different version is found, which can only happen in the middle of an upgrade, startup is refused with "Chess version X is still running (CLI / web server / desktop). Quit it first."
- **Against a running server or a stale tab.** A second `chess serve` of another version is refused the same way. A browser tab from another build gets `ProtocolMismatch` and reloads (§5.6).
- **Journals.** The header's `journal_version` must equal the build's. A different value is refused with the same message.
- **One-way for data (accepted by the user, 2026-10-26).** Once a newer build has migrated `chess.db`, no older build can open it. The only way back is the pre-migration backup `chess.db.bak-v<N>`, with pending journals replayed on top, or a full-data export and import (§11.3).
- Tests: an older build opens a newer DB (`SchemaTooNew`); a heartbeat from another version is refused; migration replays journals first (DAT-1).

### 7.6 Move journal: the local cache that always wins (Q54, D-23)

The user's rule (Q54) has three parts:
- every move is cached locally and written to the database when the database is available;
- after any disconnect, hard reset, close, or crash, the clock is restored to the last recorded clock time;
- if the cache and the database disagree, the local cache wins.

**Files.** One append-only file per live game, `<data dir>/journal/<game_uuid>.jnl`. It starts with a header (`journal_version`, `game_uuid`, `store.id`). Each record is `{seq, kind, payload, crc32}`, length-prefixed. The kinds and payloads are:
- *Amended 2026-10-31 (AMD-4, D-41; G3, decided before any store code; `journal_version` stays 1 because nothing is released). Amended again the same day by Addendum A (rulings 1 and 2).* The record body is `seq`, `kind`, `wall_ms` (the wall time of the change, taken from the `Stamp`) and the JSON payload. The kinds are `Start`, `Move`, `Takeback`, `Clock`, `DrawOffer`, `DrawDeclined`, **`DrawDropped`** (new: the offer was dropped, by an engine failure or a suspend), `Pause`, `Resume`, `Suspend`, **`SuspendResume`** (new: a suspended game was resumed, so replay needs no implicit rule), `GameEnd`, `Abort` (c-c only) and `Voided` (written after the reset transaction commits, D-30, §7.2, or after `VoidUnreadableGame`, DAT-7). `DrawOffer` is followed by a `Clock` record with both clocks stopped (RULE-2). Clocks (`white_ms`, `black_ms`) are **nullable** in `Move` and `Clock`, for an untimed game. Every identifier is a UUID (D-40), and a side is the store's `StoredSide` (`white` or `black`), converted by the codec.

**One fact, one record kind (AMD-4, D-41; table rewritten by Addendum A).** `JournalCodec` in `chess-app` (§5.7) converts between the domain's `Fact` (the vocabulary of state changes, owned by `chess-domain`) and the journal record (the format, owned by `chess-store`). The mapping is one to one. **This table is authoritative** over the store design 3.11.1, the domain design 3.11.1 and DD-APP-18, and it uses the domain's variant names (`Ended`, `Aborted`; FIND-JR-004). Column 3 lists the `Fact` fields, each of which is written into the record. Column 4 lists the payload fields that the codec adds from the `RecordCtx` or an assembler and that `record_to_fact` ignores. **That is why the FF-3 round trip holds by construction: no `Fact` field is missing from a record, and no record field that is not in the `Fact` is needed to rebuild it.** A unit test asserts, per kind, that the payload field names equal columns 3 and 4.

| Domain `Fact` | Journal record | Payload fields taken from the `Fact` | Payload fields added by the codec (not in the `Fact`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Start{setup, started_at}` | `Start` | `game` (= `setup.game_id`), `white`, `black` (the two `Seat` players), `white_kind`, `black_kind` (`human` or `computer`), `matchup`, `mode`, `custom_start` (bool; defined once, D-64), `start_fen` (null exactly for the standard start, SF-02), `clock_spec` (null when untimed; text `base+inc` in seconds, SF-11). There is no `started_at` field: `started_at` is the record's `wall_ms` (SF-16, decided by Brian, 2026-11-04; the same rule as `Move.played_at`) | `rated`, `self_play` (from the `Start` assembler; frozen, authoritative for the restored `GAME` row, never recomputed) |
| `Move{ply, mv, think_ms, clocks_after, played_at}` | `Move` | `ply`, `uci` (= `mv.to_uci()`), `think_ms`, `white_ms`, `black_ms` (both null when untimed). `played_at` is the record's `wall_ms` | `san`, `fen_after` (from the `RecordCtx`; replay recomputes the SAN and compares; *Amd 8, D-72:* a SAN mismatch with a legal `uci` is not an error: the `uci` is trusted, `san` and `fen_after` are recomputed and the event is logged; *Addendum D, D-79:* the codec hands the two values to `Game::replay` as a `MoveCheck` beside the unchanged `Fact`, and a `fen_after` difference is always an error) |
| `Takeback{to_ply}` | `Takeback` | `to_ply` | none |
| `Clock{clocks, running}` | `Clock` | `white_ms`, `black_ms` (both null when untimed), `running_side` (null when stopped) | none |
| `DrawOffer{by}`, `DrawDeclined{by}` | `DrawOffer`, `DrawDeclined` | `by` (side) | none |
| `DrawDropped{by, cause}` | `DrawDropped` | `by` (the side whose offer was dropped), `cause`: `engine_failure` or `suspend` | none |
| `Pause`, `PlayResume`, `SuspendResume` | `Pause`, `Resume`, `SuspendResume` | none: the payload is `{}` | none |
| `Suspend{cause}` | `Suspend` | `cause`: `detach`, `shutdown` or `recovered` (a D-14 detach, a server stop, a D-58 crash recovery). *Amd 8, SF-21, decided by Brian, 2026-11-04 (option A); `Fact::Suspend` and `decide(Suspend)` carry it, so the domain and the app change (G2, approved)* | none |
| `Ended{outcome}` (`Outcome{result, reason}`) | `GameEnd` | `result`, `reason` (stable snake_case codes, SF-15, decided by Brian, 2026-11-04; the codec maps them 1:1 to the unchanged `GAME.termination` strings) | `ended_at`, `eco`, `opening_name`, `analysis_used`, **`content_hash`**, and, *Amd 8 (D-63, SF-01)*, **`white_time_used_ms`, `black_time_used_ms`, `total_active_ms`** (`FinalizeInputs`, from `assemble_game_end`) |
| `Aborted` | `Abort` | none | `ended_at`, **`content_hash`** |
| `Voided{reason, reset_player}` | `Voided` | `reason` (`VoidReason`: `reset` or `unreadable`, SF-10), `reset_player` (UUID or null) | **`content_hash`** (advisory, D-67) and, *Amd 8 (D-69, SF-05)*, **`ended_at`** |

**`content_hash` (ruling 1, D-42).** It is `"sha256:" + hex(...)`, never null in `GameEnd`, `Abort` and `Voided`, because the game is terminal. The store cannot compute it (it has no `chess-rules`), and a restore re-runs the finalize, abort or void from the journal, so the value must be in the record. `chess-app` computes it before the store transaction and passes it in. For a reset, the same value is in the `VoidSpec` of each voided game (§7.4), and the `Voided` journal record carries the hash that was committed to the `GAME` row. The earlier journal payload note "the finalize inputs `ended_at`, `eco`, `opening_name` and `analysis_used`" is now the five-field list above. *Amd 8 (APPROVED by Brian, 2026-11-04; D-63, D-68, D-69):* the value has the form `sha256:v1:<hex>` (SF-03). It is the hash of the game **as of game end**: it uses the names and the rule-owned tags as they were then, so FF-4 holds only when no Player is renamed between export and re-import (SF-04). For a **voided** game it is advisory and FF-4 and the second property of DD-APP-18 exclude `Voided` (D-67). *Addendum F (OQ-27, decided by Brian, G3, 2026-11-04, option A):* `canonical_form` leaves out `SetUp` and `FEN` when the `FEN` equals the standard start in all six fields (the SF-02 comparison; consistent with CR-9.11 (2)), so the hash of a game imported with the standard `FEN` tag equals the hash of its own export; any other `FEN` stays in the canonical form. `v1` is defined with this rule. The `GameEnd` payload also carries the three finalize time totals (SF-01) and the `Voided` payload carries `ended_at` (SF-05). Numeric kind codes, the checksum, the record size limit and the text forms are fixed in SF-07 to SF-16 (§11.4).

**Flags for the Review Board (OQ-16, OQ-22).** These are fields or choices that the architecture settles but that no requirement or earlier text justifies on its own.
1. *Not carried.* The store design lists `resigning_side?` and "times" in `GameEnd`. The resigning side is the loser of `result` when `reason` is resignation, in every matchup, so the field is redundant. "Times" names no consumer: per-move times are in `Move`. Both are dropped. `setup JSON as sent` in `Start` is dropped too, because it would give two truths beside the flat fields. *Amd 8 (D-63, approved, 2026-11-04; Board BP-001; Brian accepted at G-1):* the drop of "times" is **rejected**: the three time totals return to `GameEnd` (SF-01), because they cannot be derived from `Move.think_ms`. The drop of `resigning_side?` and of `setup JSON` stands.
2. *`Pause`, `Resume`, `Suspend`, `SuspendResume` had an empty payload (Amd 8: `Suspend` now carries `cause`, see the table).* The store design says "small" and names no field, and replay and finalize need none. The clocks at the instant are in the `Clock` fact that follows each of them (state machine T11, T14). The change time is the record's `wall_ms`. If a cause for `Suspend` (detach, shutdown, lease loss) is wanted for diagnostics, it is a new field and a decision. *Amd 8: accepted by the Board; Brian decided on 2026-11-04 that `Suspend` gets a `cause` now (SF-21, option A). The other three kinds stay empty.*
3. *`DrawDropped.cause` has no replay consumer.* It exists for the audit trail and the ENG-4 message. `by` is also derivable (it is the pending offerer), so replay checks it against the pending offer instead of trusting it. *Amd 8: accepted; a mismatch is logged and replay continues (SF-22).*
4. *`Start` additions.* `white_kind`, `black_kind` and `custom_start` are not in the store design's list. Without them `record_to_fact` cannot rebuild `Seat.kind` or `StartPosition`, because `matchup` does not say which side of an h-c game is the computer, and a custom FEN equal to the standard start still makes an unrated game. They are added here, not requested by a ruling. *Amd 8 (D-64): `white_kind` and `black_kind` accepted. `custom_start` is changed: it is defined once, a start equal to the standard start is standard, and `GAME` gets the column (SF-02).*
5. *`Start.rated` and `Start.self_play` are redundant with the setup.* They are kept as history frozen at the start, so that a restore creates the `GAME` row without re-running a rule that a later build may have changed. `record_to_fact` ignores them. *Amd 8 (accepted, folded into D-64): the frozen value is authoritative for the restored `GAME` row and replay never recomputes it.*
6. *`ended_at` equals the record's `wall_ms` on the normal path.* It is kept because restore orders games by it (Restore, step 4), and a `Voided` appended by crash recovery carries a later `wall_ms`. `Voided` has no `ended_at`, so the three terminal records are not symmetric. The Board should decide whether `Voided` gets one. *Amd 8 (D-69): changed. `Voided` gets `ended_at` (SF-05).*
7. *Hash of a game voided while live elsewhere.* The reset computes the hash from the moves the store holds at commit. A move that the other process journalled but had not yet applied is added afterwards (the journal wins on moves), so the stored hash can belong to a shorter move list (OQ-20). *Amd 8 (D-67): accepted with five conditions; the hash of a voided game is advisory.*

- **No implicit-resume fallback.** The domain's earlier rule that any non-terminal fact after `Suspend` resumes the game is removed, so replay has one interpretation. A `Move` in `Paused` is a stepped move, stated once in the domain replay rules; there is no `Step` kind.
- **Fields closed by construction.** `fen_after` and `wall_ms` come from the post-state and stamp that `Game::decide` already returns, so the domain `Fact::Move` does not change.
- **Start order.** The slot claim and the `GAME` row come first, in one store transaction; then the journal is created with `Start`; then the start is acknowledged (§7.4). *Addendum E, D-84 (desk review F-02):* **one stamp.** The `Stamp` that the session takes once at the start is the `started_at` of `Fact::Start` and therefore the `wall_ms` of the `Start` record (SF-16, approved), **and it is the value of `GAME.started_at`**: the row and the record carry the same value, not two readings of the clock a few milliseconds apart. A restore of a journal with no later change thus gives the same `started_at`, and the same UTC `Date` of the hash (SF-04), as the live row; a game started just before UTC midnight does not change its `Date`. *Mechanism (decided by Brian Prascher, 2026-11-04, G2, OQ-26 option A; Addendum F):* the row receives the stamp through a `started_at: i64` field of the store's `NewGame`. The app takes the `Stamp` once, before `start_game`, and passes its `WallMs` in `NewGame.started_at`; the store writes it unchanged to `GAME.started_at`, reads no clock for it, and the session builds `Fact::Start` from the same `Stamp`. The field crosses the `chess-store`/`chess-app` contract (§5.7); it is not a protocol type. **A row with no journal** (a crash between the two steps): the first resume creates the journal and writes, as record 1, a `Start` whose `wall_ms` is `GAME.started_at` read from the row and whose fields come from the row (the frozen `rated`, `self_play` and `custom_start`, the players, matchup, mode, `start_fen`, clock), and then continues as for a game with zero moves; the row and the journal agree from the first record. *Unacknowledged start (D-88, desk review F-13):* that row holds a slot for a start that was never acknowledged. It is **not voided automatically**: `StartGame` is idempotent by `game_uuid` (a retry returns it), a different `game_uuid` meets the slot rule, and the Player sees the game in the resume list with zero moves and may play or leave it. Voiding it would use the unreadable-game path and report damage that does not exist. The cost is one visible zero-move game after a crash inside a window of a few milliseconds `ESTIMATE`; no move exists, so NFR-2 is untouched.
- **Conformance.** A property test over every `Fact` variant checks `record_to_fact(fact_to_record(f, ctx)) == f` (FF-3), and a second property checks, for `GameEnd`, `Abort` and `Voided`, that the finalize inputs (for `GameEnd` including the three time totals), the `content_hash` and, for `Voided`, the `ended_at` that an assembler writes are the ones the decoder reads back. *Addendum E, JR5-002 and F-10 (one reading, no decision changes):* (i) **the round trip has a precondition**: for `Start` and `Move` the record carries no copy of the stamp (SF-16), so the property holds when the context's `wall_ms` equals the fact's `started_at` or `played_at`; the generator derives the context from the fact. (ii) **For `Voided` the second property does not compare the hash with a recomputation of the moves** (D-67 (i): the hash is advisory); it still checks that the decoder reads back the value that was written, and `ended_at`. (iii) A third test finalizes a game live and from its `GameEnd` record alone and compares all `GAME` columns (UT-APP-26); a fourth: a restore of a journal with no later change gives the same `started_at` as the live row (D-84).


**Write path.**
1. The `GameSession` holds an exclusive `flock` on its journal for as long as it owns the game. That lock *is* the lease (§7.3).
2. For each state change it appends the record and calls `fcntl(F_FULLFSYNC)`. Only then is the event broadcast, the move acknowledged, and the clock switched.
3. The record is passed to the store thread. In one transaction, the store thread writes the corresponding rows and `GAME.journal_seq = seq`.
4. If the DB is busy or locked (another process's write, an import, a migration) or unavailable, the store thread retries with backoff while play continues. The backlog is reported with `StoreBacklog`.

**After a failed or ambiguous append (Addendum E, D-82; desk review F-01; G1, inside `chess-store`, no format change).** The writer keeps `acked_len` (the file length after the last record whose `F_FULLFSYNC` returned) and the next `seq`. An append is one blocking task that runs to completion even if its future is dropped: write the record at `acked_len`, `F_FULLFSYNC`, and on success advance `acked_len` and `seq`. If the write or the sync fails, **the same task cleans up before it returns**: `ftruncate` to `acked_len`, then `F_FULLFSYNC`. If the cleanup succeeds, the file is exactly as it was before the append, `seq` is **not** consumed (it is reused by the next record, which is safe because the failed record is gone), the writer stays usable and the append returns `WriteFailed{cause}`. If the cleanup also fails, **or** the future was dropped before the task reported its outcome (the caller cannot know whether the record is durable), the writer is **poisoned**: every later append returns `WriteFailed` with the cause `Poisoned` without touching the file, and the only exit is to release the lock (`Detach`, `Shutdown`, process exit) and resume, where the new owner's scan applies the torn-tail rule and truncates to the last intact record. A poisoned writer never writes again, so a `seq` is never used twice in one file and a failed record is never followed by another record: the scan cannot meet a damaged record followed by intact records from this cause. Consequences: (i) a record whose append returned an error and whose cleanup failed may still be intact on disk; the resume applies it (journal wins), so a move that the player was told had failed can appear after the resume. This is accepted: the only alternative is to discard intact records, which could discard an acknowledged one; the resume reports the recovered moves as usual. (ii) D-65 part 2 "discards the rest" means the session discards those facts from its state; the file holds them only in case (i). (iii) The terminal re-check of D-65 part 1 and D-80 ("run at once with the same write path") appends only through a writer that is not poisoned. If the failed step poisoned the writer, the re-check cannot append, the session stays degraded (D-80) and the re-check runs at the resume. (iv) **A poisoned writer puts the session into the degraded state of D-80 even when the failed step had a single record**, because every later command would be refused anyway; D-80's command set is unchanged, and this is one more way to enter the state. (v) Limit, not a defect (desk review I-1): bit rot of the last acknowledged record while the store is backlogged cannot be told from a torn write and is lost silently; the design covers torn writes, not media faults. Failpoints `journal.partial_write` and `journal.fsync_error` and the tests UT-ST-087, CT-ST-087 and UT-APP-35 verify the rule.

**Recovery (resume, takeover, startup).** Any process that acquires a game's `flock` first replays the journal:
- records with `seq > GAME.journal_seq` are applied in order;
- if a DB row differs from the journal for the same ply, for example after `RestoreBackup` brought back an older DB, the DB rows from that ply on are replaced by the journal's (**the cache wins**);
- the clock is restored from the latest `Move` or `Clock` record;
- a torn final record (bad CRC or short length) is dropped **silently**. It was never acknowledged, so no completed move is lost, and the user is told nothing. *Amd 8 (D-66, approved 2026-11-04, Board BP-004):* this holds only while the store does not hold a later record. If `GAME.journal_seq` is greater than the last intact `seq` of the journal, the store applied a record that the journal no longer proves (an acknowledged tail damaged or lost after the fact), so the case is **damage, not a torn tail**: reconcile applies the longer-run rule below, never deletes a stored `MOVE` row beyond the journal's end, and reports `JournalUnreadable{game_id, recovered_moves}` to the user;
- **a damaged record in the middle** (bad CRC with intact records after it, or an unreadable header) is not a torn tail. The journal is read up to the first damaged record, and the number of moves in that **intact run** is compared with the moves held by the store (`MOVE` rows, which are valid by replay). The game resumes from the **longer** of the two runs when they agree on their common plies. If they disagree at some ply and the journal is intact up to there, the journal wins (the cache wins, Q54). The core returns `JournalUnreadable{game_id, recovered_moves}` and the client tells the user how many moves were recovered. The damaged journal file is kept aside as `journal/damaged/` and never deleted. Clocks come from the last intact `Move` or `Clock` record, or from the `GAME` checkpoint when that is later. *Addendum E, D-83 (desk review F-03; G1):* **the game continues on a rebased journal**, defined here so that the architecture and the store design say the same thing. (1) The new file `<game_uuid>.jnl.rebase` (mode `0600`) holds the header (same `journal_version`, `game_uuid`, `store.id`), then the records of the intact run **copied byte for byte** (same `seq`, `wall_ms` and payload: nothing is renumbered or re-timed, so the `Start` keeps its stamp and every `Takeback`, `DrawOffer`, `Pause` and taken-back `Move` that the intact run holds stays), then, only when the store holds plies beyond the intact run that agree with it on the common plies, **synthesized `Move` records** from the `MOVE` rows (`seq` continuing from the last intact `seq`; `wall_ms` = the row's `played_at`, or the previous record's `wall_ms` when it is NULL; `san`, `fen_after`, `think_ms` and clocks from the row, which holds them `[OBS store DDL]`), and one closing `Clock` (the reconciled clocks, `running_side` null, `wall_ms` of the previous record, never `now`). The file and the directory are synced. **If the intact run holds no `Start` record** (the header or record 1 is the damaged part, so the run is empty), the rebase writes a `Start` as record 1, synthesized from the `GAME` row exactly as for a row with no journal (`wall_ms = GAME.started_at`, fields from the row, D-84), and numbers the synthesized `Move` records from `seq` 2 on, one per stored ply in force (the `MOVE` rows are valid by replay, so the agreement test is vacuous); a replay of the rebased file can then build the game, and the Player is not offered `VoidUnreadableGame` for a recoverable game (RR-A-1 (a), Addendum F). **A game whose `GAME.status` is final or voided is not rebased** (the case where the lost tail held the `GameEnd`, `Abort` or `Voided` and the store applied it): nothing will be appended to it. Its damaged file is moved to `journal/damaged/<game_uuid>.<k>.jnl` by the crash-safe rename of (3), no new file takes its place, the `GAME` row and its `MOVE` rows are not changed, and `JournalUnreadable` is reported as in the other damage cases (RR-A-1 (b)). (2) One store transaction applies the reconcile plan and sets `GAME.journal_seq` to the new file's last `seq` (which can be lower than the old value when the intact run was short: the old file is no longer the journal, so D-66 does not compare against it). (3) The damaged file is renamed to `journal/damaged/<game_uuid>.<k>.jnl` (`k` the first unused integer), then the new file is renamed to `<game_uuid>.jnl`; **both renames have no-replace semantics** (they fail when the target exists, so neither can overwrite a file; the exact call is the store design's choice, for example `renamex_np` with `RENAME_EXCL` `[UNV]`); **after the first rename both directories are synced, `journal/` and `journal/damaged/`**, because the first rename crosses them and a power loss must not leave the move recorded in one directory only, where the second rename could then replace the damaged file (RR-A-5); after the second rename `journal/` is synced.
