Whether the SPRT code should sit behind an optional `calibration` Cargo feature was OQ-24; **Brian ruled on 2026-11-04: no feature in v1, the calibration code stays as designed** (D-77) | Keep "refuses the default data directory" only: a user whose store is elsewhere (`CHESS_DATA_DIR`, `--data-dir`) is unprotected and first launch creates 25 computer Players in whatever store it opens | **APPROVED 2026-11-04** (Board-recommended; confirmed by Brian on 2026-11-04 as a minor item). Tier **G1** (inside `chess-app`); the new public items were already recorded G2 under D-57 |
| D-74 | Where `EngineGateway` gets the game state it policy-checks (OQ-13; UF-APP-16; app SDD OQ-APP-007; Amd 8 Addendum A) | `GameContextProvider`, a trait private to `chess-app` (`seams.rs`, `pub(crate)`, not in the curated `pub use` list): `context(game_id) -> Result<GameContext, ContextError>`. Direction: gateway depends on the trait, `SessionRegistry` implements it (dependency inversion), so the gateway never names the registry or a session and §4.1 has no cycle (`SessionRegistry -> GameSession -> EngineGateway` exists). **Live here:** the registry returns the `GameContext` that the `GameSession` last published after a completed handler (a read-only snapshot behind a `tokio::sync::watch` or a short `RwLock`; no message to the actor, so a request raised by the session itself, such as `ComputerMove`, cannot wait on its own mailbox). **Not live here:** the context is built from the `GAME` row through the store (`get_game`: mode, matchup, status, the two kinds); a game that is `in_progress` in the store and not live in this process (suspended, or live in another process) is the domain's existing suspended state, and a final status maps to the matching terminal state. **Fail closed:** the gateway never takes game state from its caller; if no context can be produced (unknown game, store error) it returns `Denied` or `Internal` and the engine is not touched. Only `FreeAnalysis` and `Calibration`, which name no stored game, skip the provider. A snapshot read by another task (`AnalysisService`) can be one handler behind; that is safe because the mode and the in-progress status do not change while a game is live, and the Strict rule denies live analysis on that pair alone. This ruling needs no change to `GameContext` in `chess-domain`; if the app design finds that a store-built context needs a new state value, that is a separate G2 change and is **not decided here** | (b) The caller passes the `GameContext` in the request: no lookup, but ANA-1 then depends on every caller (`AnalysisService`, `GameSession`, `AnalysisWorker`) building it correctly, two places to forget, and it contradicts §4.5. (c) The gateway reads the store only: `GAME.status` and `mode` suffice for the Strict rule, but the row lags the live session (store backlog up to 30 s, §4.8) and holds no turn, pending-offer or engine state, which `ComputerMove`, `ComputerDrawDecision` and Training `LiveAnalysis` need. (d) The gateway holds `SessionRegistry`: a cycle, registry to session to gateway to registry. (e) The gateway asks the session by message: a session's own request would wait for its own mailbox. (f) Defer: leaves the gateway contract open for the app code | **CONFIRMED by Brian Prascher on 2026-11-04 as a G2 decision** (beads chess-a0r.1.19; Addendum D). It was proposed the same day (Amendment 8, Addendum A; architect). Tier **G2**, Brian's ruling: the architect's own reading of SWE-001 section 3 had been G1 (one crate, no §3.2 edge, no §5.7 contract, no protocol change), and the work order had treated a new §4.1 edge as G2. The ruling text in the previous column is unchanged. No review other than Brian's confirmation has been held |
| D-75 | Where the ECO table and `eco::lookup` live (readiness condition C-1, FIND-001; amends D-20; Amd 8 Addendum A) | The generated table and the position lookup `eco::lookup(&PositionKey) -> Option<EcoEntry{code, name}>` live in **`chess-rules`**, with the generator (a `build.rs`) reading `third_party/eco/`. The **"deepest named position" rule and everything about a game** (`EcoTable::observe` and `classify`, the tag normalisation, the import mismatch classification) stay in **`chess-domain`**, which calls `rules::eco::lookup`. This is what both SDDs already say (SDD-chess-rules IFP-RUL-6, DD-RUL-013, DR-RUL-008; SDD-chess-domain 3.11.9, DD-DOM-015) `[OBS]`. The D-20 wording "in `chess-domain`", §3.1, the §3.3 rows, §5.7 and §8.1 are corrected to match. `eco::lookup` becomes a provided interface of `chess-rules` in §3.3 and §5.7 (new key types `PositionKey` and `EcoEntry`). **The generator needs `shakmaty` as a `[build-dependencies]` entry of `chess-rules`:** a build script cannot call the crate it builds, so the SDD sentence that `build.rs` replays each line "with `parse_move`" cannot be implemented as written `[OBS dependency-lookup-step1.md F7]`. That is the same crate and version already admitted (D-3). It adds **no crate to the workspace and no edge to §3.2**. The key derivation (the EPD of a position) is written once, as a module that both the build script and `PositionKey::to_epd` include, and UT-RUL-023 and UT-RUL-030 test through the public lookup, so a mismatch between the two would fail a test `[INF]`. A separate generator crate or `xtask` is not needed and would add a workspace member (G2) | (b) Table and lookup in `chess-domain`, with a `build.rs` there that has a **build-dependency on `chess-rules`** (the existing edge in its build kind) and calls the same `parse_move` and `PositionKey`: one parse path, no provided interface added to `chess-rules`, no G2 `[INF]` (not compiled). Viable and **not chosen**: both SDDs, their tests (UT-RUL-023, UT-RUL-030, CT-RUL-003, UT-DOM-070 to 072) and the readiness assumption ASM-RR-1 already place the table in `chess-rules`; moving it rewrites those entities; and a position-only fact belongs with the crate that owns positions. The cost of the chosen option is the duplicated key derivation, handled by the shared module. The argument in the work order that a generator in `chess-domain` would need `shakmaty` directly (and so break the domain-to-rules-only edge) does not hold, because that build script could depend on `chess-rules` instead; it is not the reason for this ruling. (c) A separate generator crate or `xtask` writing a checked-in file: a new workspace member, G2. (d) Parse the TSV at start-up: run-time I/O and cost, against NFR-1 | **DECIDED by the architect 2026-11-04 under Brian Prascher's delegation ("do what you think is best", relayed by the program manager).** Tier **G2** (adds a provided interface to `chess-rules`, used across the `chess-domain -> chess-rules` edge): **G2, approved by Brian's delegation to the architect, 2026-11-04.** Brian gave no separate approval text for this record, and he may overrule it. Not reviewed by anyone but its author |
| D-76 | Toolchain and test-tool policy: stable Rust only; coverage tool admitted; fuzzing tools not admitted (dependency-lookup F1, F4; Amd 8 Addendum B) | (1) **No nightly Rust toolchain, ever.** The project builds, tests and releases on the stable toolchain pinned in §3.4 on every machine it is built on, and no document, CI job or script may require nightly (Brian: the project deploys on many machines and has no nightly capability). (2) **`cargo-llvm-cov` 0.9.1 is admitted** as the coverage tool of the 90 % line-coverage gate; it needs only the stable `llvm-tools` component and is not linked into the product. (3) **`cargo-fuzz`, `libfuzzer-sys` and `arbitrary` are not admitted** (cargo-fuzz needs a nightly compiler; the other two exist only to serve it). The `cargo-fuzz` entry of the §3.4 table is removed. (4) **NFR-3 illegal-state verification uses stable-only means:** `proptest` property tests (random legal playouts to a bounded depth, arbitrary move text, FEN and PGN into the parsers, random and malformed frames into the protocol decoder), the 1000-game computer-versus-computer soak and the perft tables. "Fuzz" in test-strategy and §9 names these random-input tests, run by `cargo test` on stable. (5) "Nightly" in CI job names (the calibration and soak runs) means scheduled overnight on the stable toolchain | (a) A pinned nightly for a `fuzz/` crate only (the lookup's proposal P7): coverage-guided fuzzing reaches deeper parser states, but every machine that runs it needs a nightly, which the project cannot assume. (b) Admit `cargo-fuzz` and run it only on a CI machine that has nightly: also a requirement for nightly, and no such CI is ruled in. (c) Leave coverage unspecified: the 90 % gate would have no tool. **Accepted loss:** no coverage-guided fuzzing. Random generation is weaker at reaching narrow, deep input structure `[INF]`, so the evidence for NFR-3 is property tests and soak, not a fuzz corpus; the SDD verification wording must say so | **APPROVED 2026-11-04** (Brian Prascher, G2; recorded in beads chess-a0r.1.14). Tier **G2**. FOR BRIAN (G2): approved 2026-11-04 |
| D-77 | OQ-24 and OQ-25: calibration feature and the store-work block (Board BP-015; the Board's verdict; Amd 8 Addendum C) | (1) **No `calibration` Cargo feature in v1**: the calibration code stays as designed in `chess-app` (D-57, D-73), guarded from the wire (UT-APP-05) and by the directory guard of D-73. (2) **No `chess-store` code of any kind starts before OQ-16 closes** (the full block, as the Board's verdict and OQ-16 were written). OQ-16 closes only when the batch is in the SDDs (chess-a0r.1.8) and the desk review has passed, so the store task behind gate chess-cdb (chess-a0r.3.6, per the program manager) stays blocked until then | (1) An optional `calibration` feature: keeps SPRT code out of the server and desktop binaries, at the cost of a feature matrix to build and test. (2) Let store work that avoids journal payloads, the `GAME` DDL and recovery (data directory, open and migrate framework, backups, heartbeat registry) start earlier: less idle time, but a second definition of "store work that is safe" to police | **APPROVED 2026-11-04** (Brian Prascher, 2026-11-04, relayed by the program manager and recorded in beads chess-a0r.1.15). Tier **G1** for (1) (inside `chess-app`), a scheduling ruling for (2). Not reviewed by anyone but its author |
| D-78 | Time totals of a game that is suspended and resumed: where the accumulated time comes from (XC-DOM-A8-1, XC-APP-A8-3; store minors XC-ST-A8-1 to XC-ST-A8-4; beads chess-a0r.1.16; states what D-63 left open; Amd 8 Addendum D) | **(1) No new journal field, no new record kind, no change to SF-01 or to the `Suspend`, `Clock` or `Move` payloads.** (2) When a session resumes a game (`resume`, `open_held`), it seeds its time accumulator with the pure domain function `TimeTotals::seed_from_facts` over the replayed facts: `white_used_ms` and `black_used_ms` are the sums of `think_ms` over **every** `Move` fact of that side in the journal, **including plies that a later `Takeback` removed** (time is never refunded, domain 3.11.13; the journal keeps the `Move` records of taken-back plies `[OBS app SDD, takeback bullet]`), and `total_active_ms` is their sum. A `Move` fact without a `think_ms` adds 0. From then on the session adds live time with `time_accrual` as before, and the `GameEnd` assembler writes the final values (SF-01 unchanged). (3) **Stated loss, as part of the definition (option A, ratified by Brian, Addendum F):** after a stop (detach, shutdown, crash) the seed **may omit** (i) the time the side to move had already spent on its unfinished turn, because no journal record carries it in a form the seed uses (for a clean stop the `wall_ms` gap between the last record and the `Suspend` record could carry it; the seed does not use it, it mixes wall time with the monotonic `think_ms`, and a crash leaves no such record `[RAN: model of the design's record stream, not the product; INF for the product]`), and (ii) the time spent in `DrawOffered`: a `Fact` carries no stamp for a draw offer or its answer, so the seed counts none of it, whether the interval was open at the stop or closed earlier (it belongs to `total_active_ms` only) `[INF]`. **"May" is the wording Brian fixed: a resumed game may lose up to one turn of time per stop and is not required to.** The loss of (i) is at most one unfinished turn per stop. The loss of (ii) is the `DrawOffered` time of the segments before the last stop; the earlier text of this row said "one open draw-offer interval", which the seed rule does not give, so (ii) is stated as the rule gives it (`ESTIMATE`: seconds in a typical game; reported to Brian and the systems engineer in §11.4.14). Both are zero for a game that is never stopped. **The loss is accepted; it is not a limit that must not be improved:** a later change to `seed_from_facts` may recover (i) at a clean stop (for example from the `wall_ms` gap) with no change to DAT-3, its verification case or any stored format. The clock is not affected: it is restored from the `Clock` records, at most 1 s lost (RULE-5, §7.2). A live finalize and a finalize from the journal's `GameEnd` record still give the same `GAME` row (FF-3, UT-APP-26), because the record holds the final values. (4) The gap can be narrowed later **without any format change**, because the inputs are already in the journal (for a timed game, the fall of the running side's clock between its last `Move` and its last `Clock` record). That is a change to `seed_from_facts` alone (two-way). It is not done now: increments and takebacks make it a rule with its own tests, and an untimed game has no running clock to read `[INF]` (ASM-10 is closed, §14: an untimed game records `think_ms`, but it has no running clock). (5) **Store SDD minors, confirmed (G1):** XC-ST-A8-1: both reads stay; the live session reads `RowGone` from `lease_beat` (called every 2 s anyway), and the repair path of §7.6 reads `get_game`. XC-ST-A8-2: the import path computes `content_hash` for every imported game before the store call (D-34, D-42), so SF-17 stands; ASM-9 closes when the app SDD's import design says so. XC-ST-A8-3: the volume probe uses a scratch file removed after the probe. XC-ST-A8-4: a restore re-runs the void with the same function as the live void (store SDD 3.11.4), including slots, leases and the analysis job | (B) Add the three accumulated totals to the `Suspend` payload (G3): exact for a clean stop, but the `Suspend` that crash recovery writes cannot know them, so the derivation of (2) is needed anyway and there are two mechanisms. (C) Add the totals, or the in-flight fragment, to every `Clock` record (G3): exact to 1 s even after a crash, at the price of a larger record written every second and a change to the `Clock` fact, the codec and the FF-3 table. (D) A new journal kind `Totals` (G3): the same crash gap as B and one more kind code. (E) Count only the time since the last resume: loses all earlier time, worse for DAT-3. (F) Allow null totals when they are not exact: contradicts SF-01 ("always present") and UI-2 | **RATIFIED by Brian Prascher on 2026-11-04: option A (G2; OQ-28 closed; CR-12 item 1; beads chess-a0r.1.41).** Ruled first by the architect under Brian's delegation (Addendum D); Brian then chose option A from the proposal. Tier **G2** for (1) to (4) (a domain function and the app session, two crates, and a reading of DAT-3; no stored format changes), **G1** for (5). **The DAT-3 statement text is not changed**: the proposal's amendment wording (its section 5) is not applied, and the DAT-3 verification case of CR-12, worded "may lose up to one turn of time; is not required to", carries the acceptance. Options B and C (G3) are history. Not reviewed by anyone but its author. *Addendum F, note on the text above:* the phrase "ASM-10 is still open" in (4) is replaced by "closed, §14" and (3) is qualified as shown. The proposal and its evidence are in [PROPOSED-D-78-DAT-3-for-Brian.md](design/PROPOSED-D-78-DAT-3-for-Brian.md), now marked decided (A). The statement "including plies that a later `Takeback` removed" holds for an intact journal; for a journal rebased after damage see D-83 (taken-back plies in the lost part are not recoverable) |
| D-79 | The SAN comparison of D-72 (BP-013) needs `san` and `fen_after`, which `Fact::Move` does not carry: the replay interface (XC-DOM-A8-2; beads chess-a0r.1.17; Amd 8 Addendum D) | **`Game::replay` takes items, not bare facts:** `replay(impl IntoIterator<Item = ReplayItem>)`, `ReplayItem { fact: Fact, check: Option<MoveCheck> }`, `MoveCheck { san: String, fen_after: String }`, all public in `chess-domain`. `check` is `Some` only for a `Move` item built from a journal record and `None` for every other fact and for items built by tests or imports. **`Fact` does not change**, nor does `fact_to_record`, the §7.6 table or any stored format. `JournalCodec` gains `record_to_replay_item` (§5.7); `record_to_fact` stays as the Fact-only conversion. **One reading of the comparison (the XC-DOM-A8-2 hedge is closed):** (1) a `uci` that is illegal in the replayed position is `ReplayError::Illegal`, replay stops. (2) For a legal `uci`, replay computes the SAN and the position after the move itself and never takes either from the record. (3) With a `check`, a `fen_after` that differs from the computed position is `ReplayError::FenMismatch{ply}`, replay stops, **whatever the SAN comparison says**: the SAN is only a rendering of the move and cannot explain a different position, so the Board's "not explained by SAN" (BP-013) is read as "not a SAN rendering difference". The two FENs are compared as parsed six-field values with the same normalisation as the standard-start test of SF-02, so a difference of text only (for example how an en passant square is written) is not an error `[UNV: depends on the `chess-rules` FEN type, XC-DOM-A8-5]`; an unparseable recorded `fen_after` counts as a mismatch. (4) With equal positions and a different SAN, replay logs `ReplayWarning::SanMismatch{ply, recorded, computed}` and continues with the computed values. (5) Without a `check`, nothing is compared. A replay error is handled as every other one (`JournalUnreadable{recovered_moves}` up to the ply before). No crate edge, dependency or protocol shape changes | (B) Add `san` and `fen_after` to `Fact::Move`: the `Fact` would carry derived data and two truths, `decide` would have to produce both, the "Fact does not change" bullet of §7.6 is reversed, and FF-3 and the domain table change (G2) for no gain. (C) Compare in the app after replay, from `Game::view().plies`: no domain API change, but plies removed by a `Takeback` are not in the view, so their records are never checked, and the domain has already trusted the `uci`; this is a weaker check in a different place. (D) The app keeps a shadow position through `chess-rules` and replays beside the domain: a second replay to keep in step, with its own takeback handling. (E) Drop the comparison and recompute silently: loses the detection of a record that disagrees with the rules, which Brian ruled against. (F) A second method `replay_checked` beside `replay`: two entry points for one rule | **RULED under Brian's delegation, 2026-11-04, and RATIFIED by Brian Prascher, 2026-11-04 (beads chess-a0r.1.30).** Tier **G2** (a public `chess-domain` interface used by `chess-app`, as BP-013 already was in D-72). No requirement text and no §3.2 edge changes `[OBS]`: `MoveCheck` holds two strings and the app already depends on the domain. The reading of D-72's wording in (3) is a clarification of an approved text; ratified by Brian with the rest of the ruling, including this reading of D-72's wording. Not reviewed by anyone but its author |
| D-80 | The command set of the `JournalDegraded` state that D-65 part 2 left to the app design (XC-APP-A8-1; beads chess-a0r.1.18; Amd 8 Addendum D) | A session that adopted a durable prefix after a failed append (D-65 part 2) accepts **only**: (a) read-only queries (the game view, `GetGame`, subscriptions), which show the adopted prefix; (b) `Detach`; (c) `Shutdown`. **`Detach` and `Shutdown` release the journal lock and append nothing** (no `Suspend`, no `Clock`; this replaces the app SDD's choice to try to journal them). The next resume finds a journal that ends in a non-terminal state that is not `Suspended`, which is the interrupted-game case of D-58, and writes `Suspend{cause: recovered}` and `SuspendResume` in a fresh session. The only append the degraded session makes is the terminal re-check of D-65 part 1, run once on entry, which journals the `Ended` fact that the failed step had already decided. **Every other command is answered `JournalWriteFailed{cause}`** with no state change and no record: `MakeMove`, `Takeback`, `OfferDraw`, `RespondDraw`, `Resign`, `Abort`, `Pause`, `PlayResume`, `Step`, a move-now request, the engine turn and a flag fall. The clocks keep the adopted values, no flag timer is armed and no engine search starts. The state ends only by the terminal re-check or by a resume. **Resign and abort, said plainly:** they append an `Ended` or `Aborted` fact, so they are **not** consistent with "appends no further facts", and they are not offered in the degraded session. The player reaches them in two steps: `Detach`, then the resume prompt, whose `open_held` session already accepts `Resign`, `Abort` and `Detach`. Reasons: after a terminal re-check whose own append failed, the position is terminal and a `Resign` could overturn the result; and a session whose write path just failed is the wrong place to attempt a new durable fact. Consequence: if the journal volume is still unwritable at the resume, the recovery append fails, `JournalWriteFailed{cause}` is returned and the game stays unfinished with its journal intact, which is the journal-first rule (NFR-2) and not new. No protocol shape, error code, §5.7 contract or stored format changes: `JournalWriteFailed`, `Detach`, `Shutdown` and `open_held` already exist | (A) The app SDD's set: queries, `Detach` that first tries to journal `Suspend` and `Clock`, and `Shutdown`: the attempt is a new multi-record step inside a state that exists because a multi-record step failed. (C) The same set plus `Resign` and `Abort` as one terminal append: shorter for the player, but it appends in a session that cannot write, and it needs a rule that stops a `Resign` after an unwritten mate. (D) Queries only: the lock stays held until the process ends, so the player cannot resume. (E) Re-run the recovery inside the same session: it hides the fault and can loop. (F) Close the session at once on a partial failure: simplest, but the player loses the view of the game and D-65's approved degraded state disappears | **RULED under Brian's delegation, 2026-11-04, and RATIFIED by Brian Prascher, 2026-11-04 (beads chess-a0r.1.30).** Tier **G1** (inside `chess-app`; the ruling fills the space D-65 left to the app design and uses only existing codes and commands). The G1 desk review is part of the OQ-16 desk review: the system-architect part checked D-80 on 2026-11-04 and found that it stands, with F-09 (front-end consequences, handed to the front-end SDDs); the re-review after Addendum E has not been held. Not reviewed by anyone but its author |
| D-81 | `cargo-nextest`: not adopted for now (dependency-lookup F3; amends the "not decided" line of D-76; Amd 8 Addendum D) | `cargo-nextest` is **not adopted**. The suite runs with plain `cargo test` on the stable toolchain, locally and in CI; `cargo llvm-cov` wraps it. No document, CI job or script may name or require nextest. The copy on the development Mac, 0.9.128, is in the affected range of RUSTSEC-2026-0412 `[RAN lookup E3, E9, F3]` and is not used for any gate result. Admitting it later needs a fresh lookup of a patched version and a G2 decision. `cargo-insta` stays not decided and not admitted (the `insta` crate of §3.4 is a test library, a different item). Consequence: tests of one crate run as threads of one process, so a test that needs process isolation (signals, environment, cross-process `flock`) must start its own child process `[INF]` | Adopt it at a patched version now (G2; the lookup named a patched range, not re-verified): a better runner, but one more tool to pin and scan before step 1 needs it. Keep it "not decided": leaves the CI definition open | **Recorded as instructed by the work order of 2026-11-04 (Brian's decision as relayed by the program manager; the architect has the relay, not Brian's words).** Tier **G0** for the text (no tool is admitted; a later admission is G2). Not reviewed by anyone but its author |
| D-82 | What the journal writer does after a failed or ambiguous append (desk review F-01; beads chess-a0r.1.21; Amd 8 Addendum E) | **One rule, in §7.6 "After a failed or ambiguous append".** An append is one blocking task that runs to completion; on a write or sync failure the same task truncates the file to the last acknowledged length and syncs; if that succeeds the file is as before, `seq` is not consumed and the writer stays usable; if it fails, or the append future was dropped before the outcome was known, the writer is **poisoned** (`WriteFailed` with the cause `Poisoned`): it never writes again and the only exit is releasing the lock and resuming, where the torn-tail rule truncates. A failed record is never followed by another record in the same file. A poisoned writer puts the session in the D-80 degraded state; the terminal re-check of D-65/D-80 appends only through a healthy writer. A record whose cleanup failed may still be intact and may be applied at the resume (accepted). No stored format, kind, protocol shape or error code changes (`Poisoned` is a member of the store-internal cause of the existing `WriteFailed` variant, so the §5.7 `JournalError` list is unchanged) | (B) **Never append again in the session after any append error** (poison always): simpler, but one transient `ENOSPC` that the user fixes by freeing space would end every session, and the 1 Hz `Clock` tick would turn a recoverable error into a degraded game. (C) **Leave the file as it is and make the scan tolerant** (accept intact records after a damaged one): changes the scan rule and the torn-tail reasoning of NFR-2, a stored-format-adjacent change (G3). (D) Do nothing: the Major finding stands (a false `JournalUnreadable`, a duplicate `seq`, a rejected move replayed) | **RULED by the architect, 2026-11-04 (Addendum E). Not reviewed by anyone but its author.** Tier **G1** (inside `chess-store`, with the session rule inside `chess-app`; no format, edge or §5.7 contract changes) |
| D-83 | The journal rebase after damage (desk review F-03; beads chess-a0r.1.23; Amd 8 Addendum E) | **Define the rebase, keep "the damaged file is kept aside" (§7.6).** New file `<game_uuid>.jnl.rebase`: header, the intact run copied **byte for byte** (same `seq`, `wall_ms`, payload), synthesized `Move` records from `MOVE` rows only for stored plies beyond the intact run that agree with it, one closing `Clock`; sync file and directory; one store transaction applies the plan and sets `GAME.journal_seq` to the new last `seq`; then the old file moves to `journal/damaged/<game_uuid>.<k>.jnl` and the new file is renamed into place; startup renames a complete `.rebase` into place when `<game_uuid>.jnl` is absent. `seq` stays continuous, so D-66 is not re-triggered. Crash windows are listed in §7.6. *Addendum F (RR-A-1, RR-A-5):* if the intact run holds no `Start` record, the rebase writes a `Start` as record 1 synthesized from the `GAME` row (as for a row with no journal, D-84) and numbers the synthesized `Move` records from 2; a game whose `GAME.status` is final or voided is **not** rebased (nothing will be appended): its damaged file is moved aside and its rows stay; the first rename syncs both directories (`journal/` and `journal/damaged/`); both renames fail rather than replace an existing target; and the "row with no journal" path of D-84 looks for a complete `.rebase` first. What it cannot restore: records in the lost part of the file (taken-back plies, `Pause`, `DrawOffer`, `Clock` ticks); D-78's seed after a rebase omits the time of taken-back plies lost with it | (B) Drop the rebase: truncate the damaged file at the end of the intact run and append: does not work when the store is longer than the journal, because `seq` could not stay continuous without synthesized records, or the scan rule would change. (C) Rebuild the new journal from `MOVE` rows only (the earlier store text): renumbers `seq`, loses the `Start` stamp, `Takeback` and `DrawOffer` records, and re-triggers D-66 after a crash between the file swap and the DB transaction. (D) Do nothing | **RULED by the architect, 2026-11-04 (Addendum E); rated G2 and approved by Brian Prascher on 2026-11-04 (Addendum F; CR-12 item 4).** Tier **G2**: inside `chess-store`, with no record kind, field or version change, but it adds two names to the data directory (`.jnl.rebase`, `damaged/<uuid>.<k>.jnl`; store design 3.5.1), which is a stored-layout element. The architect had rated it G1; Brian rated it G2. Not reviewed by anyone but its author |
| D-84 | One stamp for `GAME.started_at` and the `Start` record's `wall_ms`; the start order and the row-without-journal path (desk review F-02; beads chess-a0r.1.22; Amd 8 Addendum E) | **Invariant (applied, text only):** the session's single start `Stamp` is `Fact::Start.started_at`, the `Start` record's `wall_ms` and `GAME.started_at` (SF-16 already approved the record side). A row with no journal gets a `Start` at seq 1 with `wall_ms = GAME.started_at` and fields from the row. FF-3 states its precondition and gains a restore test. **Mechanism (DECIDED by Brian Prascher, 2026-11-04, G2, OQ-26 option A; Addendum F):** `NewGame` gains `started_at: i64` (a `WallMs`, milliseconds since the Unix epoch like `wall_ms`), supplied by the app as the session's one start `Stamp` and written by the store unchanged to `GAME.started_at`; the store reads no clock for it and `start_game` returns the existing game for a known `game_uuid` without rewriting it. `Fact::Start.started_at` and the `Start` record's `wall_ms` carry the same value. `NewGame` is a Rust DTO of `StoreApi` (§5.7), not a protocol type, with no `ts-rs` derive; the `StartGame` command and the generated TypeScript types do not change | (B) The store stamps the row from its own clock and `start_game` returns the value, which the session must then use for the `Start` fact: the domain `Stamp` is taken first, so the fact is built before the row exists. (C) The app reads the row back with `get_game` and patches the `Start` stamp: an extra read in the start path and a fact that is patched. (D) Do nothing: `started_at` differs by a few ms between row and record, FF-3 fails on `started_at`, the hash `Date` can differ across UTC midnight | **Invariant: RULED by the architect (G1 text; it implements SF-16). Mechanism: DECIDED by Brian Prascher, 2026-11-04 (G2, option A; OQ-26 closed; CR-12 item 2).** Not reviewed by anyone but its author |
| D-85 | `ended_at` of a game ended by the terminal re-check (desk review F-08; Amd 8 Addendum E) | `ended_at` is the `wall_ms` of the final `Move` record (the instant of the terminal move), as on the normal path; the `GameEnd` record's own `wall_ms` is the resume time. Restore and startup finalize therefore order games by when they ended | (B) The resume time: orders Elo changes against a computer Player by recovery order, not game order (D-72, BP-012). (C) The `Move`'s `wall_ms` plus one tick: invented | **RULED by the architect, 2026-11-04 (Addendum E).** Tier **G1** (domain and app text; no format change). Not reviewed by anyone but its author |
| D-86 | The held session and `SuspendResume` (XC-APP-A8-2; desk review F-12; Amd 8 Addendum E) | **Accepted as the app design stated it:** `open_held` journals no `SuspendResume`, the `Game` stays `Suspended` (T16 and T17 allow `Resign` and `Abort`), clocks keep their last values. D-80's route (Detach, resume prompt, `open_held`, `Resign` or `Abort`) was re-checked against it and holds | (B) `open_held` journals `SuspendResume` first: restarts a clock that must not run in a held game, and needs a `Pause`-like record to stop it. (C) A new held state in the domain: a new state for no new rule | **RULED by the architect, 2026-11-04 (Addendum E).** Tier **G1**. Not reviewed by anyone but its author |
| D-87 | A journal with `GameEnd` followed by `Voided` (XC-APP-A8-4; XC-DOM-A8-3; desk review F-12; Amd 8 Addendum E) | The journal is **voided**: moves and `Voided` are applied and the `GameEnd` is never finalized by the scan, resume or restore. A session appends no `Voided` after its own `GameEnd`; only the repair routine produces the pair. §4.3 gains the replay-only `Completed -> Voided` transition; domain T18 already has it. *Addendum F (RR-A-2):* the rule covers `GameEnd` only. `Abort` followed by `Voided` is not a pair this design produces (`Abort` exists only for computer-against-computer games, and the reset that writes `Voided` needs a human Player; the unreadable-game void is written for a journal with no readable run), and §4.3 and domain T18 allow `Voided` only from `Completed`, so such a journal is a replay error and is reported as damage (`JournalUnreadable`). The smaller change was chosen: the text drops "or `Abort`"; no edge is added to §4.3 or T18 | (B) Finalize the `GameEnd` then void: settles a rating for a game that does not count. (C) Forbid the pair and call it damage: a recoverable window becomes a false `JournalUnreadable` | **RULED by the architect, 2026-11-04 (Addendum E).** Tier **G1** (restating D-67 (v), "a restore skips the pair", as a rule). Not reviewed by anyone but its author |
| D-88 | A start that was never acknowledged leaves a zero-move row that holds a slot (desk review F-13; Amd 8 Addendum E) | Keep it as a zero-move resumable game; no automatic void. `StartGame` is idempotent by `game_uuid`; the resume list shows the game with zero moves | (B) Void rows with no journal and no moves at startup: uses the unreadable-void path (a false damage report) and may void a game whose journal file the user deleted. (C) Delete the row: loses the slot guard and the audit | **RULED by the architect, 2026-11-04 (Addendum E).** Tier **G1**. Not reviewed by anyone but its author |

### 11.1 D-4: the GUI board component (AGREED 2026-10-25)

**Decision.** chessground plus our own shared keyboard and accessibility layer (Option C in the [refined trade study](trade-study-gui-board-refined.md)). The user ruled out building our own board for v1. The [first-pass study](trade-study-gui-board.md) is kept for the record. **Fallback:** react-chessboard with the same layer and a small patch removing its built-in keyboard drag (study Option D1, not decision D-1), if the spike below fails.

**Why.** C and D1 scored nearly level (88.8 vs. 87.4). C was chosen because:
- the main reason for using a library is mature pointer interaction, and chessground's is proven on Lichess;
- C's weak spot (selection reconciliation) is our own testable code, while D1's (a carried patch, a single maintainer, a pending drag-engine migration) are external;
- chessground is inert to focus and assistive technology as shipped, so it needs no modification.

**Design.**

```mermaid
flowchart TB
    props["BoardProps<br/>(fen, orientation, legalMoves, interactive,<br/>lastMove, check, arrows, theme, onMove)"]
    model["BoardModel reducer (pure TS)<br/>cursor · selected · pendingPromotion · announcement"]
    overlay["AccessibleOverlay<br/>transparent CSS grid over the board<br/>role=grid · 64 labelled gridcells · roving tabindex<br/>visible focus ring · pointer-events: none"]
    live["LiveRegion (aria-live)"]
    picker["PromotionPicker"]
    subgraph adapter["chessground adapter (D-4, chosen)"]
        map["toConfig(props, model)<br/>fen · orientation · movable.dests · lastMove · check ·<br/>selected · drawable.autoShapes · viewOnly · jsHover ·<br/>theme CSS classes"]
        cg["chessground instance<br/>(aria-hidden=true)"]
        rec["Reconciler<br/>events.select → PointerSelect<br/>events.change → read api.state.selected<br/>movable.events.after → PointerMove"]
    end
    fallback["Fallback adapter (only if the spike fails)<br/>react-chessboard + patch (study option D1)<br/>same BoardProps, same shared layer"]
    props --> model
    props --> map
    model --> map --> cg
    cg --> rec --> model
    overlay -- "keys" --> model
    model --> overlay
    model --> live
    model --> picker
    model -- "move complete" --> onMove["onMove → MakeMove{ply,…}<br/>(rejected move: core keeps fen → piece reverts)"]
    adapter -. "swappable behind the Board contract" .- fallback
```

- **`Board` contract.** Screens use the board only through `BoardProps` (refined study §5, first-pass §5.0), so the fallback replaces just the adapter.
- **One selection state.** `BoardModel` is the source of truth. Its selection is pushed into chessground with the `selected` config value. chessground's own changes come back through `events.select` (including drag start). Silent unselects, which fire no event, are caught on `events.change` by reading `api.state.selected`.
- **Accessibility overlay.** A transparent ARIA grid sized from chessground's `---cg-width`/`---cg-height` CSS variables sits exactly over the squares.
  - Keyboard focus and the VoiceOver cursor land on the real squares.
  - Mouse and touch pass through to chessground.
  - The chessground board is `aria-hidden="true"`, which is axe-clean because chessground has no focusable elements.
- **Moves.** Pointer moves are applied optimistically by chessground and confirmed by the core's next snapshot. A rejected move (e.g., `StalePosition`) is reverted by re-setting `fen`. Keyboard moves go through `onMove` and are animated when the snapshot's `fen` arrives.
- **Read-only.** `viewOnly` while rewound (GAME-3), on computer turns, and in computer–computer games.
- **Spike (2 days, first GUI task).** The exit criteria are:
  1. `BoardModel.selected` equals `api.state.selected` after every step of scripted mixed mouse/keyboard sequences: click→Enter, Space→click, drag→Esc, click elsewhere, rejected-move revert, and promotion by mouse and by keyboard.
  2. axe is clean.
  3. The overlay aligns in both orientations at three sizes.
  4. Drag works in Safari, Chrome, Firefox, Edge, and the Tauri WKWebView.
  5. The overlay stays aligned during continuous window resizing down to a narrow width (no GUI minimum size, Q55).

  If criterion 1 can't be met reliably, switch the adapter to study option D1 (about 3–4 days; the shared layer carries over).
- **Upstream.** Propose a configurable sensor list to react-chessboard, to keep the fallback patch-free.

### 11.3 Door type, consequences, and revisit triggers

One-way door = costly to reverse: weeks of work or data migration. Rationale for D-1–D-11 is in the table above and in §11.1. D-4 is gated by its spike.

`GATE-005 | Subject: amendment 2, D-31 to D-37 | Scores: blast 1, reversibility 2, novelty 1, regulatory 0, cost 1, data 1, coupling 1 = 7 | Tier: R1 | Overrides: none.` R1 asks for the architect's checklist, two options noted per decision, and an ADR for any one-way door. The options are in the table above, and the one one-way door (UUID derivation after a release, D-34) has its escape route in the table below. The checklist was run by the architect against the guardrails and the definition of done; no separate Review Board pass was held at R1. **Human sign-off is not claimed.** The systems engineer and the user may correct any entry marked DECIDED (delegated).

`GATE-006 | Subject: amendment 7, D-38 to D-50 | Scores: blast 2, reversibility 2, novelty 1, regulatory 0, cost 1, data 1, coupling 2 = 9 | Tier: R2 | Overrides: none.` Reversibility is scored 2 because the stored-format batch (D-41 to D-43) is cheap to change before the first release and a data migration after it. R2 asks for a separate Review Board pass with four or more lenses, a lightweight ATAM, ADRs and fitness functions for the top scenarios. Here: the ADR-style records are D-38 to D-50 in §11 and the door table below; the fitness functions are FF-1 to FF-8 (§9); the decisions were approved by the named human approver, Brian Prascher (G2/G3, relayed to the architect in the task for this amendment). **A separate Review Board pass was not held for this amendment. The user answered on 2026-10-31 that it must be held on D-41 to D-43 before the first store code, and it is an entry condition for store implementation (OQ-16).** The user's approval does not replace it, and the architect does not waive it. No claim of independent review is made.

`GATE-007 | Subject: amendment 7 addendum A, D-51 to D-61 and the amended text of D-41, D-42, D-45, D-46 | Tier: R2, inherited from GATE-006 (the addendum amends D-41 and D-42, whose Board pass, OQ-16, has not been held) | Overrides: none | Not scored separately.` The Review Board pass of OQ-16 must cover the `content_hash` payload fields, the per-kind payload table with its seven flags (§7.6) and D-57 and D-58. **Human sign-off is not claimed:** Brian Prascher ruled on items 1 to 4 and 6 and delegated item 5 for his review of the shapes chosen here; he has since confirmed D-56, chosen option C for OQ-21, and agreed D-59 with OQ-ECO-1 to OQ-ECO-3 closed (G2).

`GATE-008 | Subject: amendment 8, D-63 to D-73 and the stored-format batch SF-01 to SF-22 | Scores: blast 2, reversibility 2, novelty 1, regulatory 0, cost 1, data 1, coupling 2 = 9 | Tier: R2 | Overrides: none.` The factors are the same as GATE-006, because Amendment 8 changes the same stored-format batch and the same recovery paths (scored by the author, `[INF]`; the score is not an approved score). Reversibility is 2 because the batch is cheap to change before the first release and a data migration after it; if it were scored 3, the floor of R2 would apply and the total would be 10, so the tier does not change. **Independence statement (plain).** Amendment 8 was written by the system-architect agent in the Architect stance, in one invocation. **No review of Amendment 8 had been held when GATE-008 was written** (*update 2026-11-04: the two OQ-16 desk reviews were held later the same day, PASS WITH CONDITIONS; see Addendum E and GATE-011*): no Review Board pass, no desk review, no lightweight ATAM. The Board pass of OQ-16 ([Board-pass-store-journal.md](design/Board-pass-store-journal.md)) reviewed the Amendment 7 text, not this one; it was one reviewer of the same agent family and it states that it does not satisfy R2 alone. The step that Brian set as the closing condition of OQ-16 is a **desk review by the system-architect and the systems engineer**, and it must be performed by a different invocation than this author. It is a later step; this text makes no claim that it has happened or that it would satisfy the R2 requirements (four lenses, ATAM) unless Brian rules so. Brian's G-1 decisions are his. **Brian approved the text on 2026-11-04** (recorded in beads chess-a0r.1.14), including the G2 tier of D-67, the D-72 and D-73 minor items, D-58 as one-way after release and the six format choices as recommended. That approval is the owner's decision on the text; it is not a Review Board pass or a desk review, and this record does not present it as one. SWE-001 (FIND-016) waives a separate independent reviewer for G3 in v1; that waiver is stated here and is not relied on. **No sign-off is claimed beyond Brian's approval of 2026-11-04.** The fitness-function additions are listed in §11.4.

`GATE-009 | Subject: amendment 8 addenda A, B and C, D-74 to D-77 | Scores: blast 1, reversibility 1, novelty 0, regulatory 0, cost 1, data 0, coupling 1 = 4 | Tier: R1 | Overrides: none.` Scored by the author `[INF]`; not an approved score. R1 asks for the architect's checklist, two options noted, and an ADR for any one-way door. The options are in the D-74 to D-76 rows of §11 (at least three each); none of the three is a one-way door (§11.3). The checklist was run by the architect against the guardrails and the definition of done, in one invocation, with no separate review. **D-74 was proposed here and is now CONFIRMED by Brian as a G2 decision (2026-11-04, beads chess-a0r.1.19; Addendum D).** D-75 was decided by the architect under Brian's delegation, relayed by the program manager. D-76 was ruled by Brian (G2, approved 2026-11-04). D-77 records Brian's answers to OQ-24 and OQ-25 (no design change). The addenda touch no stored format, so GATE-008 and OQ-16 are not affected. **No review and no sign-off beyond Brian's rulings is claimed.**

`GATE-010 | Subject: amendment 8 addendum D, D-78 to D-81 (and the confirmation of D-74) | Scores: blast 1, reversibility 1, novelty 0, regulatory 0, cost 1, data 1, coupling 1 = 5 | Tier: R1 | Overrides: none.` Scored by the author `[INF]`; not an approved score. ### 11.4 Amendment 8: stored-format batch, items for Brian, drafted logging text (APPROVED by Brian, 2026-11-04; addenda A to D)

**Status.** Written by the architect (author) on 2026-11-04. Brian Prascher dispositioned the Board findings at G-1 on 2026-11-04 and **approved the text of D-63 to D-73 and SF-01 to SF-22 on 2026-11-04** (recorded in beads chess-a0r.1.14), with the six format choices as the architect recommended. The approval is Brian's; **no Review Board pass, desk review or ATAM of this amendment has been held** (GATE-008, §11.3), and **OQ-16 stays open** until the batch is carried into the SDDs (chess-a0r.1.8) and a desk review by the system-architect and the systems engineer (different invocations from the author) has passed. Addenda A to D (§11.4.8, §11.4.9, §11.4.11, §11.4.12) were added the same day: D-74 was proposed and is now confirmed by Brian (G2), D-75 was decided by the architect under Brian's delegation, D-76 was ruled by Brian, D-77 records Brian's answers, and D-78 to D-80 were ruled by the architect under Brian's delegation (*Addendum E: Brian ratified D-79 and D-80 on 2026-11-04; D-78 awaited his choice; Addendum F: Brian ratified D-78, option A*). Evidence tags are as in the header. Nothing was built; the only thing run is stated in the Mermaid line at the end of this section.

"Board-recommended" items are ones Brian did not rule on individually at G-1; he confirmed D-72 and D-73 as minor items on 2026-11-04. #### 11.4.2 Stored-format batch (Amendment 8): SF-01 to SF-22

One batch, one gate (G3, with G1 sub-items as marked). Every item has a proposed value and a reason. Six items (SF-02, SF-03, SF-04, SF-15, SF-16, SF-21) were a user choice; **Brian made all six on 2026-11-04 as the architect recommended**, and each cell says so. Reversal notes: **before the first release** the cost is an edit of this section, the §7.2 DDL, the §7.6 table and the SDDs, with no migration (ASM-6, nothing is released); **after release** it is as stated in the last column. The decoder uses `deny_unknown_fields` (store design 3.11.1), so even an additive journal field after release needs `journal_version` 2 with a reader for 1 (`[OBS]` store design; the mechanism is a consequence, `[INF]`). `journal_version` and `format_version` stay 1 until the first release.

| ID | Item (finding) | Proposed value or rule | Rationale and evidence | Tier | Reversal after release |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SF-01 | `GameEnd` finalize time totals (BP-001; flag 1; D-63) | `GameEnd` payload adds `white_time_used_ms`, `black_time_used_ms`, `total_active_ms`: whole milliseconds, `>= 0`, always present. Added by the assembler, not in the `Fact`. Finalize and restore write them to `GAME`. `total_active_ms` is the session's measured active time as already described in §7.2 (excludes time with the app closed, suspended or paused; the exact treatment of a pending draw offer is the domain clock rule, T8 and T11, and the domain design must state it once) | `FinalizeRequest` has the three fields and the journal does not `[OBS store:480, arch:1536]`; the totals cannot be derived from `Move.think_ms` (Board's three cases) `[INF]`. Whether an untimed game also has measured totals was ASM-10 (closed 2026-11-04, Addendum E: it does) | G3. FOR BRIAN (G3): approved 2026-11-04 | `journal_version` 2 with a v1 reader that sums `think_ms` (lossy) |
| SF-02 | Custom start (BP-005; flags 4, 5; D-64) | Defined once: `custom_start = 1` iff the start differs from the standard start. A start equal to the standard start is standard: `custom_start = 0`, `start_fen = NULL` (JSON `null` in `Start`). The domain constructor normalises. `GAME.custom_start INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (custom_start IN (0,1))`, `CHECK (custom_start = (start_fen IS NOT NULL))`, `CHECK (custom_start = 0 OR rated = 0)`. `StartPosition = Standard | Custom(Fen)` is defined in the domain design. `Start.rated` and `Start.self_play` are frozen history, authoritative for a restored row. **Decided by Brian, 2026-11-04, as recommended: (A)** all six FEN fields must equal the standard start's (a different move counter or clock changes numbering and the fifty-move count, so it is not "the standard start"). Not chosen: (B) placement, side to move, castling and en passant only (counters ignored). Imported games: `custom_start` follows the same rule applied to the `FEN` tag | Resolves the three definitions (architecture flag 4, RULE-8 and CR-9.11, the app SDD) and gives the store a column to return for statistics and history `[OBS req:502-507, 623; domain:370, 753]` | G3. FOR BRIAN (G3): approved 2026-11-04 | Numbered migration `ADD COLUMN ... DEFAULT 0`, backfill `= (start_fen IS NOT NULL)`; a changed comparison does not re-rate old games |
| SF-03 | Version marker in `content_hash` (BP-007; D-68) | `sha256:v1:<64 lowercase hex>`. `v1` versions `canonical_form`. The importer compares only values with the same version; another version never matches and is not an error. **Decided by Brian, 2026-11-04, as recommended: (A) `sha256:v1:<hex>`** (keeps the algorithm prefix of D-42 first). Not chosen: (B) `v1:sha256:<hex>`; (C) `sha256-v1-<hex>`. *Addendum F:* `v1` is defined with the OQ-27 rule that `canonical_form` omits `SetUp` and `FEN` when the `FEN` equals the standard start (Brian, G3, 2026-11-04) | The prefix versions the algorithm, not the form; a stored hash cannot be told from a new one after a change `[OBS arch:1994, 2149]` | G3. FOR BRIAN (G3): approved 2026-11-04 | Bump to `v2`, keep the `v1` reader; old values stop matching new ones (a harmless extra review-only copy on re-import) |
| SF-04 | Hash scope and the `Date` rule (BP-006; D-68) | The stored hash is the hash as of game end (names, `Date` and rule-owned tags as then). FF-4 holds only without a rename between export and re-import, and its test says so. **Decided by Brian, 2026-11-04, as recommended: (A)** the UTC calendar date of `started_at`, written `YYYY.MM.DD` (independent of the time zone and of the export day; present for every game this app plays). Not chosen: (B) the local date of `started_at`; (C) the UTC date of `ended_at`. For an imported game the `Date` is the tag value stored in `GAME_TAG` | Today the source and time zone of `Date` are stated nowhere `[OBS Board BP-006]`; (B) changes with a time-zone change; (C) differs from (A) for a game that spans UTC midnight | G3. FOR BRIAN (G3): approved 2026-11-04 | A changed rule is a new `canonical_form` version (SF-03) |
| SF-05 | `Voided.ended_at` (BP-009; flag 6; D-69) | `Voided` payload adds `ended_at` (epoch ms integer, the unit of `GAME.ended_at`). Reset: the commit time written to `GAME.ended_at`. Unreadable void: the void time. A `Voided` appended later (repair, other process): copied from the `GAME` row, never `now`. The record's `wall_ms` stays the append time | A restore re-running the void from the record would otherwise write a later time `[OBS arch:1548]` | G3. FOR BRIAN (G3): approved 2026-11-04 | `journal_version` 2 |
| SF-06 | `journal_seq` of a voided game; DB effect of `Voided` (BP-009; D-69) | After `Voided` is appended and synced, the holder passes it to `apply_records`. Its DB effect: set `journal_seq` to the record's `seq`; only if the row is not yet `voided` (restore), re-run the void from `reason`, `reset_player`, `ended_at`, `content_hash`. The startup scan applies an unapplied `Voided` the same way. The store design's cell "none" is replaced | `reset_player` runs before the record exists and cannot set the sequence; without this, `done/` keeps reset-voided journals forever `[OBS store:833; Board BP-009]` | G1 *(Addendum E, F-04: corrected to **G2**; the item changes what `apply_records` does and passes a record across the `chess-app`/`chess-store` contract, as D-67 did. Brian approved the item on 2026-11-04 under either label, so no unapproved change exists; do not use G1 here as precedent)* | Text only |
| SF-07 | Journal `kind: u8` codes (BP-010 (1)) | `Start` 1, `Move` 2, `Takeback` 3, `Clock` 4, `DrawOffer` 5, `DrawDeclined` 6, `DrawDropped` 7, `Pause` 8, `Resume` 9, `Suspend` 10, `SuspendResume` 11, `GameEnd` 12, `Abort` 13, `Voided` 14. 0 and 15 to 255 are invalid (decode error). A code is never reused | Order of §7.6; 0 invalid so a zero-filled block does not decode as a record `[INF]` | G3 | Codes never change. A new kind takes the next number with `journal_version` 2 |
| SF-08 | Checksum (BP-010 (2)) | CRC-32 of the ISO-HDLC / IEEE 802.3 family (the zlib `crc32`): reflected, init and final XOR `0xFFFFFFFF`; the check value of the ASCII bytes `123456789` is `0xCBF43926` `[RAN python3 zlib.crc32]`. Record CRC covers `len` (4 bytes, little-endian) followed by the body, as in the store design; the `A5 5A` marker is not covered. `header_crc32` covers the header from the magic to the end of `store_id`. A test vector with that check value is in the unit tests | A common, tool-checkable choice, so a hand repair with standard tools is possible. Castagnoli would be a defensible alternative; any detection advantage on these small records is `[UNV]`, and the crate choice is OQ-17, not decided here | G3 | `journal_version` 2 |
| SF-09 | Maximum record body (BP-010 (3)) | 65 536 bytes. A larger `len` is read as damage; the writer refuses to write it (a write error) | The largest records (`Start` with a custom FEN, `GameEnd` with an ECO name) are far below 64 KiB `[INF]`; the bound limits what a corrupt `len` can make the scan skip | G3 | Raising it is a `journal_version` bump; under D-16 (one bundle) that is cheap |
| SF-10 | `VoidReason` (BP-010 (4)) | Journal strings `reset` and `unreadable`. The codec maps them to `GAME.termination` values `voided by reset` and `voided: unreadable` (unchanged, OQ-18). An unknown value is a decode error | Two variants exist today `[OBS arch:1418, 1459]` | G3 | `journal_version` 2 for a new variant |
| SF-11 | `ClockSpec` text (BP-010 (5)) | `<base>+<increment>`, both whole seconds in decimal, no sign, no spaces, no leading zeros (`0` alone is allowed): `300+0`, `180+2`. Both parts always present. Null when untimed. The CLI minutes shorthand `5+0` is input only and is converted. The decoder rejects other forms | Matches the PGN `TimeControl` seconds form named in the domain design `[OBS domain:361]`; one spelling per value keeps the text column comparable | G3 | A migration that rewrites `clock_spec` |
| SF-12 | UUID text form (BP-010 (6)) | Lower-case, hyphenated, 36 characters (`8-4-4-4-12`) in payloads, journal file names and every `TEXT UNIQUE` uuid column (compared with `BINARY` collation). Decoders reject other forms; the boundary normalises input. Proposed DDL backstop on each uuid column: `CHECK (uuid = lower(uuid) AND length(uuid) = 36)` | `BINARY` collation makes `A` and `a` different keys `[INF]`; one spelling avoids two files for one game | G3 | A migration that lower-cases values and renames files |
| SF-13 | `Start.start_fen` for a standard start (BP-010 (7)) | JSON `null`; never the standard FEN. See SF-02 | Removes the "every game becomes custom" reading `[OBS Board BP-005]` | G3 | As SF-02 |
| SF-14 | Half-null clocks (BP-010 (8)) | In `Move` and `Clock` payloads `white_ms` and `black_ms` are both null or both numbers; a half-null pair is a decode error. The `Fact` keeps one `Option<ClockValues>`. DDL backstop: SF-18 | The record has two nullable fields and the `Fact` has one option, so a half-null pair is representable and undefined `[OBS Board BP-010]` | G3 | `journal_version` 2 |
| SF-15 | `GameEnd.reason` strings (BP-010 (9)) | **Decided by Brian, 2026-11-04, as recommended: (B).** The options were: (A) Keep the domain's strings in the journal as they are in `GAME.termination` (`time forfeit, opponent cannot mate` has spaces and a comma): one vocabulary, but display-style text is a stored machine value. (B) Snake_case codes in the journal only: `checkmate`, `stalemate`, `insufficient_material`, `threefold_repetition`, `fifty_move_rule`, `time_forfeit`, `timeout_opponent_cannot_mate`, `resignation`, `draw_agreed`; the codec maps 1:1 to the unchanged, SE-confirmed `GAME.termination` strings (Q-SE-5, OQ-18). **Chosen: (B).** Unknown value: decode error | A journal string should not change when a display string is reworded; (B) does not reopen Q-SE-5 or the `games.json` list | G3. FOR BRIAN (G3): approved 2026-11-04 | `journal_version` 2 |
| SF-16 | `Start.started_at` against the record's `wall_ms` (BP-010 (10)) | **Decided by Brian, 2026-11-04, as recommended: (B).** The options were: (A) Keep `started_at` in the payload as the authority for `GAME.started_at`; `wall_ms` is the append time and need not equal it. (B) Drop it from the payload; `GAME.started_at` is the `Start` record's `wall_ms`, as `Move.played_at` already is. **Chosen: (B)** (one truth, same rule as `Move`). Note (B) changes a field that ruling 2 of Addendum A listed | Two copies of a time can disagree `[OBS Board BP-010]` | G3. FOR BRIAN (G3): approved 2026-11-04 | `journal_version` 2 |
| SF-17 | Hash CHECK (BP-016 (1)) | Replace `status <> 'in_progress' OR content_hash IS NULL` by `(status = 'in_progress') = (content_hash IS NULL)`: every terminal row, completed, aborted, voided and imported, has a hash. A voided game with zero readable moves still has one (D-67 (iv)) | The documents say "never null in `GameEnd`, `Abort`, `Voided`" and D-34 says "on every game", but only the in-progress half is enforced `[OBS store:307; arch:1540]`. That imported rows always get a hash is ASM-9 | G3 | Migration; rows without a hash would need a recompute first |
| SF-18 | Clock-pair CHECKs (BP-016 (2)) | `CHECK ((white_clock_ms IS NULL) = (black_clock_ms IS NULL))` on `MOVE` and on `GAME` (the checkpoint pair) | Backstop for SF-14 | G3 | Migration |
| SF-19 | `COMPUTER_PROFILE` CHECKs (BP-016 (3)) | `CHECK (error_permille BETWEEN 0 AND 1000)` and `CHECK (error_permille = 0 OR error_multipv >= 2)` | The second is a domain constructor rule with no DB backstop `[OBS domain:920]`; D-43 only needs the first | G3 (trivial) | Migration (relaxing a CHECK rebuilds the table in SQLite `[UNV]`) |
| SF-20 | Player ids of a non-imported game (BP-016 (4)) | `CHECK (status = 'imported' OR (white_id IS NOT NULL AND black_id IS NOT NULL))`. Self-play uses the same id twice | Imported games are the only ones with no registered Players `[OBS arch:1399]` | G3 | Migration |
| SF-21 | `Suspend.cause` (BP-017; flag 2) | **Decided by Brian, 2026-11-04, as recommended: (A).** The options were: (A) Add a `cause` to `Suspend`: `detach`, `shutdown` or `recovered` (a D-14 detach, a server stop, a D-58 crash recovery); `Fact::Suspend` and `decide(Suspend)` carry it (G2 for the domain and the app). (B) Keep `{}` and accept that a clean detach and a crash recovery cannot be told apart in a journal. **Chosen: (A)** (Medium confidence at the time: the benefit is diagnosis; the cost is one field and a domain change). With (B), nothing is added later without `journal_version` 2 | D-58 makes `Suspend` mean both cases `[OBS arch:1570]`; the strict decoder forbids a later additive field `[OBS store:749]` | G3 (a field is added). FOR BRIAN (G3; G2 for the domain part): approved 2026-11-04 | `journal_version` 2 |
| SF-22 | `DrawDropped.by` mismatch (BP-017; flag 3) | Replay checks `by` against the pending offer; on a mismatch it **logs and continues** using the pending offer's side. Never an error | An error would turn an audit field into an unresumable game `[OBS Board BP-017]` | G1 | Text only |

#### 11.4.4 Drafted text for the systems engineer (not entered in `docs/requirements.md` or `RELEASE_NOTES.md`)

approval by Brian Prascher on 2026-11-04 (recorded in beads chess-a0r.1.14). The systems engineer logs the text below as written: Amendment 8 (D-63 to D-73) as **approved by Brian on 2026-11-04**, and D-74 (Addendum A, OQ-13) as **CONFIRMED by Brian as a G2 decision on 2026-11-04** (beads chess-a0r.1.19). Addendum D (D-78 to D-81) was ruled later the same day by the architect under Brian's delegation, and by Brian for D-81 as relayed; it is part of the text below, and the entry must say that Brian may reverse D-78 to D-80 (*superseded by Addendum E: Brian ratified D-79 and D-80 on 2026-11-04 and D-78 awaited his choice and was ratified by Brian in Addendum F; CR-11 and CR-12 are logged in requirements.md, use them instead*). OQ-16 is **not** closed and the text must say so. No review of the amendment has been held, and the log entry must not claim one. The number of the Decision Log entry is the systems engineer's to assign (CR-10 is the expected next entry `[UNV]`). This section is the single source for the C-1 ruling (D-75) and the no-nightly decision (D-76), so the systems engineer need not read the addenda to write the entry.

*Draft requirements Decision Log entry:* "2026-11-04. Brian Prascher (G-1) dispositioned the independent Board pass of OQ-16 on the store and journal decisions (docs/design/Board-pass-store-journal.md, BP-001 to BP-017) and accepted all of its proposed dispositions. On 2026-11-04 he approved the text of architecture Amendment 8 (D-63 to D-73, stored-format batch SF-01 to SF-22, GATE-008), including the G2 tier of D-67, the D-72 and D-73 minor items, D-58 as one-way after release, and the architect's recommended choice for the six format choices (SF-02 compare all six FEN fields; SF-03 content hash marker `sha256:v1:<hex>`; SF-04 the `Date` tag is the UTC date of `started_at`; SF-15 snake_case reason codes in the journal only; SF-16 the `Start` record has no `started_at`, the record's wall time is used; SF-21 `Suspend` gains `cause`). He did not close OQ-16: it closes only after the batch is carried into the SDDs (chess-a0r.1.8) and a desk review by the system-architect and the systems engineer, in separate invocations from the author, has passed. OQ-24 (a `calibration` Cargo feature) and OQ-25 (store work before OQ-16 closes) were answered by Brian the same day and are closed: there is no `calibration` Cargo feature in v1 (the calibration code stays as designed), and no `chess-store` code of any kind starts before OQ-16 closes (architecture D-77). No review of Amendment 8 has been held. Also on 2026-11-04: (a) readiness condition C-1 (FIND-001) was ruled by the architect under Brian's delegation, architecture D-75: the ECO table and `eco::lookup` stay in `chess-rules`; the 'deepest named position' rule stays in `chess-domain`; D-20 and the layout text are corrected; no requirement text changes. (b) Brian ruled (G2, approved) architecture D-76: no nightly Rust toolchain, ever; `cargo-llvm-cov` 0.9.1 is admitted as the coverage tool; `cargo-fuzz`, `libfuzzer-sys` and `arbitrary` are not admitted; NFR-3 illegal-state verification uses stable-only means (`proptest` random legal playouts and random-input property tests, the 1000-game soak). (c) OQ-13 (where the engine gateway gets the game state it policy-checks) is ruled by architecture D-74 (`GameContextProvider`), proposed by the architect and CONFIRMED by Brian as a G2 decision on 2026-11-04 (beads chess-a0r.1.19). (d) Addendum D, ruled by the architect under Brian's delegation on 2026-11-04 (a delegation, not Brian's approval of the content; he may reverse any of D-78 to D-80): D-78, the three time totals of a game that is suspended and resumed are seeded from the journal's existing records, the sum of `Move.think_ms` per mover over every `Move` fact with taken-back plies included, and no journal field is added; the unfinished turn of the side to move at each stop is not counted, and the clock is not affected (G2); the store design's minor points XC-ST-A8-1 to -4 are confirmed (G1). D-79, `Game::replay` takes items of `{fact, check}` where `check` holds the record's `san` and `fen_after` for a `Move`, so the SAN comparison of D-72 stays in the domain and the `Fact` does not change; a `fen_after` difference is always an error and a SAN-only difference is a logged warning (G2). D-80, a session that adopted a durable prefix after a failed append accepts read-only queries, `Detach` and `Shutdown`, which append nothing, and refuses every other command with `JournalWriteFailed`; a game is resigned or aborted after a resume (G1). None of the three changes a requirement statement, a stored format or a crate edge. (e) D-81: `cargo-nextest` is not adopted for now (the copy installed on the development Mac, 0.9.128, is in the affected range of RUSTSEC-2026-0412); tests run with plain `cargo test` until a patched version is looked up and a G2 decision admits it; `cargo-insta` stays undecided. Requirement effects for the systems engineer to confirm: (1) RULE-8 and CR-9.11 are read as: a start position equal to the standard start (all six FEN fields) is a standard start (rated, not custom); no requirement text change is proposed. (2) DAT-3: the three time totals are stored from the journal's `GameEnd` record and are not recomputed; the definition of `total_active_ms` is to be stated in the requirements or the domain design; for a game that is suspended and resumed the totals omit the unfinished turn of the side to move at each stop (D-78), and the systems engineer confirms that DAT-3 ("time spent by each Player; excludes time the app was closed/paused") tolerates that, with no text change proposed. (3) NFR-2 / TBD-23 (stays closed at 0): a data directory whose volume cannot do a full sync is refused at open with `DurabilityUnsupported`; the systems engineer decides whether this is a verification case of NFR-2 or a new constraint on `--data-dir`. (4) UI-2 and UI-5: statistics exclude and history marks `custom_start`; a voided game's hash is advisory (no requirement effect expected). (5) OQ-20 is closed (approved); OQ-16 is open with the condition above. (6) NFR-3 and the test-strategy rows 'Property / fuzz' and 'Illegal-state fuzzing (NFR-3)': the verification wording should say stable-only random-input property tests; no coverage-guided fuzzer and no nightly toolchain (D-76), and tests run with plain `cargo test` (no `cargo-nextest`, D-81; test-strategy names neither `cargo-nextest` nor `nextest` `[RAN grep 2026-11-04]`). The test-strategy 90 % line-coverage gate has its tool, `cargo-llvm-cov` 0.9.1."

*Draft RELEASE_NOTES.md entry:* "Architecture Amendment 8 (2026-11-04), approved by Brian Prascher on 2026-11-04, carried into the SDDs; addenda A to D the same day. Applies Brian's G-1 dispositions of the independent Board pass on the store and journal (docs/design/Board-pass-store-journal.md). Added: the finalize time totals in the `GameEnd` journal record; `custom_start` defined once (all six FEN fields compared) and stored; a terminal position is ended, not resumed, after a crash; a failed multi-record step adopts the durable prefix; a database ahead of a damaged journal tail keeps its moves; OQ-20 decided as option A (the hash of a voided game is advisory); hash scope, the `Date` rule (UTC date of `started_at`) and a version marker in `content_hash` (`sha256:v1:<hex>`); `ended_at` in `Voided`; `Suspend` carries a `cause`; journal reason codes are snake_case; the `Start` record has no `started_at`; a single stored-format batch of 22 items with rollback notes; a data directory without `F_FULLFSYNC` is refused. New decisions D-63 to D-73, GATE-008, OQ-24, OQ-25, ASM-9 to ASM-11, Q-A20 (answered). Addendum A: the ECO table and `eco::lookup` stay in `chess-rules` and the 'deepest named position' rule stays in `chess-domain` (D-75, readiness C-1, decided by the architect under Brian's delegation); the gateway game-state source is D-74 (`GameContextProvider`), proposed by the architect and confirmed by Brian as a G2 decision. Addendum C (D-77, ruled by Brian): no `calibration` Cargo feature in v1; no `chess-store` code of any kind before OQ-16 closes. Addendum B (D-76, approved by Brian): no nightly Rust toolchain is ever used; `cargo-llvm-cov` 0.9.1 is the coverage tool; `cargo-fuzz`, `libfuzzer-sys` and `arbitrary` are not used; illegal-state verification (NFR-3) uses stable-only property tests. Addendum D (ruled by the architect under Brian's delegation; Brian ratified D-79 and D-80 on 2026-11-04 and D-78 awaited his choice and is ratified in Addendum F, see §11.4.13): the time totals of a game that is suspended and resumed are seeded from the journal's existing `Move.think_ms` values, with no new journal field and with the unfinished turn at each stop not counted (D-78); `Game::replay` takes a record's `san` and `fen_after` beside each `Move` so the SAN check stays in the domain and the `Fact` does not change (D-79); a session whose journal append failed part-way accepts only queries, detach and shutdown, which write nothing, and the player resigns or aborts after resuming (D-80); `cargo-nextest` is not adopted for now and tests run with plain `cargo test` (D-81). OQ-16 stays open until the batch is carried into the SDDs and a desk review by the system-architect and the systems engineer has passed. OQ-24 is closed (no `calibration` Cargo feature in v1) and OQ-25 is closed (no `chess-store` code before OQ-16 closes), both ruled by Brian (D-77). No review of the amendment has been held. Nothing is implemented."

#### 11.4.6 Fitness functions and tests this amendment implies

FF-3 (§9): its second property also covers the three time totals and `Voided.ended_at`; add a test that finalizes a game live and from its journal alone and compares all `GAME` columns. FF-4: excludes `Voided` (D-67) and states the no-rename condition (SF-04). New tests for the SDDs to name: kill between `Move` and `Ended` for each terminal reason; error injection on the second append of each multi-fact step; reconcile with the store ahead of a truncated journal; the finalize order after a crash; SAN mismatch with a legal `uci`; `ENOTSUP` injection on the data-directory probe; `open_for_calibration` refusing a non-empty directory; table tests of `custom_start`, `rated` and the statistics predicate for the four starts of BP-005. *Addendum D adds:* FF-3 also covers the `MoveCheck` of a `Move` (D-79); tests for the seed rule of D-78 and for the `JournalDegraded` command set of D-80 are listed in §11.4.12. `[UNV]` nothing is built; none of these exists yet.

#### 11.4.8 Addendum A (architect, 2026-11-04): OQ-13 and the ECO placement (D-74, D-75)

Work order chess-a0r.1.5 and the readiness condition C-1. Evidence tags as in the header. Nothing was built or compiled.

**OQ-13, where `EngineGateway` gets the game state it checks (D-74; proposed here, CONFIRMED by Brian as a G2 decision on 2026-11-04, Addendum D).**
- *Question.* §4.5 says the gateway "looks up the game's state and mode", UF-APP-16 and the app design (DD-APP-11 step 1, DD-APP-17, SEQ-APP-04, rule 3 of the dependency viewpoint) propose a `GameContextProvider`, and §4.1 drew no edge for it `[OBS]`.
- *Options.* Trait implemented by the registry (chosen); caller supplies the context; gateway reads the store only; gateway holds the registry; gateway asks the session by message; defer. The reasons are in the D-74 row.
- *Recommendation and confidence.* The trait, with a live snapshot and a store fallback, failing closed. Confidence Medium-High: the cycle and the self-wait arguments are `[INF]` from the two documents, not from a compiled prototype; the snapshot-staleness argument depends on the mode and status being immutable while a game is live `[OBS §7.2, §4.3]`.
- *Consistency with §3.2.* §3.2 lists crates; this change is inside `chess-app`. No crate edge is added, the trait is `pub(crate)` and not in the curated `pub use` list (so FF-1 is unaffected), no §5.7 contract and no protocol shape changes `[OBS]`.
- *Tier.* The architect reads SWE-001 section 3 as G1. The work order asks that a new §4.1 edge be flagged G2, and AMD-10's four earlier intra-core edges were approved inside a G2/G3 amendment, so D-74 was labelled **FOR BRIAN (G2, precautionary)**. The architect cannot desk-review his own ruling. **Brian confirmed D-74 as a G2 decision on 2026-11-04 (beads chess-a0r.1.19).**
- *Edits applied in this document:* §4.1 diagram (one node, three edges) and paragraph, §4.5, §14 (OQ-13), §11, §11.3. Validated, see the line under "Diagrams".
- *Edits proposed to the app SDD (G1 inside `chess-app`, after Brian confirms):* see §11.4.10, item L1.
- *Not decided:* whether `GameContext` needs a distinct state value for "in progress in the store, not live here" (a `chess-domain` change, G2).

**ECO placement, readiness C-1 (D-75, decided under Brian's delegation).**
- *Findings `[OBS]`.* D-20 is inconsistent with itself: its first words put the table "in `chess-domain`", and its next sentences have "a `chess-rules` build step" replay the lines. §3.1, the §3.3 domain row and §8.1 said domain. Both SDDs say the generated table and `eco::lookup` are in `chess-rules` and the "deepest named position" rule is in `chess-domain` (SDD-chess-rules IFP-RUL-6, DD-RUL-013, DR-RUL-008; SDD-chess-domain 3.11.9, DD-DOM-015). The readiness check (FIND-001, ASM-RR-1) assumed the SDD reading. `eco::lookup` was in neither the §3.3 `chess-rules` row nor the §5.7 PGN/FEN row.
- *Ruling.* The SDD reading. The table and the position lookup stay in `chess-rules`; the game rule stays in `chess-domain`. `eco::lookup` is now a named provided interface (§3.3, §5.7). That is why it is **G2**.
- *A defect found on the way (lookup finding F7 `[OBS]`).* A `build.rs` cannot call the crate it builds, so the generator needs `shakmaty` as a build-dependency of `chess-rules` (same crate and version as D-3, no workspace edge). The SDD says it replays lines with `parse_move`; that sentence is to be corrected (item L2). The generator therefore has its own use of the upstream parser, next to `parse_move`; the shared key-derivation module and the public-lookup tests contain the risk of the two disagreeing.
- *The work order's stated reason does not hold.* A generator in `chess-domain` would not need `shakmaty` directly: it could have `chess-rules` as a build-dependency, the edge that already exists. That option (b) is viable `[INF]`. It is not chosen for the reasons in the D-75 row. If Brian prefers it, it is a mechanical change before code and a rewrite of the SDD entities named there after; the tier would then be G1/G2 as the change touches two SDDs.
- *Edits applied in this document:* D-20 (history kept, wording corrected in italics), §3.1, §3.3 (rules and domain rows), §5.7 (PGN/FEN row), §8.1 (ECO row), §11, §11.3, §11.2.
- *Edits proposed to the SDDs and to other documents:* §11.4.10, items L2 to L4.

#### 11.4.9 Addendum B (Brian Prascher, 2026-11-04): toolchain and test tools (D-76)

Brian ruled (G2, approved; beads chess-a0r.1.14): `cargo-llvm-cov` 0.9.1 is admitted as the coverage tool; **no nightly toolchain, ever**; therefore `cargo-fuzz`, `libfuzzer-sys` and `arbitrary` are not admitted; NFR-3 illegal-state verification uses stable-only means. The architect recorded it as D-76 and applied it to §3.4 (table row, Rust row, the "toolchain and test-tool policy" paragraph) and §9 (unit row tools, the "Property / fuzz" row).

| Item | Decision | Reason and evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Nightly Rust toolchain | Never used | Brian: many deployment machines, no nightly capability. The lookup found none installed on the development Mac `[RAN dependency-lookup-step1.md E2]` |
| `cargo-llvm-cov` 0.9.1 | Admitted (G2) | The 90 % gate had no tool (lookup F1). Published 2026-09-06, Apache-2.0 OR MIT, MSRV 1.87, installed with the `llvm-tools` component, no advisory on the tool `[RAN lookup E3, E4, E9]`. Its published lock file carries 3 known transitive advisories and a fresh resolve carries none, so install it without `--locked` (lookup F5, `[INF]`) |
| `cargo-fuzz` 0.13.2 | Not admitted | Needs a nightly compiler `[OBS lookup F4]` |
| `libfuzzer-sys` 0.4.13 | Not admitted | Dependency of every `cargo-fuzz` target; licence `(MIT OR Apache-2.0) AND NCSA` `[OBS registry, lookup §2.3]` |
| `arbitrary` 1.5.0 | Not admitted | Only for fuzz-target input and an optional `shakmaty` feature that is not enabled |
| `cargo-nextest` | **Not adopted for now (D-81, Addendum D, 2026-11-04)**; plain `cargo test` | The installed `cargo-nextest` 0.9.128 is in the range of RUSTSEC-2026-0412 (lookup F3) |
| `cargo-insta` | Not decided; not admitted | Named nowhere in the architecture; not looked up |
| NFR-3 evidence | `proptest` random legal playouts and random-input tests, the 1000-game soak, perft | Stable only. `proptest` 1.12.0 is already admitted. A scheduled run with a high case count (the `PROPTEST_CASES` setting) is a possible soak lever `[UNV: not run]` |

*Residual risk, honest.* Coverage-guided fuzzing finds deeper parser faults than random generation `[INF]`. The decision accepts that loss, so the SDD tests named `FZ-...` become random-input property tests (item L5), and a parser fault that only a corpus-guided fuzzer would find can ship. The mitigations are the typed errors and the bounded readers the SDDs already require (DD-RUL-005 to 011, FM-RUL-004) and the soak.
