None of the rulings is held back under the stop rule. The one conditional item is the DAT-3 reading in D-78, and it is stated where it is used.

##### Ruling 1: D-78, time totals of a game that is suspended and resumed (chess-a0r.1.16)

*The finding.* D-63 and SF-01 put the three totals only in `GameEnd`. A game that is stopped (detach, shutdown, crash) and resumed has no durable record of the time accumulated so far `[OBS domain 3.11.13, XC-DOM-A8-1; app XC-APP-A8-3]`. The software engineer provided `seed_from_facts` summing `Move.think_ms` and called it lossy.

*What the journal already holds `[OBS §7.6]`.* `Move.think_ms` for every move (the journal keeps taken-back `Move` records, because replay needs `Takeback` to remove them `[OBS app SDD takeback bullet]`); `Clock` records at every move, pause, offer and suspend and once a second while a clock runs; `wall_ms` on every record; `Suspend.cause`. What it does not hold: the thinking time of the side to move since its last `Move`, for an untimed game (no running clock, so no `Clock` ticks `[INF]`), and the length of a draw-offer interval.

*Options and consequences.*

| Option | Stored-format change | Result | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| A. Derive on resume from existing records, state the loss | None | Exact for a game never stopped; a stopped game omits the unfinished turn at each stop and any draw-offer time | **Chosen** |
| B. Totals in the `Suspend` payload | G3 | Exact for a clean stop only. Crash recovery writes the `Suspend`, and it cannot know the totals, so A's derivation is still needed: two mechanisms | Not chosen |
| C. Totals or the in-flight fragment in every `Clock` record | G3 | Exact to 1 s even after a crash. Largest change: a bigger record every second, the `Clock` fact, the codec, FF-3 | Not chosen; the fallback if DAT-3 must be exact |
| D. A new `Totals` kind | G3 | Same crash gap as B, one more kind code | Not chosen |
| E. Count only the time since the last resume | None | Loses all earlier time; worse for DAT-3 | Rejected |
| F. Nullable totals when not exact | G3 (SF-01 says "always present") | Breaks UI-2 and the CHECKs | Rejected |

*Ruling D-78.* Option A. The rule is in the D-78 row (items 1 to 5). In short: sum `think_ms` over every `Move` fact, taken-back plies included (the software engineer's "lossy for taken-back plies" does not apply if the sum runs over the journal's facts and not over the plies in force of the rebuilt `Game` `[INF]`); `total_active_ms` seed is white plus black; the unfinished turn at each stop and draw-offer time are not counted; the clock is untouched. The loss is bounded per stop and zero for a game that is not stopped. It can be narrowed later by changing `seed_from_facts` alone, because the inputs exist.

*Why not add a field now, although it is cheap now.* Brian's rule is to add one only if no existing record can carry the information. Here the existing records carry all but one unfinished turn per stop, which is a statistic and not a move, a clock value or a result (NFR-2 is about moves). A new field is a G3 change that freezes at release (D-41); a better `seed_from_facts` is not.

*Store SDD minors (part 5, G1).* XC-ST-A8-1 to -4 are confirmed as the software engineer chose them, with the one addition that XC-ST-A8-2 is a rule: the importer hashes every imported game before the store call.

*Consequence.* Totals of resumed games can be a little low. *Rollback.* Before release: B or C is an edit of §7.6, the SF-01 note, the `Suspend` or `Clock` fact, the codec and FF-3, with no migration. After release: `journal_version` 2 with a v1 reader that applies this rule. *Tier.* G2 (two crates, and a reading of DAT-3) for parts 1 to 4; G1 for part 5.

##### Ruling 2: D-79, the replay interface for the SAN comparison (chess-a0r.1.17)

*The finding.* D-72 (BP-013, G2, approved) says replay compares the recorded SAN and `fen_after` and treats a SAN mismatch with a legal `uci` as a warning. `Fact::Move` has neither field, and §7.6 says the `Fact` does not change `[OBS XC-DOM-A8-2; §7.6 "Fields closed by construction"]`. The software engineer proposed `ReplayItem{fact, check: Option<MoveCheck{san, fen_after}>}`.

*Options and consequences.* The table is in the D-79 row (B to F plus the chosen one). In one line each: B, put `san` and `fen_after` in `Fact::Move`: the `Fact` carries derived data and two truths, and the §7.6 bullet is reversed; C, compare in the app from `Game::view().plies`: misses taken-back plies and weakens the check; D, shadow position in the app through `chess-rules`: a second replay to keep in step; E, drop the comparison: Brian ruled against weakening; F, a second `replay_checked`: two entry points for one rule.

*Ruling D-79.* The software engineer's interface, made final. `Fact`, `fact_to_record`, the journal and every stored format are unchanged. `JournalCodec` gets `record_to_replay_item` (§5.7 row edited). The comparison has one reading, which also closes the hedge in XC-DOM-A8-2: an illegal `uci` is `Illegal`; a different `fen_after` is always `FenMismatch` (a SAN cannot explain a different position); equal positions with a different SAN is a logged `SanMismatch` warning and replay continues with the computed values; no `check` means no comparison. The FEN comparison uses the parsed six-field form with the normalisation of SF-02, so formatting differences of the text alone do not stop a replay `[UNV: depends on the `chess-rules` FEN type; XC-DOM-A8-5]`.

*What is clarified beyond the software engineer's reading.* The SDD said "a `fen_after` difference with an identical SAN"; D-79 says any `fen_after` difference. A record with both a different SAN and a different position is therefore an error, not a warning. This is a reading of D-72's approved wording ("a `fen_after` mismatch that SAN does not explain") and is called out so that Brian can reverse it.

*Consequence.* Two public items in `chess-domain` and one more codec function; FF-3 gains a clause. *Rollback.* Mechanical before code: drop `check` and compare in the app (C), or drop the comparison (E, needs Brian). *Tier.* G2.

##### Ruling 3: D-80, the `JournalDegraded` command set (chess-a0r.1.18)

*The finding.* D-65 part 2: after a failed append in a multi-record step, the session adopts the durable prefix and "accepts no move-making command until the missing facts are re-derived ... the exact command set in that degraded state is left to the app design". The software engineer chose queries, `Detach` (which tries to journal `Suspend` and `Clock`, and on failure releases the lock) and `Shutdown` `[OBS app SDD ALG-APP-01 step 5b]`.

*The conflict, stated honestly.* Brian's guidance asks for a set that lets the player see the game, resume, or resign or abort, and appends no further facts. These cannot all hold in one session. **`Resign` and `Abort` journal an `Ended` or `Aborted` fact and then a `GameEnd` or `Abort` record, so they do append.** The software engineer's `Detach` also appends (it tries `Suspend` and `Clock`), and a failed second append there would be a new partial step in the state that exists because of a partial step.

*How the conflict is resolved without a new mechanism.* The existing resume path already does what is needed. A degraded `Detach` that appends nothing leaves a journal that ends in a non-terminal state that is not `Suspended`. That is exactly the interrupted game of D-58, and the next resume writes `Suspend{cause: recovered}` and `SuspendResume` in a fresh session. The resume prompt's `open_held` session accepts `Resign`, `Abort` and `Detach`, and the domain allows `Resign` and `Abort` from `Suspended` (T16, T17 `[OBS domain 3.10]`). So "see, resume, resign or abort" is available as: see (queries) in the degraded session; resume or resign or abort after a `Detach` that wrote nothing.

*Options and consequences.* A to F are in the D-80 row. The decisive ones: C (allow `Resign` and `Abort` as a terminal append) appends in a session whose write path just failed and needs a rule against resigning after an unwritten mate; F (close the session at once) removes the view of the game and the approved degraded state.

*Ruling D-80.* The set is queries, `Detach`, `Shutdown`. `Detach` and `Shutdown` append nothing. The terminal re-check on entry is the only append, because it journals a fact the failed step had already decided. Everything else is `JournalWriteFailed{cause}`. `Resign` and `Abort` are not in the set, and the SDD must say why (above).

*Consequence.* Resigning from a degraded session takes `Detach` plus a resume. If the journal volume is still unwritable at the resume, the recovery append fails and the game stays unfinished, intact; this is journal-first (NFR-2), not new. The software engineer's SDD choice is changed in one point: no append on `Detach`. *Rollback.* Add `Resign` and `Abort` to the set with a guard (option C); text and code inside `chess-app`. *Tier.* G1, ruled under delegation; the G1 desk review is part of the OQ-16 desk review (held for the system-architect part on 2026-11-04, see the update in the D-80 row; the re-review has not been held). *Dependency to confirm:* D-80 relies on `open_held` behaving as the app SDD's XC-APP-A8-2 describes (a held session journals no `SuspendResume` and stays `Suspended`); that finding has not been ruled here.

Replace by the §5.5 text of this addendum: after a single failed append the clock keeps running and the player may retry; if the code repeats, or follows a multi-record step, or the writer is poisoned (D-82), the session is degraded (D-80): say that this game cannot be saved from this session and that the way out is `Detach`, then resume; no clock is running; the board adopts the durable prefix and does not revert.
- **Consequences of D-80 and D-82 for the player (F-09).** (a) If the journal volume stays unwritable, resign is impossible, the game stays `in_progress`, and its unfinished slot (D-27) blocks that Player from starting another game in that mode; the only exit is reset (D-30), which voids the game and discards the Player's history: say so and name the volume problem. (b) `Abort` exists only for computer-against-computer games. (c) A degraded `Detach` of a timed game journals no `Suspend` or `Clock`, so the resume restores the last `Clock` record. (d) A move that was reported as failed can appear after a resume when the writer's cleanup also failed (D-82 (i)): the resume message already reports the recovered moves.
- **D-71's front-end behaviour** (show the path, the reason, the way out; desktop in-window screen, Addendum F) is in §5.5 and §7.1 only; the four SDDs state it.

#### 11.4.14 Addendum F (architect, 2026-11-04): Brian's decisions applied, re-review findings fixed in text

**Provenance, plainly.** Work order chess-a0r.1.41 (CR-3 closing pass T-D2, baseline B3 plus the working tree). Inputs: **Brian Prascher's decisions of 2026-11-04** as logged by the systems engineer in CR-12 (requirements.md), the OQ-16 limited re-review reports ([architect part](design/OQ-16-limited-rereview-architect.md), RR-A-1 to RR-A-5; [systems-engineer part](design/OQ-16-limited-rereview-systems-engineer.md), RR-S-1 to RR-S-10), and desk review items m-10 and XC-DOM-A8-4. **These are Brian's decisions, not the architect's; this addendum applies them as text and adds no decision.** I wrote Amendment 8 and its addenda, so this is the author applying decisions and answering a review, not an independent pass. Nothing was built. Evidence tags as in the header.

**Brian's decisions, as applied.**
1. **OQ-28 (D-78, DAT-3): option A, G2.** D-78 is **ratified**. The unfinished turn of the side to move at each stop may be lost and this is accepted. Wording fixed by Brian: a stopped-and-resumed game **may lose up to one turn of time; it is not required to**, so a recovery at a clean stop (for example from the `wall_ms` gap of the `Suspend` record) stays open later and needs no change to the DAT-3 verification case. The loss is **not** written as a limit that must not be improved. The DAT-3 statement is not changed. OQ-28 is closed.
2. **OQ-26: option A, G2.** `NewGame` gains `started_at: i64`; the app supplies the one start stamp (D-84). `NewGame` is a store input type, not a protocol type (no `ts-rs`). OQ-26 is closed.
3. **OQ-27: option A, G3.** `canonical_form` omits `SetUp` and `FEN` when the `FEN` equals the standard start in all six fields (consistent with CR-9.11 (2)). `v1` of SF-03 is defined with this rule. OQ-27 is closed.
4. **D-82 stays G1. D-83 is G2** (two names added to the data directory, a stored-layout element), approved by Brian.
5. **m-4:** the D-71 refusal of a data directory that cannot do `F_FULLFSYNC` is an NFR-2 verification case; CT-ST-085, CT-APP-33 and CT-ST-001 map to it (store SDD section 5 and 6, app SDD section 9).
6. **"Approved at G-2"** reads "approved by Brian Prascher on 2026-11-04, carried into the SDDs" in the header, §11 and §14 of this document and in the SDD approver rows.
7. **Exit class 3** in the TUI, in plain mode and in subcommands; TBD-36 is not narrowed; the Tauri refusal is an in-window screen (§5.5, §6.1, §7.1).

**Definition: `total_active_ms` and the DAT-3 bounds (desk review m-10, XC-DOM-A8-4; domain 3.11.13).** `total_active_ms` is the time, in whole milliseconds, during which the game was open in a running app process while its players were deciding: the time charged to the side to move (`white_time_used_ms + black_time_used_ms`) plus the time spent in `DrawOffered`. It excludes `Paused`, `Suspended`, closed-app and crashed time. For a game that is never stopped it is exact. For a game that is stopped and resumed, per Player *P*, let **L(P)** be the sum of `think_ms` over every completed turn of *P* in the journal (taken-back plies included) plus the live time of the final segment, and **U(P)** the value a never-stopped run of the same moves would give. The stored value *T(P)* satisfies **L(P) <= T(P) <= U(P)**; for `total_active_ms`, **L(white) + L(black) <= T <= U(total)**. The tests (UT-DOM-123, UT-APP-32) assert these bounds only: they pass whether the unfinished turn is lost or recovered, and no test asserts equality with the lower bound. **Open draw-offer interval at a stop:** `seed_from_facts` counts no `DrawOffered` time (a `Fact` carries no stamp for a draw offer or its answer), so the interval open at a stop is not counted, and neither is `DrawOffered` time of earlier segments; the same "may, not required to" wording applies, and a later change may count it.

**Disposition of the findings.**

| RR-A-2 | Smaller change chosen: "or `Abort`" dropped from D-87, the §7.6 voided-pair bullet, store 3.11.4 and the domain and app finding rows; no edge added to §4.3 or domain T18 | D-87, §7.6; store, app, domain SDDs |
| RR-A-3 | Step 5a (app) and the §7.6 partial-step bullet say the adopted records go to the store applier | §7.6; app ALG-APP-01 step 5a; UT-APP-40 |
| RR-A-4, RR-S-7 | D-78 (3) qualified (a clean stop's `wall_ms` gap could carry the turn; not used); D-78 (4) says ASM-10 closed | D-78 row |

**New tests (design text, nothing exists `[UNV]`).** UT-ST-090 (rebase with no `Start` in the intact run), UT-ST-091 (final or voided game is not rebased), UT-ST-092 (`start_game` writes `NewGame.started_at` unchanged), CT-ST-089 (no-replace renames, both directories synced, `.rebase` found by the no-journal path), UT-APP-39 (the standard `FEN` tag and `content_hash`), UT-APP-40 (adopted records reach the applier), UT-DOM-124 (draw-offer time in the seed), **UT-RUL-031 (canonical form omits `SetUp` and `FEN` for the standard start; the test the requirements RULE-8 line left unnamed)**. **Reported, not decided.**
1. **Draw-offer time.** The D-78 text said the loss was "one open draw-offer interval per stop". The seed rule (`total_active_ms` of the seed = white + black) gives a larger one: all `DrawOffered` time before the last stop. The text now says what the rule gives. The CR-12 DAT-3 case words clause (3) as "at most the unfinished turn ... and any draw-offer interval open at that stop", which the design does not meet; the systems engineer should reword it to "and the `DrawOffered` time before a stop" (or Brian asks for the seed to count completed intervals, which needs a stamp on the draw facts and is a design change). `[INF]`
2. **The added rebase cases** (final or voided game not rebased; synthesized `Start`) are text under D-83, which Brian approved before these cases were written. They need the independent re-review and, because D-83 is now G2, Brian's confirmation.
3. **Two six-field comparisons.** `chess-rules` needs its own standard-start comparison for `canonical_form` (it cannot call `chess-domain`), as private code; `StartPosition::from_fen` in the domain compares too. Drift is caught by one shared four-start table in UT-RUL-031 and UT-DOM-115. A shared public function in `chess-rules` would add to its interface; that is not decided here.
4. **RR-A-2's premise** (`Abort` then `Voided` is unreachable) is the reviewer's inference and mine `[INF]`; the text now says such a journal is reported as damage.

**Not done, and why.** RR-S-3 (case count N for NFR-3), RR-S-4 (CLI wording), RR-S-9 (RELEASE_NOTES.md), JR5-005 (the rules SDD's `canonical_form` version rule and `Date` rule, beyond the OQ-27 omission), F-07, XC-DOM-A8-5, OQ-CLI-007 and OQ-UI-006: outside this work order or owned by others.

**Mermaid.** No Mermaid block was edited in `docs/architecture.md`, the store, app, domain or rules SDDs by this addendum; validation is recorded in the closing report of chess-a0r.1.41.

## 12. Risks

| ID | Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| R-1 | **`tauri-driver` (WebDriver) does not support macOS**, so "same E2E suite against the desktop shell" (GUI-1) can't use the standard Tauri E2E path on our only platform. | Run the full GUI suite in Playwright **WebKit** (same engine family as WKWebView) using the Tauri transport against a test harness, plus a scripted smoke test of the real `.app`. (D-1's rejected alternative — loading the UI from the local server — would have let the desktop app be driven exactly like the web app.) Revisit if Tauri adds macOS WebDriver support. |
| R-2 | Beginner/Novice strength (400/600) is below Stockfish's limiter; calibration may take several iterations. | Personality parameters live in data, not code, and `chess calibrate` runs nightly on the M4 baseline. Generator bounds are tuned **before v1**. After release, engine parameters never change (PLY-4). Ratings are dynamic (Q41), so a Player whose parameters play weaker or stronger than its category simply drifts to its true rating. Calibration error therefore shows up as rating drift, not as a defect. |
| R-3 | Concurrent writers across three processes. | Short transactions, WAL, `busy_timeout`, leases; concurrency integration test in CI. |
| R-4 | GPL obligations if the app is ever distributed. | Choose GPL-3.0-or-later now (D-10); license audit in CI. |
| R-5 | Localhost web server reachable by other local apps or malicious web pages. | Loopback bind, strict Host/Origin checks, no CORS. Optional per-launch token in the URL if we want more. |
| R-6 | Full-screen TUIs read poorly with screen readers, and terminal state can be left broken on crashes. | Plain mode is a first-class, tested mode (automatic off-TTY); restore guard + panic hook + signal handling, each covered by a PTY test. |
| R-7 | Unicode chess glyph widths vary across terminal fonts, which can misalign the board. | Fixed cell width per square with padding; plain ASCII style always available; snapshot tests per style; manual check in Terminal.app and iTerm2. |
| R-9 | Builds of different versions share one DB (an upgraded CLI next to an old `Chess.app`), so an old build could misread a migrated schema. | D-16: `SchemaTooNew` guard, migrate only when alone, one build version for all artifacts; fixture test. |
| R-10 | A closed tab or a dropped WebSocket now suspends the game at once (D-14), so a brief network-free reload or a crash of the browser pauses a timed game. A player could also close the tab to stop their own clock, exactly as quitting the app allows (RULE-5). | Accepted, because it is the user's rule. Suspension and resume are journalled (§7.6). Reconnect resumes automatically, so a reload costs only the reload time. |
| R-11 | Synchronous `rusqlite` calls on async workers stall the actors and the TUI loop (clock display, NFR-8 responsiveness). | D-13 store thread. A lint or review rule against `Store` use outside the store thread, and a latency test with an injected slow write on the temp-directory store (`FaultPlan`, D-39). |
| R-12 | Several processes running engines at once (e.g., a c–c game in the CLI and a game in `Chess.app`) compete for CPU. Computer strength and move times drift from calibration (ENG-2, ENG-3). | Fixed `Threads`/`Hash` per instance; background analysis at low priority; time limits by `movetime`, not depth, for clocked games; a soak test with two processes. |
| R-8 | chessground's internal selection drifts from `BoardModel` in an unanticipated input sequence (it fires no event on unselect). | Single Reconciler module; reconciliation on `events.select`/`events.change`/`after`; scripted mixed-input Playwright suite; spike exit criterion; fallback to react-chessboard (study option D1) behind the same `Board` contract. |
| R-13 | **Intel support depends on Apple.** The app must support Tahoe and every later macOS (user, 2026-10-26), but later releases may drop Intel Macs. Apple said at WWDC 2025 that Tahoe is the last macOS release for Intel Macs (from memory, `Unverified` online; re-check at each app release under the PLAT-2 release checklist). Mirrors requirements RISK-1. | Keep the universal build (D-25). When a macOS release drops Intel, Intel support stays frozen at the last macOS that runs on it, and arm64 continues. Re-test on each new release (§8.2). Keep one Intel Mac for the platform matrix while Intel is supported. **No Intel Mac is available, so Intel is built but its testing is deferred and best effort (CR-7.6).** |
| R-14 | Intel Macs and M1–M3 Macs search fewer nodes in the same `movetime`, so computer Players play weaker there than on the M4 baseline, and timeouts tuned on M4 may be tight. | Timeouts have generous grace values (§4.8). Dynamic ratings (Q41) absorb the strength difference over time. Engine integration tests run on the Intel machine too. |
| R-17 | Computer ratings now change (Q41) on two tracks, including through c–c games. Ratings can drift away from real strength, and soak or calibration runs could pollute the user's ratings (mirrors RISK-2). | `chess calibrate`, soak, and every test run against an isolated store (`CHESS_DATA_DIR` set to a temp dir; calibrate refuses to run on the default data dir). Engine parameters stay fixed, so drift reflects real results only. Monitor class-band spread in the nightly report. |
| R-15 | Journal-to-DB recovery (replay, conflict replacement, torn tails, takeover) is new code on the most critical path (no completed move lost). | Failure-injection tests: kill at every write point, corrupt the journal tail, restore an older backup, and contend for the DB from another process. A property test checks that replay is idempotent. |
| R-16 | The TUI's resize request (`CSI 8 t`) can be disabled by the user's terminal settings (e.g., iTerm2) or ignored inside tmux/screen. | Fall back to the "please enlarge" message (§6.1). PTY tests cover both paths. |
| R-18 | A reset voids games of other people too (D-30): in a hot-seat h–h game, one human's reset voids the game in progress for the other human, who may have been winning. | Accepted, because it is the user's rule (2026-10-27). The confirmation names each game, its opponent, and the moves played, and says the game will count for neither player. The opponent's history keeps the game, marked "voided by reset", and it can still be analysed. |
| R-19 | A restore cannot rebuild everything a journal does not hold. A journal records the moves of a game, not the registration, rename or reset of a Player. A game that finished after the backup and involves a Player created after it cannot be finalised in the restored copy (D-35, OQ-12). | The game's journal is kept in `journal/unrestored/` and named in the restore message, so nothing is deleted. The automatic backup cadence (24 h, TBD-33) bounds the window. No automatic recovery in v1. Accepted and stated in DAT-5 (CR-5.1). |
| R-20 | In a hot-seat h–h game an unanswered draw offer stops both clocks with no time-out (CR-4.7), so a player can pause a timed game by offering and not answering. | Accepted, because it is the user's rule and the same two people share one screen (nobody is cheated). An offer to a computer is bounded by the ENG-3 maximum think time. Revisit if a networked seat is ever added (§8.4). |

**Follow-up questions for the user (Q-A).** IDs are never reused. Answered questions stay listed with their answer.

| ID | Question | Status / working default | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q-A1 | Tab closed during a timed game? | **Answered:** suspend immediately (D-14) | §4.3 |
| Q-A2 | One unfinished game per machine or per Player? | **Answered:** per Player; c–c counts for each computer (D-27) | §7.2 |
| Q-A3 | Case-variant names duplicates? | **Answered:** no. Names are unique case-sensitively, and identity is the UUID | §7.2 |
| Q-A4 | What does a reset clear? | **Answered:** everything except Elo, humans only (D-26). See Q-A15 | §7.2 |
| Q-A5 | Do c–c games change computer Elo? | **Answered:** yes, both computers (Strict track) | §4.4 |
| Q-A6 | Hot-seat abort / c–c abort? | **Answered:** humans only Resign (h–h asks which side). The watcher's c–c abort has no effect | §4.4 |
| Q-A7 | Computer category label? | **Answered:** follows its current Strict rating (Q79). Engine settings fixed | §4.6 |
| Q-A8 | Port scope? | **Answered:** `chess serve` and the browser URL only | §8.2 |
| Q-A9 | Computer K-factor? | **Answered:** same as humans | §4.6 |
| Q-A10 | Aborted Strict game in W/L/D? | **Superseded:** human games have no abort; a resignation counts in Elo and W/L/D | §7.2 |
| Q-A11 | Terminal bell for sound? | **Superseded:** no sound in v1 | §6.2 |
| Q-A12 | Do you accept the PROPOSED decisions D-12, D-13, D-16, D-18–D-20, D-22–D-24, the mechanisms in D-26–D-29, and ASM-1? | **Answered:** yes, all AGREED 2026-10-26. D-12 and D-16 are one-way for data, consequences accepted. ASM-1 CONFIRMED | §11 |
| Q-A13 | Whose max-think time in c–c and h–h? | **Answered:** the active profile's. In h–h it applies to engine analysis only | §4.6 |
| Q-A14 | macOS releases after Tahoe? | **Answered:** Tahoe and every later release must be supported. Intel only as long as Apple supports it (R-13) | §8.2 |
| Q-A15 | After a reset, should the Player's old games be hidden from their own views, or deleted outright, which also removes them from opponents' history? | **Answered:** hidden. They stay in opponents' history and stats and are hidden only from the reset Player's views (D-26, Q76) | §7.2, D-26 |
| Q-A16 | In a Strict h–c game, does the computer also use up its one unfinished Strict slot? | **Answered:** no. Human–computer games never use a computer's slot, so any Player can always play any computer. Computers hold slots only in c–c (D-27, Q77) | §7.2, D-27 |
| Q-A17 | Do computers also get a Training Elo that changes in Training games against humans? | **Answered:** yes. The K = 40 count is per track (Q78), and the label follows the Strict Elo (Q79) | §4.6, D-28 |
| Q-A18 | A voided game (D-30): is the architect's reading right? It stays in the opponent's history marked "voided by reset", with no result, and can be analysed; it is hidden from the reset Player's own views; it is not a resignation, so no loss is recorded | **Answered 2026-10-28 (user, readiness FIND-003):** yes, the reading stands. A voided game stays in the opponent's history marked "voided by reset", has no result, can be analysed, is hidden from the reset Player's own views, and records no loss | §7.2, D-30 |
| Q-A19 | Do you approve the 13 decisions of the SDD architect triage (section 5)? | **Answered 2026-10-31 (user, Brian Prascher):** yes, all as recommended, with three changes: an archive size limit with a clear message for decision 9 (PGN stays 10 MB); the single-instance mechanism dropped, with `signal-hook`, a dialog plugin, an `arc-swap`-like cell and `rand`/`rand_chacha` rejected, for decision 11; no `player_id` on `VARIATION`, `created_at` and the anchor ply added, saving a what-if branch from the final position refused, for decision 13. Applied as amendment 7 | §11, D-38 to D-50 |
| Q-A20 | Do you approve Amendment 8 (D-63 to D-73, the stored-format batch SF-01 to SF-22, GATE-008) and make the six choices in §11.4 (SF-02 comparison, SF-03 spelling, SF-04 `Date`, SF-15 `reason` strings, SF-16 `started_at`, SF-21 `Suspend.cause`)? | **Answered 2026-11-04 (Brian Prascher):** approved, including the G2 tier of D-67, the D-72 and D-73 minor items and D-58 as one-way after release, with all six choices as recommended: SF-02 compare all six FEN fields; SF-03 `sha256:v1:<hex>`; SF-04 the UTC date of `started_at`; SF-15 snake_case reason codes in the journal only; SF-16 use the record's `wall_ms`; SF-21 add `cause` to `Suspend`. OQ-16 stays open (batch carried into the SDDs and a desk review). OQ-24 and OQ-25 were not decided in that approval; Brian answered them later the same day (D-77) | §11.4 |
